Every claim about the ECB, CoinGecko, Kraken, quirion, BISON, DKB's FinTS server and CSV export, and the ETF issuers' NAV pages was checked on 12 Nov 2026 against the sources in §3; what could not be fetched is listed there as secondary and again in §18. **No household fact appears in this document**: the owners are A and B, the banks, account numbers, balances, holdings, wallet addresses and every amount below are invented, and the real values go only into the app's own records and the container's secret environment variables.

Phase 6 turns the landlord ledger into the household's ledger. It adds no tax law: the forms for capital income and crypto (Anlage KAP, Anlage SO) are phase 8, the lots and holding periods phase 7, the health metrics phase 9. What it adds is **data and plumbing**: (1) every account of the household with its **kind**, its **purpose** (rental or private) and **owner shares**; (2) the household's private bank accounts, at up to three institutes, imported through the **unchanged** statement path (camt, CSV, PDF, FinTS, plus a reader for DKB's CSV export) with one FinTS connection per institute and login, and categorised by a **household taxonomy** that lives beside the property categories, with the rules the app learns per merchant; (3) a **transfer matcher** that links every movement between two own accounts so none is ever counted as income or cost; (4) the depot read from its **documents** (statements, quarterly reports, trade confirmations, distribution notes) into asset movements and holdings that reconcile against the report; (5) the exchange's **CSV export** into the same asset movements, with no model call; (6) **assets, prices and valuations** on an append-only price table fed by four free public sources behind one `PriceSource` (the ECB, CoinGecko, Kraken, and the ETF issuers' own daily NAV files, Luis's choice of 12 Nov 2026), so one month-end net worth is reproducible; (7) the **Konten**, **Anlagen** and **Haushalt** screens of mockups 08, 09, 09b and 10, and the net-worth cards of the Übersicht (mockup 07). The principle that governs every piece: a private account's line can touch a tax figure only by becoming a **confirmed input of the form that already reads it** (a privately paid rental cost of phase 3, a donation or §35a service of phase 4b), never by a second path into the draft.

**Done when** (ARCHITECTURE.md, phase 6): every account is in with an owner share; the second month of private-account lines is ≥ 80 % auto-categorised; all internal transfers are linked, zero counted as income or cost; one month-end net worth whose every account and position agrees with its own source on that day, which the app already checks by code (a bank or card account's statement balance, a depot's report, an exchange's typed balance, a wallet's on-chain balance), and whose total Luis finds plausible; no hand-made spreadsheet (Luis, 17 Nov 2026: „Regarding the spreadsheets. It's too much work for validating the phase. First creating the sheet could also have mistakes, and second filing it out would also take time. I don't think it's worth it.“). **Measured how** (D172): "auto-categorised" counts the confirmed allocations of the private bank accounts booked in a calendar month whose method is `rule`, `matcher_transfer`, `document_match` or `llm` and which no person confirmed (`confirmed_by` null; D172 amended 15 Nov 2026: as first written, a proposal of those methods that a person confirmed counted too), over all confirmed allocations of that month, computed by `GET /accounts/auto-rate?month=` from the stored rows; "every transfer linked" is the count of confirmed `internal_transfer_*` and `owner_transfer_*` allocations without a `transfer_link` row, which must be zero after the window of the last one has passed; the net-worth check compares `GET /net-worth?on=<month-end>` with Luis's spreadsheet for one month-end, asset by asset, and the spec records the date and the result in §14.1 when it exists. The wallets are **not** part of the criterion: they come with phase 7 (the schema leaves them a kind, D156).

## 1. Scope

**In**

- **Account kinds, purpose and owner shares** (D156, D157): `bank_current`, `bank_savings`, `depot`, `exchange` (and `wallet`, reserved for phase 7, refused at creation); `purpose = rental | private`; `account_ownership` rows that mirror `property_ownership` (numerator and denominator, Σ = 1, a reason text, so "joint 50/50 because the coins were paid from the joint account" is stored with the record, as agreed on 6 Nov 2026).
- **The household's private current accounts** (three, at three institutes: one joint account of A and B and one sole account per owner, each institute with its own FinTS server and login; no savings account; Luis, 12 Nov 2026, §17 Q1; which account is at which institute is in the app's records only, never here) imported through the **unchanged** path: a Sparkasse's camt ZIP export or CSV-CAMT export, DKB's CSV export through a reader of its own (D173), the bank's PDF statement, or the FinTS sync of phase 5 with **one connection per institute and login**, each login's PIN a named container secret (D163). The two banks the app supports from this phase are the Sparkassen (as phase 5 built it, any BLZ) and DKB (D173: decoupled approval in the DKB app, camt only if its BPD offers it, verified at the first real connection). The phase 2 guard "an owner account holds no statements" goes (D156). History from 1 Jan 2026 (agreed 6 Nov 2026).
- **A household taxonomy** beside the property categories (D159): income, spending groups as mockup 10 names them, transfers, and the few tax-relevant codes, each either outside every form (`TaxTreatment.PRIVATE`) or a **proposal into the input the form already reads** (D161). A **per-purpose matching order** (D158): the rent, loan and Hausgeld matchers never run on a private account. **Name-pattern rules** for card payments, which carry no IBAN (D160), so the second-month criterion rests on learned rules, not on the model.
- **The transfer matcher** (D162): one pure rule over every own account, bank or exchange or depot, with a `transfer_link` row per matched pair; the rental account keeps its `owner_transfer_*` categories and the matcher of phase 2; the private side gets `internal_transfer_*`; a leg without a counterpart is a review card once its window has passed.
- **The depot by documents** (D165): the cash statements (Verrechnungskonto and the portfolio's Kontoauszug) as PDF statements of the depot account through the existing PDF path, their lines categorised by booking text in code; the **trade confirmations** and **distribution notes** read into `asset_movement` rows through the one pre-filled form (phase 2 D132); the **quarterly report** read into `holding_statement` rows that every position reconciles against, with the report's values as prices. The **tax certificate** is classified, stored and kept; its figures are read in phase 8, when the form that needs them exists.
- **The exchange by CSV** (D166): the BISON transaction history imported into `asset_movement` rows with **no model call**, deduplicated on the exchange's own transaction id, its EUR legs linked to the bank by the transfer matcher, its crypto "Deposit" lines (bonuses: the export carries no external address) proposed as `cashback_reward` for review, its per-asset balance typed once as the declared closing.
- **Assets, prices, valuations, net worth** (D167, D174): an `asset` registry (ETF by ISIN, crypto by symbol and CoinGecko id, cash per currency); an append-only `price` table with a date-first source precedence; a `PriceSource` interface with the ECB daily reference rates, CoinGecko's daily history, Kraken's daily OHLC and **the ETF issuers' own daily NAV per ISIN** (`issuer_nav`, one adapter per issuer, the documents as the fallback for an issuer without a usable page; Luis, 12 Nov 2026, §17 Q4); valuations computed on read; an immutable `valuation_snapshot` per month-end; a net worth that includes the property's estimate from phase 4 and the loan balances.
- **Screens** (§13): Konten regrouped with kinds, owner chips and „Stand“; Anlagen with the Übersicht and ETF tabs (the Krypto tab is phase 7); Haushalt with Einnahmen, Ausgaben, Sparquote and the spending groups; the Übersicht's Nettovermögen and Vermögensaufteilung cards; the „Private Konten mit KI zuordnen“ switch on Einstellungen (D168).
- **Model visibility** (D168): private-account lines and the depot's documents reach the model only after a new workspace switch `ai_private_accounts` is on, with the consent of the member who switches it on (both owners agreed on 12 Nov 2026, §17 Q2; owner B's own click in the app was required until the same evening, D168 as amended), and with the same minimisation as the rental account's lines.

**Out**

- Wallets, chains, lots, FIFO, holding periods (phase 7); Anlage KAP and SO, the Sparer-Pauschbetrag card, the Vorabpauschale card and the Freistellungsauftrag record (phase 8, D170); net-worth-over-time, savings rate targets, reserve months, debt ratios (phase 9: the Haushalt screen of this phase shows measured figures only, no target).
- Budgets (declined 6 Nov 2026); any write access to an account; naming an investment product (non-goal).
- Historical crypto prices before the first movement (phase 7's lots need them; the Demo tier's 365-day limit is a phase 7 fact); historical ETF NAVs before the first confirmed movement of the asset (the issuers' files reach back years, but nothing earlier is stored, D174).
- An MT940 reader over FinTS: designed as a contingency in D173 and built only if DKB's bank parameters offer no camt; a third bank (ING, comdirect, …): nothing in the design excludes one, nothing is tested.
- A non-property loan: none exists (Luis, 12 Nov 2026, §17 Q6); the net worth subtracts the property loans only (D167), and a later consumer loan would need `loan.property_id` relaxed and a spec amendment.
- A lot, a holding period or an acquisition date for any asset (phase 7); this phase only stores what phase 7 needs to carry them across a transfer (D175).
- Reading the depot's tax certificate into figures, and the quirion "Steuerreport" (phase 8).
- Betriebskostenabrechnung, email-in changes, reminders for the new kinds (one reminder, "Quartalsbericht erwartet", is listed as cheap in Q-O63 and not built).

## 2. Decisions taken in this spec

Numbering continues from D155 (phase 4, 12 Nov 2026). Rows marked "Luis" record an answer; the others are the architect's and each is a place to push back in review.

| # | Decision | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D156 | **An account has a `kind`, a `purpose` and a `provider`; the rental account is the one of purpose `rental`; the phase 2 "owner account" becomes an ordinary private account that imports.** `account.kind ∈ {bank_current, bank_savings, depot, exchange, wallet}` (text + CHECK), `purpose ∈ {rental, private}`, `provider` free text (the bank or platform name, which `bank` already holds for bank accounts: `bank` stays and `provider` is the display name for every kind), `currency` (EUR for every account of this phase). `wallet` is in the CHECK so phase 7 adds no migration for it, and `create_account` refuses it with `account.kind_not_available` until phase 7, as `AccountSource.AGGREGATOR` is refused today (phase 7 step 1 builds wallet accounts, phase 7 D179; still refused until that step lands, as of 15 Nov 2026). `is_owner_account` **keeps its column and meaning**: "one of the household's own accounts, recognised by its IBAN"; migration 0025 sets it `true` on every account (the rental one included) and `purpose = private, kind = bank_current` on the owner-account records phase 2 created, `purpose = rental` on the one account that has imports. The two guards that say an owner account holds no statements (`services/statements.py`, `rules.OWNER_ACCOUNT`, lines 123 and 294 today) and that an account with imports cannot become an owner account (`services/accounts.update_account`) are removed; what replaces them is the context rule of D162 (a line's own account is never its own counterpart). `AccountCreate` gains `kind`, `purpose` and `provider`; an IBAN is required for bank kinds, forbidden for `depot` and `exchange` (they are identified by their `provider` and an optional masked `external_ref`, e.g. a depot number masked to its last four characters and HMAC'd like an IBAN, for the statement routing of D164). *Amended 13 Nov 2026 (step 4, migration 0027, Q-O61):* a depot account may carry the IBAN its cash statement prints, stored masked with a keyed hash like every IBAN. It is proposed from an imported statement and applied only when a person confirms it on the account page, which reads it again from that statement's original (the D176 (4) pattern). Once confirmed it is an own account's IBAN, so D162 links a bank account's transfer to the depot, and the depot's payout, as transfers between own accounts. `account_identity_check` is rebuilt: a bank kind still needs an IBAN, a depot may carry one beside its `external_ref`, every other kind has none. | The phase 2 input point 1 built the owner accounts as IBAN-only records because the rental account was dedicated; phase 6 is where those records start carrying lines, and a flag whose meaning silently flips would be a trap. The CHECK reserving `wallet` follows the `aggregator` precedent. |
| D157 | **Owner shares per account mirror `property_ownership` and are enforced in the service; an account without shares is a to-do, not an error.** `account_ownership(workspace_id, account_id, owner_id, share_numerator, share_denominator, reason, created_at)`, append-only like `property_ownership` is treated today (a change is a new set of rows under a new `effective_from`, the old ones kept; the service enforces Σ = 1 over the current set). Migration 0025 writes **no shares**: it cannot know them. The Konten screen shows „Eigentümer festlegen“ on every account without a current set (principle 7); `GET /net-worth` returns the totals regardless and the per-owner split only over accounts with shares, with `unassigned_accounts` listed; the done criterion's "every account in with an owner share" is what closes those to-dos. Default offered on the form: for the rental account the property's shares; for a joint account 50/50; for a sole account 100 % of the named owner. Reason is free text, stored because the joint cold wallet's 50/50 rests on where its coins were paid from (6 Nov 2026) and that argument must survive the person who made it. | ARCHITECTURE's "Every account carries owner shares"; per-owner Freigrenzen in phase 8 need it; the reason column is what makes the attribution auditable. |
| D158 | **Which matchers run depends on the account's purpose; a rent or loan line on a private account goes to review with a question.** `match_transaction` reads `context.account_purpose`. On `rental`: unchanged (rules → owner transfer → rent → loan → Hausgeld → document → model). On `private`: learned rules → **transfer matcher** (D162) → document match (a bill of the household or of the property, D161) (since D160 amended (5) and its review fixes, 15 Nov 2026, the order is: transfer by an own IBAN → a recorded bill → a property bill → learned rules → a payment to the seller → the question below → the model; a line naming an own account's IBAN is never a rule's, and a line paying a bill is proposed as that bill's payment before any rule) → the **household model** (D168) or, with the switch off, review with `routing.ai_disabled` and every code offered. The rent, loan and Hausgeld matchers are not run on a private account; if a private line's counterparty is a tenant's IBAN, a loan account or the Hausverwaltung, the line is routed to review with the question „Diese Zahlung kommt von einem Mieter / geht an die Bank des Darlehens: gehört sie zum Mietobjekt?“ and the answer either books it as `hh_property_cost` / `hh_other_income` with the property, or marks the counterparty as also a private one (a learned rule). Fixture `matching_order.yaml`. | A tenant who also pays owner A privately for something else, or a lender that holds the car loan too, would otherwise be booked as rent or annuity on the wrong ledger; the rental account's matchers are exact because the account is dedicated, and that premise does not hold elsewhere. |
| D159 | **The household taxonomy is a set of `hh_*` codes in the one `AllocationCategory` enum, each with a tax treatment, a threshold and a group; tax-relevant codes never auto-confirm and propose the input the form already reads.** Option (a) of the design (one enum): the `allocation` and `matching_rule` category CHECKs are rebuilt by 0025 (the 0012 pattern), `rules/categories.py` and `rules/review.py` gain a row per code (their completeness checks force it), the frontend labels too. **Income**: `hh_salary` (Gehalt), `hh_child_benefit` (Kindergeld), `hh_tax_refund` (Steuererstattung), `hh_capital_income` (Zinsen, Dividenden on a bank account; tax-relevant, phase 8), `hh_other_income`. **Spending**, the groups of mockup 10: `hh_housing` (Wohnen: the household's own rent or Hausgeld, energy, internet), `hh_groceries` (Lebensmittel und Drogerie), `hh_family_child` (Familie und Kind), `hh_mobility` (Mobilität), `hh_insurance` (Versicherungen), `hh_subscriptions` (Abos und Verträge), `hh_leisure_travel` (Freizeit und Reisen), `hh_health` (Gesundheit), `hh_other` (Sonstiges). **Tax-relevant spending**, each a proposal into an existing input: `hh_donation` → a `return_input` of kind `special_expense` (phase 4b D112), `hh_household_services` → `household_services` (§35a), `hh_childcare` → `childcare_cost`, `hh_property_cost` → a phase 3 `private_cost` tax input (D161), `hh_tax_payment` (Einkommensteuer-Vorauszahlung or Nachzahlung; phase 4b D95 says these are not modelled, so this code is `PRIVATE` with a label and nothing proposed). **Movements**: `internal_transfer_in`, `internal_transfer_out` (D162), `hh_cash` (Bargeldabhebung), `hh_credit_card` (the monthly settlement of a card whose lines are not imported). All `hh_*` codes except the tax-relevant ones get `TaxTreatment.PRIVATE` (new; `rules/tax` ignores it: the Anlage V rules read only `deductible`, `capitalized`, `income`, `pass_through` of rental-purpose allocations, and a test asserts the draft is unchanged when every `hh_*` row of a fixture is removed). Thresholds (`rules/review.py`): the spending groups and `hh_salary`, `hh_child_benefit` at 0.9 (recurring merchants and employers); `hh_capital_income`, `hh_donation`, `hh_household_services`, `hh_childcare`, `hh_property_cost`, `hh_tax_payment`, `hh_tax_refund`, `hh_other_income`, `hh_other`, `hh_credit_card` and the two transfer codes `None`. `ZERO_EXCLUDED` gains the transfer codes and the tax-relevant codes. The model may propose a transfer code but it is never auto-confirmed (D40 extended: the matcher alone books transfers). **The category set is fixed per purpose and enforced by the allocation command**: on a `private` account only the `hh_*` codes, the two transfer codes, the depot codes of D165, `bank_fees` and `uncategorized` are allowed; on the `rental` account only the phase 2–4 codes (and never an `hh_*` or `internal_transfer_*` code); any other category, typed by the user or answered by the model, is refused with `allocation.category_not_for_account`, and the review card offers only the permitted set (`rules/household/taxonomy.allowed_categories(purpose)`). So a private line can never be booked `repairs` or `grundsteuer` and summed by the draft beside the `private_cost` row of the same bill: D161's invariant holds by construction, not by the user's restraint. Fixture case in `private_cost_link.yaml`. `rules/household/taxonomy.py` carries, per code, its group, its relevance (`income`, `spending`, `transfer`, `tax`) and the input kind it proposes; fixture `household_categories.yaml` lists every code with all three. | One enum keeps `allocation.category` one column, the review queue one queue and `categories.py`'s completeness check one check; principle 7: a donation on the joint account should become the return's donation line through a confirmed proposal, not a figure the user has to know to type elsewhere. |
| D160 | **A confirmed answer for a line without an IBAN becomes a name-pattern rule on private accounts.** `infer_rule` returns `None` today when `counterparty_iban_hmac` is `None` ("the user then types a pattern"), which is right for the rental account and fatal for a private one: Sparkasse card payments (booking text `KARTENZAHLUNG`, also `LASTSCHRIFT` of a merchant with a creditor id but no IBAN in the CSV) carry a merchant name and no IBAN, and they are most of a private account's lines. On a `private` account, a single-category answer (`SINGLE_CATEGORY`, any `hh_*` code with a threshold) for a line without an IBAN becomes `RuleMatcher(counterparty_name_pattern=<pattern>, amount_sign=<sign>)` where the pattern is `rules/matching.merchant_pattern(name)`: the name upper-cased, digits, slashes, dots and the trailing location token (`//<Ort>/<Land>`) removed, whitespace collapsed, regex-escaped, anchored at the start (`^REWE SAGT DANKE`). It is offered on the review card as „Künftig immer so zuordnen (REWE …)“ with the pattern shown and editable, exactly like the IBAN rule. `rule_hits` is unchanged (it already matches `counterparty_name_pattern` case-insensitively on the collapsed name). A pattern that would hit a line already confirmed under a different category is refused with `rule.conflicts` and the card says which line (the D49 safeguard, applied to names). Not inferred: on the rental account (unchanged), for a split, for a tax-relevant code or a transfer code. Fixture cases in `rules.yaml` (phase 2) and `merchant_pattern.yaml`. | The ≥ 80 % criterion is reachable only if the second month's card lines are booked by rules; the model with `auto_accept_llm` would reach it too, but D26 says the first month never auto-accepts the model, and a rule is cheaper, explainable and replayable (principle 2). **Amended 13 Nov 2026 (PR #235):** `bank_fees` is learnable on a private account too (its fee lines have no counterparty, so the rule is a typed purpose pattern); a rule that books only shared codes applies on the purpose of the account it was learned on, and learning the same rule on the other purpose does not switch off the first; the rule box is greyed out, with the reason, for a category no rule is ever learned for (since D160 amended (5), 15 Nov 2026, it is not shown, and fewer categories are without a rule). |
| D161 | **A rental cost paid from a private account touches the Anlage V only through the phase 3 `private_cost` input, and the private line is that input's proof.** The draft (`rules/tax/anlage_v`) keeps reading confirmed allocations of **rental-purpose** accounts plus the `private_cost` tax inputs (phase 4b D111); an `hh_property_cost` allocation is `pass_through` for the Anlage V and is never summed by it (fixture `private_cost_link.yaml`: the same bill paid from the rental account and from the private account, each counted once; the existing warning `tax.private_cost_paid_from_account` case of phase 3 kept). Three paths: (1) a private line document-matched to a bill that already has a `private_cost` row (amount ± 0.01, date window ± 10 days around the bill's due or issue date, the bill's IBAN or number in the purpose) is booked `hh_property_cost` with the property and the document, and **becomes the row's proof**: phase 3 D143's "a bill paid from the rental account is proven by its matched bank line" is widened to "paid from any own account", so the to-do „Zahlungsnachweis hochladen“ closes and D143's year-paid rule reads the line's booking date; (2) a private line matched to a bill of the property that has **no** `private_cost` row yet (the bill was read by D135 with its parts but paid from no known account) proposes the `private_cost` row through the existing tax-input command, pre-filled from the bill's parts and the line, confirmed by the user; (3) a private line the model or the user books `hh_property_cost` with no bill at all gets the to-do „Beleg hochladen“ on the property and in Zu prüfen, and proposes nothing to the draft until a bill exists (a cost without a bill is not deductible in practice). The reverse direction also runs: when a `private_cost` row exists and a later private-account import brings its line, the link pass of D162's `_rematch_waiting` precedent proposes the match. | Principle 7 and the first application of the seventh principle (D134, D135, D137): the user uploads the bill and imports the account, and the app finds that they belong together; principle 2: the Anlage V line's source list then holds the input, the bill and the bank line. No second path into the draft means no double count by construction. |
| D162 | **One transfer matcher over every own account, with a `transfer_link` row per pair; a leg without a counterpart is a review card after its window.** `rules/matching.match_transfer(leg, context)` is pure over `MatchContext.own_account_hmacs` (the HMAC of **every** account of the workspace with an IBAN **except the line's own account**, replacing `owner_account_hmacs`, which listed only `is_owner_account` rows and so could not see a private line whose counterparty is the rental account; the phase 2 fixtures load `MatchContext`, so `tests/fixture_models_phase2.py` maps the old key to the new field and the fixture files themselves stay as they are) and `context.open_legs` (the unlinked lines and asset movements of the other own accounts, same asset, opposite sign, within the window). Rule: the counterparty IBAN is an own account's → the line is a transfer by IBAN alone (confidence 1.0, category `internal_transfer_out|in` on a private account, `owner_transfer_out|in` on the rental one, so phase 2's categories, rules and fixtures are unchanged); then the **link**: the candidates are the other account's legs with the same asset (EUR), amount equal to the cent (bank ↔ bank) or equal within the recorded fee (bank ↔ exchange EUR deposit, exchange EUR withdrawal ↔ bank), out booked on or before in, within **3 banking days** (bank ↔ bank, bank ↔ exchange, bank ↔ depot cash); exactly one candidate → `transfer_link(out_leg, in_leg, kind, matched_by = matcher)` written with the allocations; several → the allocation is still booked by IBAN and the link waits for review (`transfer.ambiguous`, the card lists the candidates); none → the allocation is booked by IBAN and the link waits: **no card until the window has passed** (the other account's import may not have arrived), then `transfer.unmatched_leg` („Überweisung von 1.500,00 € am 3. März auf ein eigenes Konto, aber dort kein Eingang: Konto noch nicht importiert, anderes Konto oder keine eigene Überweisung?“) whose answers are: import the other account, pick a leg by hand, or re-book the line. A leg whose counterparty is **not** an own IBAN but whose amount and date fit an exchange or depot EUR movement exactly (the bank line names the exchange's bank, the exchange CSV has the EUR deposit) is proposed as a link for review, never auto. **Confirmed legs are linked too**: an import of the private account's 2026 history brings the mirrors of lines the rental account confirmed as `owner_transfer_*` months ago; `services/transfers.relink(account_id)` runs after every import and every CSV import over confirmed and proposed legs and writes the links it finds, so the done criterion's "every transfer linked" holds for the history as well. Linked legs are `pass_through` on both sides; the Haushalt screen counts a link whose in-leg is on a `bank_savings`, `depot` or `exchange` account as „Gespart“ (mockup 10) and a link between two current accounts as nothing. Lot movement across a link is phase 7's (the row has `kind` and both legs, which is what phase 7 needs). Fixture `transfers.yaml`. *Amended 13 Nov 2026 (PR #239):* linking by hand two legs already linked to each other returns that link instead of a conflict, and confirms a leg the model only proposed; a private account created with an IBAN queues `rematch_waiting` for the lines that name it (§14.1). | ARCHITECTURE's phase 6 row: "generalized internal-transfer matcher … all internal transfers linked, zero counted as income or cost"; design §2.3; the phase 2 owner-transfer matcher is the special case where the other side was never imported. Booking by IBAN before linking means a transfer is never mis-categorised while the other account lags. |
| D163 (revised 12 Nov 2026 with Luis's §17 Q1) | **One FinTS connection per institute and login; each login's PIN is a named container secret; the product id stays one.** The household's three current accounts sit at three institutes, two Sparkassen (different BLZ, different FinTS servers, different logins) and DKB (its own login), so the draft's two fixed slots (`primary`, `secondary`, built on the premise that the joint account was visible under either holder's login at one Sparkasse) do not fit, and a fixed set of three would break again at the fourth login (if the rental account's Sparkasse is not the one of the owner whose login it uses, the private account there is one more connection with its own slot; nothing else changes). **The scheme**: `bank_connection.credential_slot text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'primary'` with the CHECK `credential_slot ~ '^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,31}$'`; the slot's PIN is the container secret `HAUSBUCH_FINTS_PIN_<SLOT upper-cased>`; the existing `HAUSBUCH_FINTS_PIN` **is** the slot `primary`, so the live rental connection (slot `primary` by the default) changes nothing and no secret is renamed. `Settings` gains `fints_pins: dict[str, str]` (`repr=False`, built at start from every `HAUSBUCH_FINTS_PIN_*` variable plus `HAUSBUCH_FINTS_PIN` as `primary`); the start-up check "PIN and product id together or not at all" (`settings.py`, runbook step 14 point 3) becomes "any PIN set requires the product id, and the product id requires at least one PIN". The slot name is chosen on the Bankabruf card when a connection is set up (free text under the pattern, default `primary`; the card lists the slots that have a secret, read from `Settings` as names only), and it is a label, never a secret: it goes into `bank_connection` and the `bank.connection_created` event like the masked login. Several connections may share one slot (one login that shows several accounts at one institute: today's model is one connection per account, each with its own dialog and its own approval, Q-O72). The sync job reads the PIN of the connection's slot from `Settings`, never from its arguments (D119 unchanged: no secret in the database, a payload, an event or a log); a connection whose slot has no secret fails before any dialog with `bank.pin_missing` and the card names the variable to set (`HAUSBUCH_FINTS_PIN_OWNER_B` for the slot `owner_b`); the check runs at the **sync**, not at start-up, so an unset variable can never keep the API from starting. The D119 grep tests over `jobs/bank_sync.py` (`defer(` carries ids only) and the `Settings.repr` test are widened to the dict. Nothing else changes: D118's polling, D120's schedule (with D173's caveat for a bank that asks for an approval at every dialog), D148's trace, D151's codes and D154's statements run per connection, and `bank_code` and `fints_url` are per connection already, so a second Sparkasse's BLZ and server and DKB's are rows, not code. Slot names in this file and the runbook are examples (`primary`, `owner_b`, `joint_bank`); the real ones, like which login sees which account, stay in the household's own records. Answers phase 5 Q-O43. | Q-O43 offered a per-connection variable name or a secrets table; a per-slot variable keeps D119's "no secret in the database" without inventing an encryption key the container would have to hold, and a name under a pattern, unlike a fixed enum, does not need a migration for the next login. |
| D164 | **A statement file is routed to its account by the IBAN it carries, and refused when it names another.** `import_statement(account_id, data)` already reads `ParsedStatement.account_iban` (the camt `Acct/Id/IBAN`, the Sparkasse CSV's `Auftragskonto`, DKB's CSV metadata line with the IBAN (D173), the PDF extraction's `account_iban`); it now HMACs it and refuses the import with `statement.account_mismatch` when the file's IBAN is not the account's (the camt and CSV always carry it; a PDF whose extraction has none imports with the warning `statement.iban_unread` and asks „Ist das der Auszug von <Konto>?“). The upload page offers the account the IBAN matches pre-selected, every account otherwise, so a file dropped on Dokumente lands on the right account without the user knowing which IBAN is which (email-in of phase 5 already routes by exactly one matching account). A depot's cash statement names the depot by a depot or customer number, not an IBAN: it is routed by `account.external_ref_hmac` (D156) the same way. Settles phase 2 D131's "phase 6 decides how a non-rental account's statement is told apart from the rental account's". | With four accounts, "which account did I drop this on" is the first mistake a person makes, and a statement imported to the wrong account would reconcile as `unreconciled` for a reason the card could not name. |
| D165 | **The depot is one account of kind `depot`: its cash lines are transactions imported from its PDF statements through the existing path and categorised by booking text in code; its securities are `asset_movement` rows read from the trade confirmations and distribution notes; its positions reconcile against the quarterly report.** (1) **Cash.** quirion's Postbox holds a Kontoauszug of the Verrechnungskonto and one per portfolio ("Globales Portfolio"), both EUR cash statements with the bookings Einlage, Kauf, Verkauf, Verwaltergebühr, Dividende/Ausschüttung, Vorabpauschale/Steuer (§3, secondary source). Each is uploaded as a PDF statement of the depot account (`StatementFormat.PDF`, `StatementExtraction`, the extract job with the `depot_statement` subtype of migration 0021, balances read from the file, D21's reconciliation unchanged, lines hashed as today). The portfolio's cash and the Verrechnungskonto are **two accounts of kind `depot`** when quirion states separate balances for them (the forum thread says it does; step 4 confirms on the real PDFs, Q-O61), each with its own `external_ref` for D164. `rules/depot.categorise(line)` maps the booking text to a category **without a model**: `Einlage`/`Auszahlung` → `internal_transfer_*` (then D162 links it to the bank leg), `Kauf` → `security_purchase`, `Verkauf` → `security_sale`, `Dividende`/`Ausschüttung` → `distribution`, `Vorabpauschale` and `Steuer`/`KapSt`/`Soli` → `capital_income_tax`, `Verwaltergebühr`/`Gebühr` → `depot_fee`, anything else → `uncategorized` for review (these five codes join the enum with `TaxTreatment.PRIVATE` and threshold `None`; they are **not** `hh_*` because they describe the depot's own bookings, and phase 8's KAP rules read `distribution` and `capital_income_tax`). Fixture `depot_lines.yaml`. (2) **Securities.** A `trade_confirmation` document (migration 0021) gets the extraction kind `trade_confirmation` (`TradeConfirmationExtraction`: ISIN, name, trade date, settlement date, side, units (to 6 decimals), price per unit, gross amount, fees, net amount, currency, the order reference; each with page and quote), scrubbed of the depot number, customer number, IdNr and IBAN (`scrub_identifiers` analogue, D168); code proposes **one `asset_movement`** (`kind = acquisition | disposal`, the asset by ISIN, units, EUR amount, fee, `occurred_at`, the document) and the link to the cash line whose amount and date match (`asset_movement.transaction_id`), through the one pre-filled form, saved on „Übernehmen“. A distribution note (`distribution_note` extraction: ISIN, record date, payment date, units, gross per unit, gross, withholding and German taxes, net, currency) proposes `kind = distribution` with the gross and the tax split kept on the row (`tax_withheld_eur`, for phase 8) and the link to the `distribution` cash line. (3) **Holdings.** A `depot_report` (the quarterly PM-Reporting) gets the extraction kind `depot_report` (`DepotReportExtraction`: report date, per position ISIN, name, units, price, value, currency; the "Wertpapierumsätze" list: ISIN, trade date, units, amount, reference) and proposes `holding_statement` rows (account, asset, `as_of`, declared units, declared value) through the form; on confirmation `rules/depot.reconcile_holdings(statement, movements)` compares declared units with Σ movements per asset up to `as_of`: equal → the positions are reconciled through that date and the report's prices are stored as `price(source = statement)`; a difference → the card `depot.holdings_mismatch` names the asset, the two figures and the trade confirmations the report's Umsätze list that the app has no movement for („Der Bericht nennt einen Kauf vom 14. Mai, zu dem keine Abrechnung hochgeladen ist“), and **no valuation after the last reconciled date is shown for that asset** (the same guard as an unreconciled statement). A movement the report's Umsätze list but no confirmation covers can be proposed from the report alone, flagged `source = report`, so a lost confirmation does not block the quarter. (4) **Tax certificate.** The `capital_income_tax_certificate` subtype is classified, stored, kept 10 years and shown on the ETF tab's Postbox list; its figures are **not** read in this phase (D170). (5) quirion offers no FinTS and no API (§3), so the documents are the only source; the Postbox is Luis's work (§4.1). *Amended 13 Nov 2026 (step 4, read against quirion's real Postbox documents on Luis's computer, never committed; §14.1):* (a) **several depot accounts** (Q-O61): each portfolio (strategy) has its own Vermögensverwaltungskonto with its own IBAN, balance and depot value, and the Verrechnungskonto is a further account; each is its own account of kind `depot` with its own `external_ref`, and one household may hold depots under two customer relationships (one owner alone, both jointly, D157 shares). A document is never routed by the number it prints: the depot is pre-selected only when the workspace has exactly one, otherwise the person picks it on the form. (b) **The cash statement is read by code**, as the card's is (D176 (3)): statement format `depot_pdf`, no model, balances from the file, D21's reconciliation; the PDF statement path (1) relied on was never built. Only a booking's headline is categorised (a sale's detail line names its withheld tax); the headlines seen are „Wertpapier Kauf“, „Wertpapier Verkauf“ and „Vermögensverwaltungshonorar“, and the account's interest („Zinsen“) is `hh_capital_income` (D159); Einlage, Auszahlung, Ausschüttung and Vorabpauschale keep step 0's texts until a real line shows them. A line booked this way has the method `matcher_depot`. (c) **Trade confirmation and distribution note:** the side is in the heading, the reference is the Referenz-Nr, fees are up to four lines, and a sale's Ausmachender Betrag follows the taxes, so the reading asks `taxes` and a sale checks net = gross − fees − taxes; a distribution of a fund in USD states its euro side separately (rate, euro gross, German taxes in euro, euro net), and the per-unit amount has four decimals. (d) **Units moved between two own depots** arrive as an Ausbuchung and an Einbuchung with one reference: the extraction kind `securities_transfer` (migration 0027) proposes the pair as one transfer of units (`internal_transfer_out/in` movements), never a disposal or an acquisition, and the units keep their acquisition dates (the D175 principle). (e) **Holdings:** the quarterly report's Umsätze print the amount signed and repeat earlier trades; a position counts units moved in or out between own depots; an asset the confirmed movements still hold but the report omits is a position of 0 by omission that does not reconcile, so a missing Ausbuchung or sale is never silently held. Fixtures `depot_lines.yaml`, `quirion_documents.yaml`, `holdings_reconcile.yaml` corrected accordingly. *Fixed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #312, images afaa05e98fa4, no migration):* a report row whose price, value or trade amount the reading wrote with „EUR“ after it („78,5800 EUR“) is read; it had been dropped as `depot.row_unreadable`, so the Globales reports of 31 Mar and 30 Sep proposed no position and three reports lost their Umsätze list (`rules/depot._german`). Only the euro is dropped; another currency stays unreadable. The ready suggestions were rebuilt by `follow-accounts`; the one already applied as typing only needs „Neu auslesen“. The „Depot“ field's hint, which still said the printed number never picks the depot, was corrected (PR #311, images 8a8a592e07ff). | Design §2.1's depot row ("positions on the quarterly report = Σ movements per ISIN; cash on the Verrechnungskonto statement"); reusing the PDF statement path means the cash side gets dedup, reconciliation and the review queue for free, and the securities side needs exactly two readers and one pure reconciliation. *(b) amended 13 Nov 2026 (PR #258):* a depot cash statement whose account does not exist yet creates it with „Konto anlegen und einlesen“, see D176 (3) amended; the depot gets no IBAN at creation. *(a) amended 14 Nov 2026 (Luis: "when importing all the documents for a depot (or another account in general) the app should recognize that they're all for the same account and when you create the account they are all assigned directly to it"; "The Steuerjahr was not filled for the Einbuchung document for the Globales Portfolio"; merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026, PR #307, images 41f40240f7e8, no migration):* (1) **A depot document finds the depot it prints.** A depot report, trade confirmation, distribution note or securities transfer pre-selects the depot whose number or IBAN its PDF text layer prints, read by code (`rules/depot.printed_depot_numbers` and `printed_ibans`, `services/depot.printed_depot_account`), never by the model, and the scrub is unchanged. Only a **labelled** number counts („<n>Depot“, „Depot-Nr.“, „Ihr Konto <n>“, „<n> EUR Konto“), because an Einbuchung or Ausbuchung also prints the other depot's number unlabelled beside the holder's name; a document printing more than one labelled number pre-selects none. Exactly one depot holding it → pre-selected with the note „Das Dokument nennt die Nummer des Depots …“. *Review, 15 Nov 2026:* a labelled number decides alone, and the IBANs count only when none is printed; exactly one labelled number that no depot holds while every depot has its number (an Einbuchung into a depot not created yet, with only the giving depot under Konten) means the document is **another depot's**: nothing is pre-selected, not even the only depot, and the note says „Dieses Dokument nennt ein anderes Depot, als unter Konten angelegt ist. Bitte zuerst dieses Depot anlegen …“ (it was put on the only depot at low confidence until then). More than one labelled number (a Finanzreport over several depots; Luis's real one prints three) pre-selects nothing either, not even the only depot, and adds no note, since those depots may all exist. Only when the document says nothing that tells (no labelled number or depot IBAN, no text layer, or a depot without a number that could be the one printed) does the earlier rule hold: pre-selected only when the workspace has one depot. „Übernehmen“ is still required (principle 3). Checked against Luis's real quirion files (kinds of number only): each of the four kinds prints exactly one labelled depot number, the same number as the depot's cash account's Kontonummer, which is the account's `external_ref`. (2) **A new or newly numbered depot rebuilds the waiting readings.** When a depot is created with its number, gets its number from its first statement, or has its IBAN confirmed, every `ready` suggestion of a depot document is rebuilt from its stored reading without a model call (`services/proposals.rebuild_depot_proposals`), so it offers the new depot and pre-selects it; the reading's version is kept, so D132's stale guard still holds; an applied suggestion is never touched. *Review, 15 Nov 2026:* a suggestion whose document could not be read just now is left as it is (a passing store error never takes away a pre-selection), and each suggestion is rebuilt under its own savepoint, so one that fails is logged by its exception class only and the others go on. (3) **Steuerjahr by code.** A depot document's suggestion fills its Steuerjahr from its own date when the reading has none: the trade date (trade confirmation), the payment date else the record date (distribution note), the transfer date (securities transfer), the report date (report), each falling back to the document date; a `general` reading of a document typed as the depot's takes its document date. *Review, 15 Nov 2026:* the year-end documents (Jahressteuerbescheinigung, Ertragsübersicht, Kostenausweis; `document.depot_kind`) are issued after the year they report on, so they take the one year a 20xx number in their read title names (medium confidence), else the year before their date (low), never their date's own year (a Jahressteuerbescheinigung 2026 dated February 2027 had been filed under 2027 at high confidence). No fixture case: `quirion_documents.yaml` covers the readers and movements, not the typing; the cases are in `tests/rules/test_depot_printed.py`. (4) **Catch-up once after the deploy:** `hausbuch follow-accounts --workspace <id> [--dry-run]` queues one worker job (the worker holds the object store) that rebuilds every ready depot suggestion and imports the waiting statements of existing accounts (D176 (3) amended 14 Nov 2026). (5) **Fix: an Einbuchung of several funds under one reference** booked its first position and refused the second as already booked (`depot.movement_exists`), because the twin check compared only the reference; it now compares the reference and the fund, and a second upload of the same document is still refused. *D165 amended (15 Nov 2026), a quarterly report books its trades; builds the report-only movement (3) foresaw (Luis: quirion prints one Wertpapierabrechnung per fund and trade; about 170 trades are listed in the quarterly reports' „Umsätze“ list but not booked, and uploading about 170 PDFs is not reasonable):* (1) **A report's suggestion books its unbooked trades.** Each trade of the report's Umsätze list whose reference no movement on any depot carries is proposed as a movement record beside the holdings: a purchase by positive units, a sale by negative ones, the units, the listed amount as the cash (negative for a purchase, whatever sign the reading kept), the trade date, the report's reference as `external_ref` and the report as its document. Only trades up to the report's date, with an ISIN and a printed reference („unbekannt“ is none), units other than 0, and the first of each reference are proposed (`rules/depot.bookable_trades`, `report_trade_movement`); the card says how many and that the rest are booked already. One „Übernehmen“ books the trades first and the holdings after, which are then reconciled against them, and the report's prices follow (D167 amended (c)). (2) **„Umsätze aus dem Bericht buchen“ for a report already taken over.** The holdings card („Depotbericht stimmt nicht“) counts the trades the depot's current reports list and nothing books (`InboxItem.report_trades`), says so in its question and shows the button; `POST /accounts/{id}/report-trades` (`services/depot.book_report_trades`, the person as actor) books the missing references of the latest and the earlier current reports, newest report first, each reference once, in one transaction, then stores the reports' prices that now agree. The list is computed on the server again, never sent by the page; nothing to book books nothing. A fund the latest report already agrees on is left alone. (3) **The same movement a confirmation writes**: the same kinds, the same cash-line link (`_cash_line`), the same `movement.confirmed` event, with `source` = `report`. The Umsätze list prints no fee and no tax withheld, so neither is stored; they come from the yearly tax certificate in phase 8 (settled by phase 8 D217 (4), 16 Nov 2026: such a movement never needs them, since the Anlage KAP takes its bank figures from the certificate only and no fee of a depot is deductible). (4) **A later trade confirmation with the same reference** books nothing a second time and is never refused: its „Übernehmen“ returns the report's movement, and its suggestion says „Bereits aus dem Quartalsbericht gebucht. „Übernehmen“ bucht nichts ein zweites Mal; die Abrechnung bleibt unter Dokumente abgelegt.“; when its units or amount differ from the report's booking, the note names both and the report's booking stays (the person compares the two documents). A confirmation of a trade another confirmation booked is still refused as before (`depot.movement_exists`). (5) A reference booked already is never proposed again, and a report's record whose reference was booked between its reading and „Übernehmen“ (a second report read before the first was taken over) returns the booked movement instead of a second one. *Review fixes (15 Nov 2026):* (a) **One booking at a time per depot:** the button and every movement a suggestion books take the depot account row's lock before any check for a booked reference, and an „Übernehmen“ takes the locks of all the depots its movements name first, in the order of their ids (a transfer between own depots books on two); two presses, or the button and a report's „Übernehmen“ at once, had both found the trades unbooked and written each twice, for good (no unique index: a shared reference is legitimate). (b) **The button books the confirmed reading:** the trades come from the reading of the report's most recently applied suggestion, never from a later reading the person rejected or has not taken over yet; a report with no applied suggestion gives none. (c) **A fund only when it then agrees with the report:** per fund, all or nothing, the button books its unbooked trades only when, with them booked, the fund's units on that depot equal the units the depot's latest report states for it on its date (its current positions, 0 for a fund it does not list); otherwise it books none of that fund's trades, and the card, the response (`left_out`) and the page name the fund with „Bestand würde nicht zum Bericht passen“ when it disagrees now. That also covers a corrected reference, a trade booked under another reference and a doubled reading. The card counts exactly what the button books. (d) **Rows that cannot be read are left out**, never rounded or repaired: a row whose units and amount contradict each other (a purchase needs positive units and a negative amount, a sale the reverse) or that fails the movement's own checks (an amount with three decimals); the card names each by fund and day as „nicht lesbar“ and asks for that trade's Wertpapierabrechnung, and the other rows are still booked (one such row had made the whole request a 422). The suggestion's records keep (1)'s sign from the side; the person sees each row there before „Übernehmen“. (e) **The confirmation's note** promises „bucht nichts ein zweites Mal“ only when the report's booking is on the depot pre-selected on that confirmation; otherwise it names the depot the trade is booked on and says that another depot books it again there. Known limits, still open: a depot movement cannot be taken back (no correction path for `asset_movement` yet); a trade is told from a repeat by its reference only, so a trade booked earlier without a reference or under a differently read one is proposed again by a report's suggestion, and a confirmation whose trade a report booked under another reference is booked again by its own „Übernehmen“; the button's „already booked“ check and its agreement check cover the depot it is pressed on only, while a report's suggestion skips a reference booked on any depot; a later confirmation applied on another depot than the report's booking is checked on that depot only. No migration: `report` was already an `asset_movement.source` in migration 0025, which requires its document. Merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #330, images 814666ae7f85, no migration). |
| D166 | **The BISON CSV is imported by code into asset movements, deduplicated on the exchange's transaction id, with no model call; the typed balance per asset is its reconciliation.** The exported file is stored as the immutable original (`document.subtype = exchange_export`, typed by code from the upload page's „Börse / Exchange-Export“ button, never classified or sent to the model: it holds every trade and the EUR legs); a `movement_import` row (account, document, counts, status, error code) mirrors `statement_import`; `services/exchange/bison_csv.parse(data) -> list[MovementLine]` reads the columns the design recorded on 6 Nov 2026 (Transaction ID, type, asset, amount, EUR amount, fee; **not re-verifiable today**, §3), and the row shape (one row per trade, or one per leg) is settled on the anonymised real export in step 5, exactly as the camt was in phase 2 (Q-O59). `rules/exchange.movement_hash(line)` = SHA-256 over the exchange name and the Transaction ID (the bank-reference precedent of D22), with the line's position among identical rows as the fallback; a re-upload skips stored hashes. Mapping by `type`, in code (`rules/exchange.classify`): Buy → `acquisition` (asset in, EUR out, fee), Sell → `disposal`, EUR Deposit/Withdrawal → an EUR movement of `kind = internal_transfer_*` that D162 links to the bank leg (same amount, 3 banking days), Staking/Reward rows → `staking_reward`, a **crypto** `Deposit` → **proposed** `cashback_reward` for review with the question „BISON kann keine fremden Adressen empfangen; ist dieser Eingang ein Bonus?“ (never auto: the design's reading that bonuses come as Deposit is from 6 Nov 2026 and the export was not readable today), a crypto Withdrawal → `internal_transfer_out` proposed for review with the destination unknown (phase 7's cold wallet reconciles it; until then it is an open leg and the card of D162 waits), the row keeping the exchange's transaction id, the network, the amount and the fee in the asset's units, and the transaction hash when the export carries one, so that phase 7 can tie it to the wallet's deposit and move the lot with its acquisition date and price (D175). **Reconciliation**: the CSV carries no balances, so the user types the balance per asset shown by the app once per import (`DeclareHoldingsBody`, the `pending_balance` precedent of D21): Σ movements per asset = typed balance → reconciled; else `exchange.unreconciled` and nothing after the last reconciled import is valued for that asset. **Lots are phase 7**: the rows carry what FIFO needs (`asset`, `kind`, `quantity`, `eur_amount`, `fee_eur`, `fee_quantity`, `occurred_at`, `external_ref`, `network`, `tx_hash`, the link to the bank leg; D175), nothing is computed from them here, and `movement_category` as phase 7 defines it (design §2.2) is the same column `kind` with the values listed in D167. Fixture `bison_csv.yaml`. *Amended 13 Nov 2026 (step 5, read against the real export on Luis's computer, never committed; Q-O59 settled):* the export is nine columns separated by „; “ (Transaction ID; Transaction type; Currency; Asset; Eur (amount); Asset (amount); Asset (market price); Fee; Date (UTC - Coordinated Universal Time)), no BOM, **one row per trade with both legs** (a Buy carries the EUR amount and the asset amount, its Fee 0), amounts unsigned with a point as decimal mark, the asset written capitalised (Btc) and read upper-case; the types seen are Buy, Deposit and Withdraw (Sell, staking and reward rows keep step 0's mapping until a real row shows them); an EUR Deposit has Currency Eur and no Asset, a crypto Deposit and a Withdraw have an Asset, its amount and a Fee in the asset's units and no EUR amount; Transaction IDs are a UUID or a hex string, unique per row. Whether a Withdraw's Asset (amount) includes its Fee is not printed (Q-O80); the typed balance decides it. As for Binance (D178 (3)), a trade's EUR leg is its `eur_amount`, never its own movement. **Amended (3) 17 Nov 2026:** BISON's export of 17 Nov 2026 separates its columns with commas instead of „; “, writes a one-digit hour („9:45:20“) and keeps the same nine columns and Transaction IDs; the reader accepts either separator, decided by the header, and still refuses a row whose cell count is not nine, so a comma inside a cell is never read as a column (Luis: „I got this error when uploading the bison csv“, `exchange.unreadable`, the import had refused the whole file). Dedup by the Transaction ID is unchanged, so the rows already imported from the earlier semicolon files are skipped. Merged and deployed 17 Nov 2026 (PR #420, images ed75ef44a354, no migration). | Design §3 and §9.3; an exchange export is a bank statement with assets instead of EUR, so it gets the same hash, dedup and declared-balance guard; a model has no place in a mapping a column already states. |
| D167 (revised 12 Nov 2026: the precedence is date-first and the ETF issuers join the sources, §17 Q4) | **Assets, an append-only price table with a date-first source precedence, four free price sources behind one interface, valuations computed on read, one immutable snapshot per month-end.** (1) `asset(workspace_id, id, kind ∈ {cash, etf, stock, crypto}, symbol, isin, name, currency, coingecko_id, kraken_pair, decimals)`; EUR is an asset of kind `cash` so an exchange's EUR balance is a position like any other; created from a trade confirmation's ISIN or a CSV's symbol on first sight, named by the document, and matched to a CoinGecko id or Kraken pair, or to an issuer adapter and fund reference (D174), from a **registry file** `rules/household/assets.yaml` (symbol → ids; ISIN → issuer, fund reference, NAV currency; the coins and funds the household holds plus the majors; a symbol or ISIN not in it gets `price.no_source` and a to-do „Kursquelle wählen“, principle 7). (2) `price(workspace_id, id, asset_id, on, price_eur, source ∈ {trade, statement, issuer_nav, coingecko_daily, kraken_daily, ecb_fx, user}, fetched_at, document_id, raw)` is **append-only**; `rules/valuation.price_on(asset, on, prices)` picks **the latest day on or before `on`** that has any price, then among that day's rows by precedence `trade` (the trade's own EUR leg that day, agreed 6 Nov 2026) > `issuer_nav` (the issuer's published NAV, the official price of the day, Luis 12 Nov 2026) > `statement` (a report's value) > `coingecko_daily` > `kraken_daily` > `user`, then the latest `fetched_at` within a source. Date first, then source: a NAV from yesterday beats a report value from a month ago, and a trade price from last quarter never beats today's NAV (the draft's wording was source-first, which would have done exactly that). A price older than 7 days → `valuation.stale_price` on the screen, unchanged for every source. (3) `PriceSource` protocol (`services/prices/`): `ecb_fx` reads the ECB's daily XML (`eurofxref-daily.xml`; `eurofxref-hist-90d.xml` for a backfill) and stores USD, GBP, CHF per EUR (and, since D174 amended 16 Nov 2026 (C), the rate of any other currency a held fund's NAV is quoted in) as `price` rows of the cash assets, so a figure quoted in another currency becomes EUR by the ECB rate of the day (the reference rates are set around 16:00 CET, §3; a day without a rate, a weekend or a TARGET holiday, takes the latest rate before it); `coingecko_daily` reads `/coins/{id}/history?date=dd-mm-yyyy` (the snapshot at 00:00 UTC of that date, EUR field, Demo key in the header `x-cg-demo-api-key`, the Demo tier limited to the past 365 days and to 10,000 calls a month, attribution required, §3) for **the days since the first movement of the asset and today**, nothing earlier (phase 7 backfills for lots); `kraken_daily` reads `/0/public/OHLC?pair=<pair>&interval=1440` (no key, the 720 most recent daily candles, the close as the day's price, §3) as the fallback when CoinGecko has no price or the key is unset; `issuer_nav` is D174. A periodic task `fetch_prices` (07:00 UTC, after the ECB has published the previous day) fetches the ECB rates first, then each held crypto asset's close for every day since its last stored day, at most 31 days per run, then each held ETF's NAV file (D174); a failed source logs its class and status code only and leaves the price stale, never a card, except D174's card for an issuer that has failed for seven days. The CoinGecko key is a container secret `HAUSBUCH_COINGECKO_KEY` (`repr=False`); without it the fallback is Kraken. The provider terms (CoinGecko: attribution) are met by the line „Kurse: CoinGecko, Kraken, EZB, NAV der Fondsgesellschaften“ on the Anlagen screen. (4) **Valuations are computed on read**: `rules/valuation.value(positions, prices, on)` = per account and asset Σ confirmed movements through `on` × `price_on`, only through the asset's last reconciled date (D165 (3), D166); a `bank_*` account's value is its last reconciled closing balance on or before `on` (the Σ of its lines from the anchor); the property's value is phase 4's projection value for the year (the purchase price grown by the scenario's value growth, D86), the loans' balances from the schedule, both labelled as estimates. `NetWorth(on) = Σ bank balances + Σ positions × price + property estimate − Σ loan balances`, with the split per owner by `account_ownership` and the property's shares, and by class (Bank, Depot, Krypto, Immobilie, Verbindlichkeiten) for mockup 07's bar; no loan but the property's exists (Luis, 12 Nov 2026, §17 Q6). (5) **A `valuation_snapshot` row per account, asset and month-end** (since migration 0040, 15 Nov 2026: also one per property, its estimate, and one per loan, its balance, so a stored month-end is read from its rows alone and nothing in it is recomputed; see the amendment at the end of this row), written by the periodic task `month_end` on the 1st at 08:00 UTC (after the 06:30 bank fetch and the 07:00 price fetch; 05:00 until 15 Nov 2026) for the previous month-end and on demand by „Monatsabschluss festhalten“, **immutable** (`forbid_mutation`, like `tax_export`), carrying the units, the price, its source and the value; the Übersicht shows the latest snapshot's net worth and today's on-read figure side by side, so the done criterion compares a stored object, and a later price correction (a new `price` row) changes today's figure, never the stored month-end. Fixtures `valuation.yaml`, `net_worth.yaml`. *Amended 13 Nov 2026 (step 5b and its review, PR #254):* (a) **a price a person types („Kurs eintragen“) ranks first on its own day**, above the trade's own price: the table is append-only and unique per asset, day and source, so a wrong fetched price could otherwise never be corrected; the fetched price applies again from the next day. The order is now `user`, `trade`, `issuer_nav`, `statement`, `kraken_daily`, `coingecko_daily`, `ecb_fx` (Kraken before CoinGecko was already step 0's code; this decision's prose had not said it). (b) **(4)'s guard is applied on read:** a depot position whose latest current quarterly report disagrees with the movements booked through it is valued only through the newest earlier report that agrees, else not at all, and is left out of the total; a confirmation booked later that makes the report agree values it again without a new report; an account with no report is valued as its movements stand. The ETF tab and the Übersicht read the same check. *Amended 15 Nov 2026 (c):* **a report's price (value ÷ units, `statement`) is stored also when its position agrees only later.** A quarterly report usually arrives before some of its trade confirmations, so its position disagreed when it was taken over and stored no price; the holdings card cleared once the confirmation was applied, but no price ever followed, so a fund without an issuer feed kept its „Kein Kurs“ card and stayed unvalued (the live case: two depots, 15 funds). Now applying a depot movement through the pre-filled form (a trade, distribution or transfer note; the only path that confirms a depot's units) re-checks that depot's current report positions and stores the price of each one that agrees now and has none from that reading yet; „Neu auslesen“ of the report does the same for its unchanged positions; `hausbuch store-report-prices` does it once over every depot, for the confirmations applied before this change. Idempotent; the stored row's `reconciled` flag keeps what the check said when the report came in. A fund that a depot's latest report lists while that report disagrees, or omits while the movements still hold it, gets no separate „Kein Kurs“ card (checked on read, like the holdings card): the holdings card („Depotbericht stimmt nicht“) names the fix and now adds „Sobald der Bericht stimmt, liefert er auch die Kurse der Fonds.“; a fund that keeps its card now says why in true words: no report names it yet, the latest report states no value for it, or no current report values it (it said „es liegt noch kein Depotbericht vor“ in every case). No migration. Merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #325, images 7ec2eb18c093, no migration); `store-report-prices` stored 0 prices that day: neither joint depot has a trade confirmation booked yet (170 trades listed by the reports, 5 Mar to 8 Sep 2026). *Amended 15 Nov 2026 (d), step 6 (migration 0040):* **a stored month-end holds the property and its loans too.** 0025's `account_id` was required, so step 6 as first built stored the accounts and positions only and recomputed the property's estimate and the loans' balances on read: a later „Wert heute“ or loan change moved a past month-end's total, against this decision's "a stored object". Migration `0040_valuation_snapshot_scope` makes `account_id` nullable and adds `property_id` and `loan_id`; each row values exactly one of an account (asset null: its balance; else a position), a property or a loan (negative: what is owed); the run's unique key spans all three. A stored month-end's figures, classes and total are its rows; only the labels and the owner split (the account shares in force on the month-end, the property's shares) are looked up on read. The downgrade is refused while a property or loan row exists. *Amended 15 Nov 2026 (e), step 6's review:* **the 1st's task stores the month-end again when today's reading of it differs from the stored run** (it first wrote only when the month-end had no run, so a „Monatsabschluss festhalten“ pressed on the 31st, before the last lines and prices were in, froze that early figure). The stored runs stay immutable and the newest run of a month-end wins on read; after the 1st, a later correction changes a stored month-end only when a person presses the button, which the Übersicht then offers as „Monatsabschluss neu speichern“ beside the difference. | Principles 2 and 4: a valuation is a derived figure over append-only rows with a `fetched_at`, so "as of" is reproducible; the design's precedence (the trade's own EUR leg first) is kept for the day of the trade; four free sources cover own use (the Stack row says they must be replaced or contracted for SaaS). |
| D168 | **Private-account lines reach the model only behind a new switch with both owners' consent, with the rental account's minimisation and a household prompt.** *(Amended 12 Nov 2026, evening, Luis: "that's my personal account it has no information from her. It shouldn't need approval from her. Also the joint account is only for household expenses. We transfer to it from our personal accounts … No salary, no employer". The switching member's consent is now enough (`CONSENTS_NEEDED = 1`); another member may still add their own. As built in step 1 the switch waited for a second member's click, and the workspace has one member, so it could never take effect: Luis's first private import of 106 lines got „0 Zahlungen an die KI geschickt“ with no reason shown. The retry now counts the private lines it holds back and says why (`AiRetryResult.private_held_back`). Merged and deployed 12 Nov 2026 (PR #230, images 805a22111d84, no migration). The text below is the original design.)* `workspace.ai_private_accounts boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false` (0025); the Einstellungen switch „Private Konten mit KI zuordnen“ follows the `ai_tenant_documents` pattern (phase 2 §11.2) and the phase 4b D103 consent record: switching it on writes `workspace.settings_changed` with `consented_by` = the signed-in member and asks for the other owner's consent as a second click by that member, recorded as its own event, because a joint account's lines name owner B's employer, her pay, her purchases, and some lines are Art. 9 GDPR data by their counterparty (a church, a union, a pharmacy, a clinic). While the switch is off, a private line no rule, matcher or document explains goes to review with every household code offered (`routing.ai_disabled`), which is a workable mode for a month of ~100 lines. What the model sees (`CategorizeInput` of a `private` line, `ai/prompts/categorize_household.md`): the counterparty name, the purpose text, the booking text, the amount, the booking date, the last four characters of the counterparty IBAN, the account's **kind** (never its label or its owner's name), the household codes with one line each, and up to five similar confirmed answers **from private accounts only** (name and category, never amounts); never another account's lines, a tenant's name, a property label (the rental prompt keeps those). The prompt's first line changes from "a German private landlord's bank statement; the rental runs on a dedicated account" to the household's account, and the rental prompt is untouched. Auto-accept: `auto_accept_llm` (D26) applies as today, first month never; thresholds per D159; a model answer with a transfer or tax-relevant code always goes to review (D40 extended). The depot's documents are read under the terms of the property documents once the same switch is on (the second owner's consent of 9 Nov 2026 covered the pay documents; the depot is joint, and both owners agreed on 12 Nov 2026 to the private bank lines and the depot's documents alike, §17 Q2, with owner B's own click still recorded in the app as the consent event); the exchange export is never sent (D166). Scrubbed from every depot extraction's quotes and never stored: depot number, customer number, IdNr, Steuernummer, IBAN, date of birth, address (the `scrub_identifiers` list of phase 4b, plus depot and customer numbers). | Phase 2 §11 and phase 4b D103: a new class of personal data gets its own explicit decision by the people it concerns; the minimisation rules of the AI layer apply unchanged. |
| D169 | **A retention class for holdings: a trade confirmation or an exchange export is kept while the position is held and 10 years after the year it is sold.** `RetentionClass.HOLDING_RECORDS` (`while_held` hold: the document is held while any asset it carries a movement for has a non-zero position on the account; the 10 years count from the end of the year the position reaches zero; a document with no movement yet is `untyped`-held as today), for `trade_confirmation` and `exchange_export`; `depot_statement`, `depot_report`, `distribution_note` (a new subtype, D165) and `capital_income_tax_certificate` are `tax_records` (10 years after the tax year). The retention table in ARCHITECTURE gains the row (§16). | A disposal's gain in phase 8 is computed from the acquisition's confirmation however old; the phase 1 precedent is `ownership_records` for the Kaufvertrag. |
| D170 | **Deferred to phase 8, with the screen saying so: the Sparer-Pauschbetrag and Vorabpauschale cards, the Freistellungsauftrag record, the tax certificate's figures.** Mockup 09b shows both cards; computing them needs the Basiszins per year as rule data, the Teilfreistellung per fund class and the per-owner split of the Pauschbetrag, which are phase 8's `kap_2026/` fixtures; a card with a figure this phase cannot check would break principle 1's spirit (a number the user believes). The ETF tab shows the two cards' places as „Kommt mit Anlage KAP (Phase 8)“ and the documents the Postbox list holds. The `freistellungsauftrag` table of the design's §9.3 is **not** in 0025. *Taken up by phase 8 D216, D217, D220, D221 (spec merged 16 Nov 2026, PR #384, not built): the certificate read by the model and confirmed (D216, D217), the Freistellungsauftrag a `return_input` kind and the Sparer-Pauschbetrag card (D220), the Vorabpauschale forecast (D221); the ETF tab's placeholder „Kommt mit Anlage KAP (Phase 8)“ gives way to the two cards in phase 8 step 5.* | Phase 4 D91 precedent: a card that cannot be computed correctly is not shown half-right. |
| D171 (revised 12 Nov 2026) | **Migration `0025_household_accounts` adds four columns to `account`, eight tables, one column each to `workspace` and `bank_connection`, rebuilds four CHECKs; nothing is dropped or moved.** `account`: `kind`, `purpose`, `provider`, `currency`, `external_ref_masked`, `external_ref_hmac` (D156), with the data step of D156 (every existing account `kind = bank_current`; `purpose = rental` for the account that has a `statement_import`, `private` otherwise; a workspace with two accounts that have imports is refused by the migration, which cannot pick); `account_ownership`, `asset`, `price`, `holding_statement`, `transfer_link`, `valuation_snapshot` append-only (`forbid_mutation`); `asset_movement` append-only with a `superseded_by` like `allocation` (D23), with `network`, `tx_hash` and `fee_quantity` (D175); `movement_import` mutable with status (like `statement_import`); the `allocation.category` and `matching_rule.category` CHECKs rebuilt with the codes of D159 and D165 (the 0012 pattern), `document.subtype` CHECK gains `exchange_export` and `distribution_note` (0021 pattern), `statement_import.format` CHECK gains `dkb_csv` (D173; `mt940` only by the amendment D173 names), `bank_connection.credential_slot` as a named slot under a pattern CHECK, default `primary` (D163), `workspace.ai_private_accounts` (D168), `price.source` CHECK with `issuer_nav` (D174). `workspace_id` on every table, `UNIQUE (workspace_id, id)`, composite FKs, FORCE RLS with `workspace_isolation`, `hausbuch_app` grants, the `tables.py` mirror and the schema-consistency test as every migration since 0001; the downgrade is refused while any row of the eight tables, any account of a kind other than `bank_current`, any allocation with a new code, any import of format `dkb_csv`, any connection with a slot other than `primary` or any workspace with the switch on exists. | The 0019 docstring is the template; mutable vs append-only follows what each row is. |
| D172 | **The done criterion is measured by the app from stored rows, and the first-month exception is explicit.** `GET /accounts/auto-rate?month=YYYY-MM` returns, per private bank account and in total, the confirmed allocations of the month by method and routing, and the ratio of the header; „every transfer linked“ is `GET /transfers/open` returning zero legs past their window; the month-end check is a stored `valuation_snapshot` against Luis's spreadsheet, recorded in §14.1 with the date. The first imported month (the 2026 history) is excluded from the 80 % criterion by definition (D26: the model never auto-accepts in the first month, and every merchant is new); the criterion is read on the **second** calendar month after the first import. *D172 amended 15 Nov 2026 (orchestrator's decision: the done criterion asks what the app booked without a person):* an allocation counts as automatic only when its method is `rule`, `matcher_transfer`, `document_match` or `llm` **and no person confirmed it** (`confirmed_by` null). A proposal a person confirms keeps its proposer's method, so as first built a rule's near-miss proposal or a bill-payment proposal (which on a private account only ever proposes) counted as automatic once a person confirmed it; `by_routing` still splits the month by who confirmed. | A criterion nobody can compute is not a criterion; phase 2's was measured the same way (`lines_new`, reconciliation). **Amended 18 Nov 2026 (Luis: the Consors Finanz card, unused since January, has no newer statement):** a credit card idle by phase 5 D122 amended (6) (its latest statement closed at 0 and no own account paid it since) counts at 0 on every later day, its line is not stale and no „Kreditkartenabrechnung hochladen“ to-do is raised, so it no longer holds a month-end incomplete; until then every month-end since February had waited for a statement the issuer never sends. |
| D173 (12 Nov 2026, from Luis's §17 Q1) | **DKB joins the supported banks: the same read-only facade and decoupled flow over FinTS, camt only if its bank parameters offer it, its CSV export through a reader of its own, and one source per account until the first real connection answers what the BPD holds.** (1) **FinTS.** DKB's parameters (its own page, §3): FinTS 3.0 at `https://fints.dkb.de/fints`, BLZ 12030000, the Anmeldename as the login, the customer id left empty, security medium „DKB-App (decoupled)“ or chipTAN; python-fints 5.0.1 (the pinned version) handles DKB's decoupled answer, which lists 3955 followed by 0030 in one message (the 3955 flag was overwritten in 4.2.3, fixed in January 2025 and confirmed working with a registered product id in May 2026, §3), so D118's polling of `send_tan` applies unchanged and the card says „Jetzt in der DKB-App freigeben“ when the connection's institute is DKB (the text comes from a small table keyed by the BPD's bank name, never typed). **A chipTAN-only login fails with `bank.tan_not_decoupled` as today.** (2) **The format.** Whether DKB's BPD lists HICAZS (camt, HKCAZ) could not be verified from any public source (§3); the facade already refuses a bank whose parameters list no readable camt with `bank.unsupported_format` (`services/bank/fints.py`, read 12 Nov 2026), so a DKB connection without camt fails cleanly before any read, and the card says to use the CSV export (3). An MT940 reader (`StatementFormat.MT940`, HKKAZ through the facade, `mt-940` already pinned, balances from `:60F:`/`:62F:` so D21's reconciliation reads the file, the `:61:` bank reference as the hash's reference when the bank fills it and D22's position fallback otherwise) is **designed here and built only by a spec amendment with its own fixture** if the first real connection shows no camt (Q-O70).
