(3) **The CSV export** (secondary sources of 2025, §3; the exact layout is settled on the anonymised real export in step 1, as the camt was in phase 2 and as Q-O59 does for BISON): the web banking's „Umsätze exportieren“ gives a semicolon-separated UTF-8 file with a BOM, four metadata lines (account type, IBAN, „Kontostand vom DD.MM.YYYY“) and the header in line 5: `Buchungsdatum`, `Wertstellung`, `Status`, `Zahlungspflichtige*r`, `Zahlungsempfänger*in`, `Verwendungszweck`, `Umsatztyp`, `IBAN`, `Betrag (€)`, `Gläubiger-ID`, `Mandatsreferenz`, `Kundenreferenz`; dates `DD.MM.YY`, amounts German with a comma, in quotes; one source reports the delimiter and the period line changed in 2024/25, so the reader sniffs the delimiter and the BOM and finds the header by its first column name. `services/bank/dkb_csv.parse(data) -> ParsedStatement` (`StatementFormat.DKB_CSV`, detected by its header line) maps to `StatementLine` as `csv_camt.py` does: a line with `Status ≠ Gebucht` is dropped (the camt `PDNG` analogue), the counterparty name from the payer or payee column by the amount's sign, the IBAN column as the counterparty IBAN (masked and HMAC'd on store), `Mandatsreferenz`, `Gläubiger-ID` and `Kundenreferenz` kept in the purpose for the rules and the model. **Balances**: the file carries no opening balance and no period line in its 2025 layout, and whether „Kontostand vom <date>“ is the balance of the export day or of the range's last booking day is unknown (Q-O75), so the reader cannot prove the range from the file. The import therefore waits at `pending_balance` exactly as the Sparkasse CSV does (D21), with the Kontostand's value and date **pre-filled** into the closing-balance field and the question „Ist das der Saldo nach der letzten Buchung vom <last booking day>?“ for the user to confirm or correct (principle 7); `balance_source = file` for DKB only once the real export has answered Q-O75 and the reader can prove the range, by a spec amendment. **Dedup**: the file carries no bank-assigned reference (`Kundenreferenz` and `Mandatsreferenz` are the counterparty's), so `line_hash` takes D22's fallback (the line's content and its position among identical lines), and a later camt import of the same days hashes differently. (4) **One source per account.** Because the CSV and a FinTS camt share no reference, D116's overlap guarantee does not hold for DKB. So: the 2026 history of a DKB account comes from the CSV export (it reaches back years; a FinTS window may be limited to about three months, §3); a FinTS connection created afterwards gets its **first** window from the day after the account's last reconciled closing date, not from the anchor: `SyncWindowFacts` gains `imports_through` (the last reconciled closing date of the account's imports, `None` without any) and `request_window` starts a first sync (`last_booking_on is None`) at `max(opening_balance_on + 1, imports_through + 1)`, unchanged otherwise (the 14-day overlap after a successful sync stays; for a Sparkasse the shared reference keeps it free). *Amended 12 Nov 2026 (step 3 review):* the overlap of a later sync is bounded below by the end of the account's history from files without the bank's reference: `SyncWindowFacts.unreferenced_through` = the last booking day of the account's reconciled imports whose format is not `camt` (`dkb_csv`, `csv_camt`, `pdf`), and a later window starts at `max(last_booking_on − 14, unreferenced_through + 1)`. Without it, connecting within two weeks of the CSV export (the order this decision prescribes) fetched the CSV's last days again on the second sync, stored both copies (they share no reference), and left that import unreconciled behind a stack of duplicate cards; `sync_window.yaml` gains the case. What the window boundary still leaves (a line booked after the export's closing but before its date, a value-dated line) is what D22's cross-format duplicate review exists for, and D117's HKSAL drift check catches a gap. Phase 5's `sync_window.yaml` gains the case, an amendment of D120 recorded in §16. (5) **The approval cadence.** Two third-party guides of late 2024 and a developer's note of 2026 report that DKB asks for an approval at every login and every retrieval, against the Sparkasse's 90 days (§3, unverified). If the first manual syncs show it, D120's daily schedule would raise a `needs_approval` card every morning; so a DKB connection's „Täglich automatisch abrufen“ stays **off** until two consecutive manual syncs have run without an approval, the card saying why, and if every sync needs one the account is synced by hand or imported from its CSV (Q-O71). (6) **PDF statements.** DKB's page says its electronic statements are served over FinTS as PDF through HKEKP; D154 applies unchanged when the BPD holds HIEKPS v1 with the number allowed, and is skipped silently otherwise. (7) **Unverified until the first real connection** (the bank's BPD answers them, as phase 5's Q-O34–Q-O36 did for the Sparkasse): HICAZS present and which camt versions; HKTAN version and the decoupled mechanism's name and id; the approval cadence; HIEKPS; whether private customers see every account of the login (DKB's own FinTS page is written for business customers; a third-party guide says every account the web banking shows is served). Fixture `dkb_csv.yaml`. | Luis's accounts are at three institutes and one is DKB; the facade, the import path and the reconciliation are bank-agnostic by design (D115), so the cost is one CSV reader, one window rule and a card text; the camt question is a fact only the bank's parameters state, and phase 5 learned to leave such facts to the first connection rather than guess them. **Amended 13 Nov 2026 (PR #235):** the closing balance the file states („Kontostand vom“) can be confirmed on the account page too („Endsaldo eingeben“), and since the starting balance of a DKB account is not shown anywhere, the earliest import offers „Anfangssaldo daraus übernehmen“: the starting balance worked out as the stated closing balance less the lines, set on the account only when the user clicks. |
| D174 (12 Nov 2026, Luis's §17 Q4: "These are standard funds. You can fetch their prices at the daily close from the funds' issuer.") | **An `issuer_nav` price source: the fund's own published daily NAV per ISIN, one adapter per issuer, keyed through the asset registry; built per issuer when a held ISIN needs it and verified on the real page; the documents stay the fallback.** (1) **What the issuers publish** (checked 12 Nov 2026, §3; quirion's published investment universe names iShares, Amundi (with Lyxor), SPDR (State Street), Xtrackers (DWS), Invesco, UBS, HSBC, BNP Paribas Easy, Vanguard and the active Assenagon fund): State Street serves a per-fund NAV history as an XLSX at a stable path keyed by the fund's ticker slug (`/library-content/products/fund-data/etfs/emea/navhist-emea-en-<ticker>.xlsx`, seen on the SPDR MSCI World page with the NAV of 11 Nov 2026) — **usable**; Vanguard's product page shows a ten-day NAV table and a „Download <n> prices“ link, but its terms forbid "devices or software that provide repeated automated access" and limit the site to personal, non-commercial use, so a daily fetch was **not usable as designed** until Luis read that clause; he did on 12 Nov 2026 (Q-O73: one request a day per held fund for the household's own use is acceptable to him), so Vanguard gets an adapter like State Street, written from the real download in step 5; iShares' product pages state the NAV of the day and its terms allow personal and internal use with no clause on automation, but the NAV-history download could not be confirmed on the fetched pages (only a document endpoint) — **adapter written once the endpoint is read from a real product page**; Xtrackers (DWS) pages are rendered by script and could not be read (a search snippet names a Historical Data download in Excel); Amundi's and UBS's product pages could not be found at the guessed addresses; HSBC's, Invesco's and BNP's pages were not reached — all **no adapter yet, documents fallback, Q-O74**. (2) **The adapter protocol** (`services/prices/issuer_nav/`): `IssuerNavAdapter.fetch(fund_ref) -> list[NavPoint(on, nav, currency)]` over the HTTP client the TEM client uses, with a `User-Agent` that names Hausbuch, one request per held ISIN per day, a 20 s timeout and a 5 MB cap; the registry `rules/household/assets.yaml` maps an ISIN to `issuer` (`spdr`, `ishares`, …), `fund_ref` (the issuer's own key: a ticker slug, a product id) and `nav_currency`; a NAV in another currency becomes `price_eur` by the ECB rate of its day (D167 (3), fetched first in the same run), with the native figure and currency kept in `raw`. An XLSX is read as a ZIP of XML with `zipfile` and the pinned `lxml` (shared strings, the sheet's rows), a CSV with the standard library; no new dependency. (3) **Storage and precedence**: rows `price(source = issuer_nav, on = the NAV's valuation day)`, append-only, only for days on or after the asset's first confirmed movement and not yet stored (the files are full histories, so one fetch brings every missing day; at most 400 rows per asset per run); D167 (2)'s date-first precedence puts the day's NAV below the trade's own price on the trade day and above a report's value; the quarterly report's value stays stored as `statement` and reconciles the holdings (D165 (3)), and the ETF tab's „Kurs“ shows the NAV with its date and source. (4) **Failure**: a fetch that fails or a file the adapter cannot parse (the issuer changed the page) logs the adapter's name, the exception class and the HTTP status only, stores nothing and leaves the price stale (D167's rule unchanged); after seven consecutive days without a new NAV for a held ETF whose adapter exists, the card `price.source_failed` (Zu prüfen › Automatisch) names the fund and the issuer and offers „Kurs eintragen“ (a typed `user` price, the lowest precedence) and the note that the next report or trade confirmation prices it again; a held ETF with no adapter shows „Kurs aus dem letzten Bericht“ and no card. (5) **Terms**: own use, one daily request per fund, the figures stored for the household only and never redistributed; the Anlagen screen's attribution line names the issuers' NAV; for SaaS the Stack row says a licensed NAV feed replaces the adapters. Fixture `issuer_nav.yaml`; the real files are read from Luis's computer in step 5 and never committed (the eval-set rule of 6 Nov 2026). *Amended 16 Nov 2026 (merged and deployed 16 Nov 2026, PR #381, images 07438ec2d478, no migration; this marker said „not merged, not deployed“ until then):* **the live price table held no `issuer_nav` row on 15 Nov 2026 although some held funds are in the registry; the registry is now a seed, and a held iShares or SPDR fund it does not list is found by its ISIN in the issuer's own catalogue.** (a) *Why no NAV row (settled on the live data the evening of 15 Nov 2026 UTC, read-only):* timing. Both price runs so far (14 and 15 Nov, 07:00 UTC) asked only `ecb_fx` and `kraken_daily`, because no fund was held yet: every fund movement of the two joint depots was confirmed in the afternoon of 15 Nov 2026 with „Umsätze aus dem Bericht buchen“ (PR #330), after that day's run. The registry funds held count the same under the deployed rule and under the per-account rule of (b), so netting was not the cause on these data; (b) stays as a correctness fix for a later move between own depots. The run of 16 Nov 07:00 UTC is the first to ask the issuers. No command or button runs the price fetch outside its 07:00 schedule. (b) *Fixed:* held is now units above zero on some account, its first day the earliest movement on any account (the fetch and the price cards read `held_subquery`; the Anlagen overview keeps its own units query, so a position negative on one account and positive on another is still valued on both, a follow-up). An iShares file whose fund name carries a bare „&“ (a real fund's file, read live 16 Nov 2026) was refused as bad XML; such an ampersand is escaped before parsing, nothing else repaired. (c) *Discovery:* an adapter may name its issuer's catalogue (`catalogue_url`) and the words a fund's name carries for it (`brands`). A held ETF with no registry row and no issuer NAV stored yet, whose stored name carries a brand (iShares: „iShares“, „iShs“; SPDR: „SPDR“, „SSgA“, „State Street“), is looked up once per run in that catalogue: iShares' product-screener data (`https://www.ishares.com/varnish-api/blk-product-screener-server/api/v1/product-screener/product-data?country=de&language=de&siteName=de-ishares-v2&userType=individual`, one JSON document of about 4 MB), every product's `portfolioId` and ISIN; SPDR's German fund finder data (`https://www.ssga.com/bin/v1/ssmp/fund/fundfinder?country=de&language=de&role=individual&product=etfs&ui=fund-finder`, about 600 kB), the ISIN and ticker slug of each fund's document links. An ISIN listed under two references is never guessed; a catalogue that cannot be read counts as `issuer_nav:<issuer>:catalogue` in the run's errors and fails nothing else. The reference found is only a candidate: the NAV file must state the same ISIN (`isin_mismatch` otherwise, nothing stored), and it is stored with every price row it brings, as `raw.issuer` and `raw.fund_ref`, so the next run asks no catalogue and the ETF tab names the issuer („NAV iShares“). A registry row always wins. Checked live on 16 Nov 2026: both catalogues reproduce the registry's 10 references, none different; the robots.txt of www.ishares.com, www.blackrock.com and www.ssga.com disallow none of the catalogue or NAV file paths. No migration (the reference lives in `price.raw`, a JSONB column 0025 created). A fund whose name carries no brand, or whose issuer has no catalogue, stays on „Kurs aus dem letzten Bericht“. *Amended again 16 Nov 2026, the three follow-ups of PR #381's review (PR #391, merged and deployed 16 Nov 2026, images e4f92a339fd7; no migration, no OpenAPI change; with the fixes of its review; this said „not merged, not deployed“ until then):* (A) *A moved fund:* a stored catalogue reference whose file answers HTTP 404 or 410, or 400 (what iShares answers for a product id it does not know; a wrong guess costs one catalogue lookup, and the file read through the catalogue's reference must state the ISIN anyway), or states another ISIN (`isin_mismatch`), asks that issuer's catalogue once in the same run (by the stored issuer, not the fund's name), and a different reference listed there for the ISIN is read at once; its rows keep the new `raw.fund_ref`, so the next run reads it without a catalogue. A timeout, a 5xx or an unreadable file is an outage, never a reason to ask the catalogue; a registry row is not looked up again (the registry is fixed by a data change). (B) *The card for a catalogue fund:* every fund whose issuer file failed in a run (or whose NAV currency (C) cannot be converted) is named in that run's `workspace.prices_fetched` event with the issuer source asked (`fund_sources`: asset id, issuer, reference, never a price; a file read fine that brings no new day names nothing), so a fund whose catalogue reference never stored a row (its file failed from the first day) gets D174 (4)'s card „Der Kurs von … kam seit n Tagen nicht von iShares“ after seven days, like a registry fund, instead of „veröffentlicht die Fondsgesellschaft keinen Kurs“, which was false; a run before this change named no source, so such a fund's failed days alone also lead to the card, naming „der Fondsgesellschaft“. The card now says „in der Vermögensübersicht fehlt sein Wert“ when the fund has no price at all, instead of „beruht auf einem älteren Kurs“. Only a failure in the latest run leads there: a fund without a registry row or stored reference whose latest run did not fail it (its catalogue was down, so it was not asked) falls through to the no-source card, at once when it has no price at all; when an older run named its issuer, that card says „Für … hat Hausbuch keinen aktuellen Kurs: <Issuer> hat ihn zuletzt nicht geliefert“ and offers „Kurs eintragen“, never that the issuer publishes no price (the review of PR #391 found the older note had hidden every card for up to a month). (C) *NAV currencies:* the fetch reads every currency of the ECB file and stores, besides USD, GBP and CHF, the rate of any currency a held fund's NAV is quoted in (the 90-day file too on a first need), and once the workspace holds a rate of such a currency it stores the new ones on every run like the dollar's, whether or not the fund's file was read, so a NAV day a failed file left to a later run is converted by its own day's rate; a NAV in yen or crowns is thus converted like one in dollars. A currency is converted only when the ECB's answer of the run lists it on its newest day; one the ECB stopped publishing is treated like one it never published (below), never converted at its last, frozen rate (the review of PR #391 found both stale-rate paths); a rate the reader cannot read drops that currency only (one of the three still refuses the file). A NAV in a currency the ECB does not publish is never stored (never a price in the wrong currency): the run names the fund and the currency in `fund_sources`, and Zu prüfen shows at once the card „<Issuer> veröffentlicht den Kurs von … in <Währung>. Für diese Währung gibt die Europäische Zentralbank keinen Referenzkurs heraus …“ with „Kurs eintragen“, until a person types a price (kind `price_source_failed`, so no new card kind). Both this card and the seven-day card come back about a month after a typed price, while the cause lasts, as a reminder that the price is still typed by hand (kept on purpose after the review). *Amended 19 Nov 2026 (specced in the spec PR of the branch `docs/d174-more-issuers`; built on `feat/d174-more-issuers`; neither merged nor deployed):* **an adapter per issuer for the held funds that only the quarterly reports priced, older ECB rates so a foreign-currency NAV is stored back to a fund's first movement, and a check on a fund's first read.** Luis, 19 Nov 2026: a daily NAV per issuer (most of the held funds were priced only by the depot's quarterly reports, so every month-end that is not a quarter-end was incomplete and phase 9 stored no key figures for it); read on 19 Nov 2026 (§3, „Issuers' NAV, verified on held funds 19 Nov 2026“). **Consent (Luis, 19 Nov 2026: „Read them, own use only“):** Xtrackers, ARK Invest Europe and Legal & General are read although Xtrackers' robots.txt disallows `/api/` and its terms §5.1 say „Es ist nicht gestattet, die Webseiten im Ganzen oder in Teilen oder Inhalte auf diesen Webseiten – sofern nicht gesetzlich erlaubt - ohne vorherige schriftliche Zustimmung herunterzuladen, zu vervielfältigen und/oder zu verbreiten“, Legal & General's robots.txt disallows `/srp/api/`, and ARK's terms say „No part of (or information contained on) this website may be reproduced, distributed or transmitted without the prior written permission of ARK Invest International Ltd.“; this is Luis's informed choice for the household's own use, one request a day per held fund (two for Invesco and HANetf, one for all of Legal & General's), the figures stored for the household only and never redistributed, recorded like Vanguard's (Q-O73). Amundi's and BNP Paribas' services are their pages' internal services, not published downloads, and are read on the same terms (Amundi's notice allows downloading parts of the site „ausschließlich für Ihren persönlichen Gebrauch“; BNP's names no clause on automated access). (a) *Older ECB rates:* `nav_rows` stores no foreign-currency NAV without an ECB rate on or before its day, and the app read only the ECB's daily and 90-day files, so a USD fund had rows only from about mid-August 2026 although its issuer's file is a full history. `ecb_fx` also reads the ECB's full history (`https://www.ecb.europa.eu/stats/eurofxref/eurofxref-hist.zip`, one CSV `eurofxref-hist.csv`, about 650 kB, newest first, a trailing comma, „N/A“ for a day a currency has no rate; unpacked size capped at 8 MB) when the earliest stored rate of FX_CURRENCIES is later than the earliest first movement of a held fund (the NAV currency of a fund without a registry row is not known before its file is read, so the test is on the funds' first day, not per currency); it passes the rates from that day on, plus the last rate before it, through the run's FX like the 90-day file's, so the missing days only are stored and the same run's NAVs use them. The ZIP is asked at most once a run and only while the gap lasts: after it is stored, the next run asks the daily file only. A ZIP that fails is counted in the run's log line, the daily or 90-day file is still read, and the next run asks again; it never fails the run. Days before the first movement stay out ((3) unchanged), and at most 400 rows per asset and run are stored, so a longer gap finishes on the next run. (b) *New adapters* (the URL, layout and quirks of each in §3 and §14.1): **Amundi** (with the former Lyxor funds; `POST https://www.amundietf.de/mapi/ProductAPI/getProductsData`, a JSON body asking the ISIN's `officialNav` and `navCurrency` from the fund's first movement to today; the answer states the ISIN and the currency, each day in epoch milliseconds read as a UTC day, a point on a weekend kept under its own day; reference: the ISIN); **Xtrackers (DWS)** (`GET https://etf.deutsche-am.com/api/pdp/de-de/etf/<ISIN>/historicaldata/download?startDate=<first movement>&endDate=<today>&includeTax=false`, an XLSX stating the ISIN and the currency, the NAV column found by its header, a row without a NAV skipped; reference: the ISIN); **Invesco** (two requests: the share class's JSON `https://dng-api.invesco.com/cache/v1/accounts/de_DE/shareclasses/<ISIN>?expand=nav&idType=isin`, with the ISIN, currency, full name and the latest NAV with its day, and the workbook `…/shareclasses/<ISIN>/performance?idType=isin`, which states neither ISIN nor currency; the workbook is accepted only when its share-class name equals the JSON's and its NAV on the JSON's day equals the JSON's to 8 places, otherwise `isin_mismatch` and nothing is stored; a workbook with more than one share-class block is refused, since no multi-class workbook was read; reference: the ISIN); **ARK Invest Europe** (the Rize funds; the fund page `https://europe.ark-funds.com/funds/<section>/<slug>/`, whose inline chart series `rawChartData` (day, NAV) is read with the page's own ISIN and „Base Currency“ from its key facts, never a constituent's ISIN from the holdings table; the series' last value must equal the page's „NAV per share“, otherwise the page is unreadable; catalogue `https://europe.ark-funds.com/funds/`, ISIN and page link per fund; reference `<section>/<slug>`); **BNP Paribas Asset Management** (`GET https://api.bnpparibas-am.com/push-raw/navs/fast?currency=<CUR>&product_gids=<ISIN>&granularity=daily`, rows of type `PRICE_NAV` keyed by the ISIN, out of order; the answer states no currency and is an empty list for a wrong one, so the reference is `<CUR>:<ISIN>`, found at discovery by asking EUR, USD, GBP and CHF once each and kept only when exactly one of them has rows, see (h); this said „stopping at the first currency with rows“ until the review of 19 Nov 2026); **Legal & General** (`GET https://fundcentres.landg.com/srp/api/fund-centre/48?audience=150&language=3`, one document with every share class's ISIN, currency and latest NAV with its day, `midPrice`, which the document's own field list labels NAV, its values positional by `metadata.share_class_fields`; one request a run for all held funds; **one point a day and no history**, so a day whose run failed is not recovered and the past month-ends are not backfilled; an ISIN under two share classes, or a NAV without its day, is refused, never guessed; reference: the ISIN); **HANetf** (it answered the app's own client on 19 Nov 2026: the fund sitemap, one fund page and its NAV file; two requests per fund: the English fund page `https://hanetf.com/fund/<slug>/`, whose key facts state the ISIN and „Base Currency“ and which links the NAV history `https://hanetf.com/wp-content/assets/upload/NAV_history-<TICKER>-<ISIN>-all-all.xlsx`, then that file, whose ISIN must equal the page's; reference: the page's slug). (c) *The protocol, amending (2):* a request may be a POST with a JSON body (the HTTP client's `Fetch` gains an optional body, sent only when present; still no redirect, no cookie, the same user agent, timeout and cap). An adapter makes **at most two requests per held fund per run, or one request for all its funds** (cached for the run, whatever the number of workspaces); „one request per held ISIN per day“ stays the rule for an adapter whose file is self-describing. An adapter whose service takes a date range asks from the fund's first movement to today, never only the days after the newest stored NAV, so an older gap is never skipped; `nav_rows` still stores only the missing days. (d) *Discovery, amending 16 Nov (c):* an adapter keyed by the ISIN itself (Amundi, Xtrackers, Invesco, BNP, Legal & General) needs no catalogue: a held fund with no registry row and no stored issuer reference, whose stored name carries one of the adapter's brands, is asked by its ISIN, and the answer must state that ISIN. ARK keeps a catalogue like iShares and SPDR. HANetf's sitemap names no ISIN, so its fund pages whose slug words the held fund's name starts (at least two of them, the name's own brand words and „ICAV“, „UCITS“, „ETF“, „UETF“, „Acc“ and „Dist“ left out) are read, the best match first and at most three a run, until one states the ISIN. A lookup that finds nothing, or whose answer states another ISIN or fails, stores nothing, is no failure and raises no card: the fund stays on its report price as before; it costs at most four requests a run per such fund (one for most issuers), counted in the run's log line. A reference found is stored with the price (`raw.issuer`, `raw.fund_ref`) as before, so the next runs read the fund directly; once it has priced a fund, a failure of its file counts toward the seven-day card like any other. Brands (lower-case word starts): Amundi `amundi`, `amu`, `lyxor`; Xtrackers `xtrackers`, `xtr`; Invesco `invesco`; ARK `ark inv`, `ark i`, `rize`; BNP `bnp p`, `bnpp`; Legal & General `l&g`, `legal & general`; HANetf `hanetf`. No held fund is added to the registry for this. (e) *Not readable:* UBS and WisdomTree answer 403 to a non-browser client and are never imitated; Dimensional's service gives no history and its terms forbid repeated automated access. Their funds stay on „Kurs aus dem letzten Bericht“; no third-party source whose terms allow a daily automated request was found (Q-O74). **A held fund no adapter can price** keeps its report price, and once that price is more than seven days old the net worth's existing to-do „Der Kurs von … ist vom …“ with „Kurs eintragen“ lets a person type the day's price (a `user` price, D167 amended 13 Nov 2026), which a past month-end then uses: no new code. (f) *Terms, (5) unchanged:* own use, the figures stored for the household only, never redistributed; the Anlagen attribution line („NAV der Fondsgesellschaften“) already names the issuers as a group, and each price row names its issuer (`ISSUER_NAMES`). (g) *The first-read check:* where a source states no ISIN or no currency (BNP, Invesco's workbook) or labels its figure loosely (Legal & General's `midPrice`, ARK's chart), a wrong figure would be stored silently, so on a fund's **first read** (no `issuer_nav` row stored for the asset yet), for every issuer including iShares and SPDR, the newest day of the parsed file (before the first-movement filter and the 400-row cut) on which the depot report also stored a `statement` price for the fund and, for a NAV in another currency, an ECB rate on or before it exists, is compared in euros (`rules.valuation.nav_check`, the NAV converted like a stored row): when the NAV differs from the report's price by more than 2 % of the report's price (`valuation.NAV_CHECK_TOLERANCE`), nothing is stored for the fund in that run, and the run's event names it in a new optional field `PricesFetched.nav_disagreed` (asset ids, default empty, so older events still read); it is not counted as a failed fetch and raises no card (Q-O173), and the fund keeps its report price. When no day can be compared (no report day in the file, as with Legal & General's single point before the next report, or no ECB rate that early because the history ZIP failed), the rows are stored: the check guards what it can compare and says so. The plan reads the `statement` prices of the held funds for this; once a fund has an `issuer_nav` row the check no longer runs for it. Fixtures: issuer_nav.yaml's `read_cases`, `catalogue_cases`, `discovery_cases`, `ecb_history_cases` and `guard_cases` (synthetic samples in the real layouts, invented ISINs, names and values). No migration: the reference lives in `price.raw` (JSONB, migration 0025). **Amended 19 Nov 2026 after the review of the build (one round):** (h) *BNP's currency:* a share class's currency does not change, and BNP's answer states none, so the currency is decided once and never moved. Discovery asks all four currencies once and keeps the reference only when exactly one has rows. When none or several have rows, nothing is stored, and the run's event names the fund in a new optional field `PricesFetched.nav_unresolved` (asset ids, default empty). This is no failed fetch and raises no card; the fund keeps its report price. A lookup that cannot tell (a timeout or a 5xx on one currency) stores nothing and names nothing: it is counted in the run's errors and asked again the next run. A stored `<CUR>:<ISIN>` reference whose currency no longer has rows while another currency does is never switched to that currency. Nothing is stored, the stored reference stays, and the fund is named in `nav_unresolved`, not counted as a failed fetch. When no other currency has rows either, it is a failed fetch as before. (i) *Invesco:* a workbook with more than one share-class block is refused as unreadable (`parse`), whatever the blocks' names: (b)'s rule, which the first build did not enforce. (j) *HANetf's currency:* the NAV history workbook read on 19 Nov 2026 states no currency (only the fund's name, ticker and ISIN above the table), so the page's „Base Currency“ cannot be tied to the file, and the first-read check (g) is the only guard against a wrong currency. (k) *The lookup misses of (d)* are counted in the run's log line as `lookup_misses` (per issuer, kind and status), apart from `errors`, so a fund no issuer lists neither makes the run a warning nor raises a card. Fixtures: issuer_nav.yaml's `discover_bnp_currency` asks all four currencies, and `discover_bnp_two_currencies` finds nothing when two have rows. **Amended 20 Nov 2026 (found in phase 9's closing, Luis's choice of daily prices for every month-end, 19 Nov 2026):** the daily fetch read only the assets some account holds today (`held_subquery`: units > 0 now), so a fund held earlier in the year and sold or moved out since got no daily price for the days it was held, and every past month-end that holds it stayed incomplete with „Kurs vom <report day>“ (two funds of a depot held in spring, so 30 Apr and 31 May could not be stored). From this amendment the fetch plans every asset with confirmed, current movements on some own account, each for the days it was held: from its first movement to its last movement after which no own account holds a unit, or to today while one does (`Held` gains that last day; an asset held again later is fetched for each held stretch or, simpler, from its first movement to the last held day, never beyond). Every source, rule and check is the same as for a held asset: the issuer's NAV first, the exchange price (D264) for a fund no issuer prices daily, the first-read check against the depot report, the ECB rates; a source that serves no history for those days stores nothing and the month-end keeps its to-do, never a guess. An asset no account holds today raises no „no current price“ card and is not counted as a failed asset when only its days after the sale are missing. **When a sold asset is done (amended the same day, from the build's review):** the fetch stops asking for an asset no account holds any more only on what a read made after the sale was known showed, never on rows that happened to be stored while it was held (a fund priced daily until a few days before its sale would otherwise stop with its last held days unpriced, and a month-end in them would read complete on a price up to a week old) and never on the date of the sale or of its booking (the funds this amendment is for were sold months before it). The run event records, per sold asset and held stretch (its first and last held day), whether its sources were read successfully and stored nothing new for those days, cut nothing for the row limit and skipped nothing for a missing rate; such a run ends the asking for that stretch. A stretch whose sources never answer like that is asked at most 10 runs, then no more, whatever the reason; the month-end keeps its to-do („Kurs eintragen“). A stretch that changes (an older statement moves its first day, a later one its last, or the asset is bought again) is asked afresh. A source's failures count toward an asset's „no current price“ card only from the start of its current held stretch. | Luis declined documents-only (a value that is up to a quarter stale is not a net worth), and the issuer's NAV is the one figure that is official for the day; a per-issuer adapter behind the existing `PriceSource` keeps the model out (principle 1) and the source on every row (principle 2); honesty per issuer, because a page that was not read is not a source. |
| D175 (12 Nov 2026, Luis: "the purchase dates can come from the blockchain and from the exchange … A deposit into the wallet doesn't necessarily mean the purchase date") | **An exchange withdrawal stores what phase 7 needs to carry its lot to the wallet; a deposit into an own wallet is never a purchase; a deposit with no known source is an open leg.** `asset_movement` gains `network` (the chain the exchange sent on, as the export names it), `tx_hash` (the transaction hash when the export carries one; BISON's export is believed to carry no address and no hash, Q-O59, so the column may stay empty on the exchange side and be filled from the chain side in phase 7) and `fee_quantity` (the fee in the asset's own units, beside `fee_eur`); a crypto Withdrawal row (D166) is `internal_transfer_out` with `quantity` the amount sent, `fee_quantity` the network fee the exchange charged, and `external_ref` the exchange's transaction id. Phase 7 links the wallet's deposit to it by `transfer_link(kind = chain)` (specified in phase 7 D184 (1)): by `tx_hash` when both sides have one, else by asset, `quantity − fee_quantity` against the deposit's amount, `network` and a 48-hour window, one candidate → linked, several → `transfer.ambiguous`, as D162 does for bank legs; the lot then moves with the exchange's acquisition date and price unchanged (BMF 6 Mar 2025 Rn. 32 and the design's §2.3). **Stated for phase 7 now so that this phase's rows are right**: a deposit into one of the household's own wallets is never an acquisition and never priced on its deposit day; a deposit whose source is not an own exchange or wallet movement stays an open leg (`transfer.unmatched_leg` on the wallet side) for review, where the answers are a purchase elsewhere (typed with its date and price), a reward, or an own transfer the app has not imported, never a default. The old wallet's history is read from the chain in phase 7 (Helius, as agreed 6 Nov 2026; since phase 7 D181 and D192 over standard Solana JSON-RPC from any provider, Helius optional), where an on-chain swap is an acquisition at its own time and price; BISON's history comes from the export Luis provides (D166). No change to `transfer_link`'s shape: `kind` gains `chain` in phase 7 as planned. | Design §2.3 ("lots move with their acquisition date and cost") needs the exchange side to keep the hash, the network and the fee, which the draft's row lacked; a deposit treated as a purchase would reset the holding period and misprice the lot, the exact error the one-year rule punishes. |
| D176 (12 Nov 2026, Luis: "Forgot to say I have a credit card. It's with Avarda bank … If not I can also upload the statements."; chose "own account" over a monthly lump sum) | **The household's credit card is an account of kind `credit_card`, fed by its monthly PDF statements, its purchases categorised like a current account's lines, its monthly settlement linked as an internal transfer, and its open balance a debt in the net worth.** (1) **No connection.** The issuer (Avarda Bank AB, German brand TF Bank) is not in the DK's FinTS institute list (checked 12 Nov 2026) and offers no customer API; an aggregator stays a non-goal. The statements are uploaded. (2) **The account.** `account.kind` gains `credit_card` (migration 0026: the kind CHECK rebuilt, nothing else); purpose `private`; no IBAN (a card account is identified, like a depot, by its `provider` and `external_ref`, the card number masked to its last four digits and HMAC'd, D156); owner shares as every account (D157); listed under Bank on Konten with the chip „Kreditkarte“ and its balance shown as owed. (3) **Statements.** *(Corrected 12 Nov 2026: this point first said the card's PDF statements go "through the existing PDF statement path (phase 2)"; no such path exists. `services/bank/__init__.py` refuses a PDF with „PDF statements are not imported yet“, and only the extraction schema `StatementExtraction` was written in phase 2, so the card was moved to its own step 2b, which builds the import or, if Avarda's portal exports CSV, a CSV reader like DKB's instead, Q-O77.)* *(Settled 12 Nov 2026 on the first real statement, Q-O77: the portal offers PDF only, and the PDF is digital with a clean text layer, so it is read by code, not by the model.)* The monthly PDF statement is parsed by code from its text layer (statement format `card_pdf`, `services/bank/card_pdf.py`, like DKB's CSV reader: no model, no consent gate for the reading itself; categorising its lines follows D158 and D168 like any private line), routed by the statement's customer number (stored as the account's `external_ref`, HMAC'd and masked) with the card's last four digits shown; the full card number is never printed on the statement (it shows the first six and last four) and is never stored or quoted; the summary's old and new balance (Alter Saldo, Neuer Saldo, owed amounts negative) reconcile with the lines to the cent, as a bank statement's do. Each line carries the issuer's own transaction id (Bew.ID), which is the bank's reference for dedup (D22's reference path, not the position fallback). The issuer's interest and fee lines ("Zinsen für einen genutzten Kredit", fees) are booked by code to a new household category `hh_interest_fees` („Zinsen und Gebühren“, private; migration 0026 adds it to the category CHECK), since they have no payee a rule could learn. Its purchases are categorised by the household taxonomy and the name-pattern rules (D159, D160); a refund is a credit in the same category. (4) **The settlement.** The statement prints the IBAN the household pays the card to ("Angaben bei Einzahlungen"); the import proposes it as the card account's `payment_iban` (masked and HMAC'd like any IBAN, migration 0026; confirmed with the first import), and D162's `own_account_hmacs` includes it, so the monthly debit on the paying bank account is a transfer by IBAN alone (`internal_transfer_out`, confidence 1.0) and is linked to the statement's payment line („Bezahlung“, a credit, `internal_transfer_in`) when the amounts are equal to the cent within the D162 window. *(Amended 12 Nov 2026: this point first had the debit confirmed in review once and then booked by a learned rule on the issuer as payee, because the debit names no own IBAN; the printed payment IBAN makes the rule unnecessary. The learned rule stays the fallback when a debit goes to another of the issuer's IBANs.)* If two card accounts print the same payment IBAN, the IBAN alone books neither and the link decides by amount. `hh_credit_card` stays for a card bill paid when no card account exists. (5) **Net worth.** The card's balance from its last reconciled statement is a liability beside the property loans (amends D167, which subtracted the property loans only). Built in step 2b, after step 2's transfer linking, with migration 0026. | A lump-sum settlement line would leave the card's spending uncategorised on the Haushalt screen and its open balance out of the net worth; the PDF path, the taxonomy, the name rules and the transfer link already exist, so the card adds one kind, one routing key and one linking case. **Amended 13 Nov 2026 (PR #237):** (a) a statement is routed by the issuer's customer number, stored as the account's `external_ref` (masked and HMAC'd), not by the masked card number; only the last four digits are shown; (b) the statement's payment line „Bezahlung“ is booked `internal_transfer_in` by code (`card_pdf.yaml` lists it, spec amendment in that PR); (c) a current-account debit already answered as spending (`hh_credit_card`) before the card existed is re-booked as a transfer only when the user links it to the card's payment line („Als Übertrag buchen und verknüpfen“); a month without an imported statement keeps its `hh_credit_card` booking, the only record of that spending; (d) confirming the payment IBAN switches off any learned rule on that IBAN, with its event and a notice; (e) two cards with one payment IBAN: the debit is a transfer and the amount picks the card, a tie asks. *Amended 13 Nov 2026 (PR #242):* a card's statements rarely start at the account's opening day, so the earliest import whose file states its opening (the card's Alter Saldo; any `declared_opening`) and fails only on the opening offers „Anfangssaldo daraus übernehmen“ with that opening, dated the day before its first booking; an earlier statement uploaded later can move the opening back when its closing equals the current opening. An import later in the chain whose opening does not meet the previous closing is never offered: it names the gap („fehlt vermutlich ein Kontoauszug“) and reconciles once the missing statement arrives. A month without a statement (a card that issues none when nothing was booked) leaves no gap, since the next Alter Saldo equals the last Neuer Saldo, so no waiver exists: a gap that shows is real spending missing. *(3) amended 13 Nov 2026 (PR #258, images df408ec4c68c, no migration; also D165 (b)):* an uploaded statement whose IBAN or number no account holds offers „Konto anlegen und einlesen“: the account the file implies (kind from the format, the identifier read from the stored original and shown masked only, the starting balance and its date shown before it is saved, with a plain note when the file prints none and 0,00 € is a placeholder until „Endsaldo eingeben“ and „Anfangssaldo daraus übernehmen“), created and imported in one transaction; the browser never sends the identifier. *(3) amended again 13 Nov 2026 (PR #260, images 7831ebd7cc55, no migration): a second issuer.* Luis's second card is at Consors Finanz, whose PDF was refused as an unknown format. The format stays `card_pdf`; `services/bank/__init__.parse_card_statement` tells the issuers apart by their text, and the import, the payment-IBAN confirmation and the reading job's stop before the model all use it. A Consors Finanz statement is routed by its Kontonummer (the card account; the Kundennummer names the person, who may hold other products), prints no new balance, so the closing is the opening (ALTER SALDO) plus the printed totals (GESAMTUMSÄTZE), and the lines must equal the debit and the credit total; its payment „EINGEGANGENE ZAHLUNG“ is the card's transfer leg like „Bezahlung“. It prints no reference per line, so lines take the position path (D22). Inferred from one payment-only month and to confirm on the next statements: which printed total is the debits, how a purchase, a foreign-currency purchase, an interest line and a multi-page table look; a wrong reading fails as `statement.report_unbalanced`, never silently. The card's monthly debit may name the issuer's collection IBAN rather than the card's own, in which case it is not linked by IBAN alone (unchecked). A bank export (camt, Sparkasse CSV, DKB CSV) uploaded under Dokumente is stopped before the model like the card (`proposal.bank_file_not_read`). A card gets no guessed bank; its suggested label is „Kreditkarte“. *(3) amended 14 Nov 2026 (Luis: "when importing all the documents for a depot (or another account in general) the app should recognize that they're all for the same account and when you create the account they are all assigned directly to it"; merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026, PR #307, images 41f40240f7e8, no migration): statements follow their account.* A bank export, card statement or depot cash statement waiting under Dokumente (its read stopped as a statement file) is imported onto its account by itself, in the same transaction, whenever an account gets the IBAN or number the file prints: created from one of the files with „Konto anlegen und einlesen“, created by hand with it, or numbered later by its first statement. The files go in statement order (first booking, then last), so D21's reconciliation chains and a missing month is named as a gap, never bridged (as above). The starting balance „Konto anlegen und einlesen“ shows is the earliest file's, and the form says how many more files of the account wait; afterwards the notice says „Weitere N Kontoauszüge dieses Kontos wurden eingelesen.“. *Live, 15 Nov 2026:* an account created before this from a later statement (the second quirion portfolio, from its May statement) took its March and April statements by `follow-accounts`; both start before the starting balance, and „Anfangssaldo daraus übernehmen“ was offered on neither, because the earliest one's closing is not the starting balance (the month between sits in the chain). Since then the earliest import offers its opening when the imports before the starting balance form one unbroken chain to it (each closing the next one's opening, the last closing the starting balance, no statement across its day), so one click reconciles them all; a gap still offers nothing (merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026, PR #308, images 422b2771ede9, no migration). A file whose import refuses keeps its „Auf Konto importieren“ offer, never blocks the others, and is listed with its reason in the create response (`other_refused`); refusals during the other two paths are not shown beyond the document's own offer. *Review, 15 Nov 2026:* a followed file that fails for any other reason (its original unreadable just now, the document gone) is a refusal of that file only, `statement.follow_unreadable` („Diese Datei ließ sich gerade nicht lesen; bitte später auf ihrer Seite „Auf Konto importieren“ klicken.“), logged by its exception class only, so it never rolls back the account or the other files; the file a person chose still fails the request. The import's answer gives the chosen file's status after its account's other files followed. A statement uploaded once its account exists (exactly one account holds its IBAN or number) is imported at once by the reading job, without the model. Accounts that existed before the deploy get their waiting files through `hausbuch follow-accounts --workspace <id> [--dry-run]`, run once (D165 (a) amended 14 Nov 2026). |

Could the app give a short explanation of what happens to the account or what the document means whenever I upload them.") | **Every depot document explains itself in plain words.** The document page and its suggestion card show a short German text for the document's kind, in three parts: (1) **what it is** („Wertpapierabrechnung: die Bestätigung, dass quirion für Ihr Depot Fondsanteile gekauft oder verkauft hat“); (2) **what it means for the account**, with the document's own figures filled in by code from its reading (side, units, fund name, amount, date, the depot account; a figure not read is left out, never guessed); (3) **what the app does with it and whether the person must act** (the button that books it, or „nur zur Information, ändert nichts am Konto“). The texts are rule data, one entry per document kind (`rules/depot_explain` or a YAML catalogue beside the document catalogue), written by people and filled by code, never by the model (principle 1: the model reads, code explains figures). Covered: every quirion kind of §14.1 (Kontoauszug, Rechnungsabschluss, Wertpapierabrechnung Kauf/Verkauf, Erträgnisabrechnung, Ein-/Ausbuchung, PM-Reporting, Rebalancing-Mitteilung, Ertragsübersicht, Kosteninformation, Kostenausweis, Finanzreport, Jahressteuerbescheinigung, Depotauszug, Zinsänderung). A kind with no entry shows nothing rather than a generic text. Other document kinds can get entries later without code changes. *Built 13 Nov 2026 (PR #244, images 74ab1bbf3b42):* the texts in `rules/depot_explain.yaml` for 18 kinds (the list above, the Wertpapierabrechnung split into Kauf, Verkauf and one whose side comes from its reading, and the Quartalsbericht as its own kind beside the PM-Reporting); the kind is recorded as `document.depot_kind` (migration 0027) by the document's reading, or at upload for a statement import, which is never read by the model; all 16 real quirion documents are recognised by their full text layer. | Principle 7 (the user needs no tax or finance knowledge); the documents use terms (Ausmachender Betrag, Einbuchung, Vorabpauschale) the person cannot be expected to know. |
| D178 (13 Nov 2026, Luis chose „Binance-Dateien einlesen“ over the BISON export alone or typing each Binance purchase by hand: the household bought its coins on Binance first and on BISON once Binance stopped serving the EU) | **Binance's exports are read by code into asset movements, as BISON's are (D166): the transaction history is the source of every movement, the withdrawal history completes each withdrawal with its network, fee and transaction hash; no model, no consent gate.** (1) **Files.** Binance's „Transaction History“ (columns User ID, Time, Account, Operation, Coin, Change, Remark; one row per leg, Change signed) and „Withdraw History“ (Time, Coin, Network, Amount, Fee, Address, TXID, Status), each a comma-separated CSV with a UTF-8 BOM inside the ZIP Binance sends; the upload takes the ZIP (one CSV inside) or the CSV, and recognises each file by its header. Times are UTC. The „Spot Trade History“ is not needed: on the real files its fills sum per coin exactly to the transaction history's trade rows (checked 13 Nov 2026 on Luis's computer, never committed), so it adds nothing and its upload is refused with a plain message naming the two files to upload (`exchange.binance_spot_history`). (2) **Never stored:** the User ID and the withdrawal's Address. The address is a public wallet address, but phase 7 registers the wallet's own addresses and links an exchange withdrawal to the wallet's deposit by the transaction hash (D175), so the address is not needed; it is dropped by the parser, never in a movement, an event, a log or the raw dict. (3) **Rows to movements**, in code (`rules/exchange.classify_binance`): the trade rows of one second (`Transaction Buy`/`Transaction Spend`/`Transaction Fee`, and `Transaction Revenue`/`Transaction Sold`/`Transaction Fee` for a sale; the real file has up to five fills in one second and never two coin pairs in one second; a second with two pairs is refused as `exchange.binance_ambiguous_trade`) are summed per coin and role into one movement each: the coin received → `acquisition`, the coin given → `disposal`, each fee coin → `fee` with `fee_quantity` its amount (the fee may be paid in Binance's own coin, a third coin, or in the coin bought). A `Binance Convert` pair of rows is a trade the same way. **A EUR leg is never its own movement**: it is the trade's `eur_amount` on the coin legs, with D166's signs (negative on an acquisition, positive on a disposal). A trade with no EUR leg (a coin bought with a stablecoin; the real file has such pairs) keeps `eur_amount` empty, and phase 7 and 8 value its legs from the price table on the trade's day, since the rule "the trade's own euro leg first" (6 Nov 2026) has no euro leg there; step 5 fetches daily prices for every coin the exchanges held, the stablecoin and the fee coin included. `Deposit` of EUR → `internal_transfer_in` of the EUR asset, linked by D162 to the bank leg; a crypto `Deposit` → D166's crypto Deposit (an open leg with its question). `Withdraw` → `internal_transfer_out`, `quantity` = the amount sent including the fee (−Change; the Remark reads „Withdraw fee is included“), completed from the withdrawal history's row of the same coin whose Amount + Fee equals that amount and whose time lies within one hour before it (the real files differ by up to twenty minutes): `fee_quantity` = Fee, `network` = Network, `tx_hash` = `external_ref` = TXID. Any other Operation → `uncategorized`, proposed. (4) **Without the withdrawal history** the withdrawals keep network and hash empty and the import shows the to-do „Auszahlungsverlauf hochladen“ (principle 7); uploading it later completes each matching row by a new row that supersedes the old one (`superseded_by`, the old row kept). A withdrawal of an own export is proposed for review as D166's crypto Withdrawal, and the import's Zu prüfen card offers one answer for all of its withdrawals („Alle Auszahlungen gingen an eigene Wallets“), since a person answers that for the export, not per line; the one-by-one answer stays. (5) **Dedup.** The file has no transaction id, so `movement_hash` = SHA-256 over `binance`, the second, the role, the coin and the summed amount (a trade leg) or the row's own Operation, Coin and Change (any other row), with the position among identical rows as D166's fallback; a later, longer export re-uploaded skips every stored row. (6) **Reconciliation** is D166's typed balance per coin, the EUR asset included; the EUR position is Σ EUR movements + Σ `eur_amount` of the account's acquisitions and disposals. On the real file every coin but EUR and one stablecoin nets to zero (Q-O79), so the typed balance is a real check. (7) **The account.** Each exchange is one account of kind `exchange` with its owner shares (D157); the account form „Konto anlegen“ offers Kontoart „Börse“ (provider Binance or BISON, no IBAN), which step 4's depot showed was missing for a new kind (PR #246). *(6) amended 13 Nov 2026 (step 5a, PR #256):* the account's `eur_amount` is stored to the cent (numeric(14,2)) while Binance's EUR legs carry up to eight decimals, so each trade's summed EUR leg is rounded half up and the EUR position is exact to the cent per trade, not to Binance's last decimal. A typed EUR balance therefore reconciles when it is within 0.005 € × (the EUR-quoted trade legs through the import + 1) of the sum (the + 1 is Binance's own rounding of the displayed balance); it is shown reconciled with „innerhalb der Rundung (x Cent)“ and its difference kept; **coins stay exact**. The bound grows with the trade count (about 0.15 € for the household's history); an exact check would need the unrounded EUR leg stored, a migration not made. The fixture `binance_csv.yaml` was corrected with this amendment: `typed_balance_does_not_match` moved from one cent to two cents off (one cent is now inside the bound, pinned by `typed_eur_within_rounding`). | Without Binance the coins bought there would reach the wallet with no purchase date or price, which is what the one-year holding period is counted from; the two files Luis already exported carry every leg, and the third adds nothing. |
| D178 amended (14 Nov 2026, Luis: „Yes and the button“, after Binance's own transaction history was found to lack an inflow of one stablecoin) | **A person may book a typed balance's difference as a correction, with a reason.** D166 and D178 (6) never bridge a difference, and that stays true for the app: nothing is booked by code. When the export itself misses a movement (Q-O79, answered), the account page's „weicht ab“ row and the Zu prüfen card offer „Differenz als Korrektur buchen“: a dialog shows the coin and the difference and asks for a reason, and the command books one confirmed movement of the new kind `balance_correction` with the stored difference as its quantity (a positive difference is an inflow), dated at the import's last movement, source `user`, the reason as its `note` (1–500 characters), no `eur_amount` and no document (a CHECK, migration 0030), then re-checks the import, which then reconciles. Refused: an import whose balances agree, an import that is not the account's newest (its check would change unseen), **EUR** (D178 (6)'s rounding bound stays the only EUR tolerance), a coin not typed or already agreeing, and a coin whose movements changed since the typing (the difference shown is no longer the one booked). Events `movement.confirmed` and `account.balance_corrected` (ids only, never the reason); the reason is never sent to a model. A correction is never a trade, a transfer, an acquisition or a withdrawal to match, and counts as units held in the valuation. How phase 8's FIFO counts an inflow correction is open (Q-O96; settled by phase 8 D212, 16 Nov 2026). Merged and deployed 14 Nov 2026 (PR #268, images 4db7c2024bfb, migration 0030 applied). | A real export can miss a movement, and the only alternative left a coin „weicht ab“ forever; a person who has checked the exchange's own balance can record the gap once, visibly and with a reason, and append-only (a wrong correction is answered by a counter-correction after a new typing). |
| D178 amended (2) (15 Nov 2026, Luis: „It says that Binance has 4k in euros which is not the case“) | The Anlagen overview (`services/prices/overview.assets_overview`) values an exchange's EUR position as its EUR movements (deposits, withdrawals, corrections) plus the euro leg (`eur_amount`) of every confirmed, current trade on that exchange before the overview's date: an acquisition's leg is negative, a disposal's positive, exactly as `rules/exchange.positions` reconciles the exchange page. Until then the overview summed the EUR movements only, so every euro spent on coins stayed counted as cash (Binance showed about 4,000 € instead of 1.40 €, BISON euros it does not hold either (it now shows none), the household total about 6,000 € too high); the coins themselves were right. A proposed trade does not count. No migration. Merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #343, images 473658f2b15f, no migration). | The exchange page and the overview must agree; the overview had a second, incomplete reading of the same movements. |
| D178 amended (3) (16 Nov 2026, from phase 7's lot rules: a coin-for-coin Binance trade's lot was unpriced and no trade fee reached a lot's cost, which D188 (1) of phase 7 requires) | **An exchange trade is one movement group.** (1) The importer writes each trade's legs into one `movement_group` of kind `swap`, confirmed, on the exchange account, with no chain and no transaction hash: on Binance the acquisition, the disposal and the fee movements of one second, on BISON its one Buy or Sell row (its EUR leg is on the row; a BISON fee is the row's `fee_quantity` and reaches no lot cost, and every fee on the real export is zero; this said „its EUR leg and fee are on the row“ until the review). Deposits, withdrawals and other rows are in no group. A `movement_group.trade_recorded` event (its own type: phase 7 step 2 gives `movement_group.confirmed` a chain transaction's routing) names the group's movements and the import's document. (2) A fee a Binance trade paid in EUR is also the acquisition's `fee_eur` (none on a sale, whose EUR fee lowers its proceeds and is phase 8's to read from the group); the EUR fee movement stays, since the EUR position counts it (D178 (6)), and `rules/exchange.positions` never reads `fee_eur`, so nothing is counted twice. A fee in a coin stays a fee movement of the group. (3) Migration 0043: `movement_group.chain` and `tx_hash` nullable, with a CHECK that they are null together and only for a `swap`; the downgrade is refused while such a group exists. (4) `hausbuch group-exchange-trades --workspace <id> [--dry-run]` ties the trades imported before: each leg is superseded by a new row carrying the group (0025's trigger refuses an UPDATE of `group_id`; the withdrawal history's supersede path, `movement.superseded` events), quantity, EUR leg, status and document unchanged, an acquisition whose trade paid a EUR fee also given its `fee_eur`; it reads only current, confirmed, ungrouped export rows that still carry their `movement_hash`, so a second run finds nothing, and a later re-import still recognises the stored rows. An exchange account whose provider was changed to text other than BISON or Binance (PATCH /accounts takes any) is skipped and named by its label, the others still grouped (review fix: it had ended the run with a traceback). Typed balances, reconciliation, the net worth, the Anlagen overview and the Zu prüfen cards are unchanged, and a withdrawal history uploaded after the run still completes its withdrawal (tested on a Binance and a BISON account). (5) What it changes for the lots (phase 7 D188 (1), (4)): a coin-for-coin trade's lot costs the given coin's value on the day plus the fee's, and a EUR buy's its EUR leg plus its EUR fee; a trade whose fee coin (BNB, say) has no price for the day now leaves its lot unpriced („Kurs fehlt“) even when its EUR leg is known, D188's never 0 €. The common case is a Binance buy for EUR whose fee is in the coin just bought: with no price row for that coin on that exact day its lot becomes unpriced, a to-do under phase 7 D188, never 0 €, where the ungrouped data had priced it by its EUR leg. Merged and deployed 16 Nov 2026 (PR #380, images 646aa95d5c4c, migration 0043 applied; this sentence said „not merged or deployed“ until then); `hausbuch group-exchange-trades --dry-run` on the live data the same day: 26 exchange trades of 51 movements to group, no acquisition given a EUR fee; the real run the same day after 08:10 UTC grouped those 26 trades (51 movements) and gave no acquisition a EUR fee. | Phase 7's lots read a trade's legs together only through its group; without it a trade fee never joins the cost (BMF 6 Mar 2025 Rn. 58 in design §4) and a coin received for a coin has no cost on a day without its own price. |
| D178 amended (4) (16 Nov 2026, found on the live data after Luis: „I'm still confused with the price transaction part“: the Krypto tab showed „10 Abgänge übersteigen den gebuchten Bestand“, every chain receipt from Binance stood about an hour before its withdrawal, and those withdrawals had no purchase before them) | **A Binance export is read in the time zone its file names name; the imports read before as UTC are moved, append-only, by `hausbuch repair-exchange-times`.** (1) **The zone.** Binance writes every time in the zone the person chose when exporting (the household's: UTC+1) and names it only in its names: the downloaded ZIP `Binance-<kind>-History-<stamp>UTC1_<id>.zip` and the CSV inside it `<kind>-History-<stamp>(UTC+1)-part1-of1.csv`. The reader (`binance_csv.utc_offset`) takes the offset from the member's name, else from the upload's name (whole hours or hours and minutes, at most ±14 h; „UTC“ inside a word is no zone; „UTC“ alone only as „(UTC)“ or right after the stamp before the download's id; a zone it can read only in part, such as „(UTC+5.5)“, „UTC5-30_“, „UTC5_30_“, „(UTC +1)“ or „_UTC_1_“, names none, review fix E, 16 Nov 2026: those had been read as UTC+5 or as UTC), and stores every time of the transaction history and of the withdrawal history as UTC. The movement hash keeps the time as the file writes it, so the same export uploaded again, or a longer one, is still recognised. Until then `.replace(tzinfo=UTC)` read every row an hour late, so a withdrawal stood after the chain's own receipt and the wallet's lot (phase 7 D188) had no purchase before it. (2) **No zone named** (a renamed file, a plain CSV): read as UTC as before; the import's event records `utc_offset_minutes = 0` and `time_zone_assumed = true`, and the exchange page shows the to-do `exchange.time_zone_unknown` with „Transaktionsverlauf erneut hochladen“ (principle 7). It ends when the same file is uploaded again under Binance's own name (its rows move; the upload returns the stored import), or when a later export naming its zone shares rows with it (those rows move by the later export's zone, the earlier file having been written in the same zone). An export whose every row was stored already writes no row and owns nothing to move, so it never shows the to-do and the command never counts it; its first and last times are those of the rows it matched, on their clock, and follow them when the repair moves them, so it never stands later than the import that wrote them and „Differenz als Korrektur buchen“ on that import is still accepted (review fix A, 16 Nov 2026: such an import had kept the to-do forever and refused the correction). (3) **The repair** (`services/exchange/times.repair_times`, `hausbuch repair-exchange-times --workspace <ws> [--dry-run]`; the same runs at the start of every Binance upload): for each Binance import whose stored file name names a zone and whose zone is not yet known, every live row whose time differs is superseded by a copy at its UTC time (status, transaction hash, network and fee kept; `movement.superseded` and the copy's `movement.confirmed` or `.proposed`, the existing events without amounts); a trade's group (D178 amended (3)) by a new group holding all its legs with its trade event; a balance correction (dated at the import's last row) with them; a deposit the app wrote at a withdrawal's time (phase 7 D181 (c) amended: a person's pick for a hash the node never knew) with its withdrawal, as a new group still routed as written; a deposit read from the chain at its block time never, since that time is UTC already. The import's first and last times move by the offset. Each current transfer link that holds a moved row is superseded by one holding the copy, with its kind, its `matched_by` and its `transfer.linked` reason and confirmations kept, then the D162 relink and phase 7's chain relink run, so a hash link and a D261 learned pair link the moved rows again; an upload that moved rows at its start runs them too, also when the file is one stored already or a withdrawal history (review fix B, 16 Nov 2026: the same bytes uploaded again had returned before linking what they had moved). One `account.movement_times_corrected` event per import names its offset in minutes, the counts and the ids, never a file name, an address or a hash. A second run moves nothing and writes nothing. A copy the repair writes is no row read later: the withdrawal history's reason (`exchange.withdrawal_history_no_match`, a history checked and fitting none) compares the time the original row was first read, through copies of the same kind only, so the repair never turns it back into „Auszahlungsverlauf fehlt“, while a row a person answered as a withdrawal after the check still asks for the history (review fix D, 16 Nov 2026). The repair and the upload lock the account's Binance import rows before the account's row, the order „Differenz als Korrektur buchen“ and „Alle Auszahlungen gingen an eigene Wallets“ take them in, and correct only the imports they locked (review fix C, 16 Nov 2026: the repair had taken the account first and could deadlock with either; a two-connection test of each now commits both). A known window stays, at run time only: a person's answer to a withdrawal holds its row and then waits for the transfer lock, which the repair takes before the rows, so the two at the same moment can still end one of them with a deadlock error, retried by the person; the transfer lock's order is not changed here. (4) **Not covered.** D261's learned withdrawal pair asks the chain's receipt at or after the withdrawal's stamp; on the live data Binance's stamp, moved to UTC, sits about 40 s after the chain's receipt, so a learned pair still does not link those (a hash link does; widening D261's window is its own decision, left open). A re-export in another zone keeps another hash (the hash keeps the written time), so an export whose zone is known skips, as stored already, each row that a stored row of an import of known zone matches by the same instant in UTC, kind, coin and quantity, matched by position so two equal rows of one second stay two; the skipped rows count among `rows_skipped` and an export whose every row is so matched takes those rows' bounds (review fix F, 16 Nov 2026: a UTC+2 export of rows stored from a UTC+1 one had stored them twice). Still not covered: an overlapping export or stored import whose zone is unknown is never matched this way (its times are not on the UTC clock), and the withdrawal history needs no such guard: it stores no row of its own, its times are read to UTC before it finds a withdrawal by time, and a history row whose transaction hash is stored already completes nothing again. A withdrawal history naming its zone, checked against an import whose zone is still unknown, compares two clocks until that import is moved, and since review fix D the move no longer asks for the history again, so its „passt zu keiner Auszahlung“ stays until the history is uploaded once more. No migration: the zone lives in the import's events. Merged and deployed 16 Nov 2026 (PR #414, images fb283ae32cd4, no migration; CI's first run failed on two tests unrelated to this change, the Anlage N browser test and the Bescheid scrub test whose IBAN fragment „0532“ can occur in a random id, and passed when re-run); `hausbuch repair-exchange-times` on the live data the same evening (22:28 UTC) moved 64 movements of the one Binance import from UTC+1 to UTC and needed no relink, a second dry run moved nothing, and the account's zone reads as known (UTC+1). On the Krypto tab the outflows no lot covered fell from 10 to 1 and the wallet's lots without a purchase from 12 to 1; the one left is 0.135 USDC sent to the wallet on 27 Mar that the export books only by the balance correction of 2 Jul, a gap in the export, not a time. |
| D159 amended (13 Nov 2026, Luis: „The list looks better… we can add more later“, then the extras confirmed and „we should add a refund category“; „Sonstiges“ held about 96 private lines and the model asked odd questions for want of a fitting code) | **Fifteen finer household codes, a refund code, a one-off re-ask and a bulk confirm per category.** (1) **Codes** (migration `0029_household_categories` rebuilds the `allocation` and both `matching_rule` category CHECKs as a superset; its downgrade is refused while a row or a rule uses a new code): `hh_personal_care` „Friseur & Körperpflege“, `hh_clothing` „Kleidung & Schuhe“, `hh_dining` „Restaurant & Lieferdienst“, `hh_home` „Haushalt & Einrichtung“, `hh_electronics` „Elektronik & Technik“, `hh_online_shopping` „Online-Einkäufe“ (a marketplace or payment service whose content is unknown), `hh_fees` „Behörden & Gebühren“, `hh_gifts` „Geschenke & Feiern“, `hh_family_transfers` „Geld an Familie / ins Ausland“ (kept apart: Unterhalt may matter for the return later), `hh_telecom` „Telefon & Internet“, `hh_work_education` „Arbeitsmittel & Fortbildung“ (possible Werbungskosten later), `hh_sport` „Sport & Fitness“, `hh_travel` „Reisen“ (split from `hh_leisure_travel`, relabelled „Freizeit & Hobbys“), `hh_investment` „Geldanlage“ (money moved to a platform the app does not hold as an account) and `hh_refund` „Erstattung“. All are private-only and map to no Anlage V or Anlage N line in either rule set; the rental account refuses them like every `hh_*` code. (2) **Haushalt totals**: `hh_refund` is negative spending in its group, never income, so a refund lowers what was spent; `hh_investment` is neither income nor spending and counts under „Gespart“ (a payout lowers it); it is not a transfer code, so D162's link never touches it. (3) **Thresholds** (what the model may book alone once `auto_accept_llm` is on, still never on a private account's first import): 0.9 like their groups for the eleven ordinary spending codes; none (a person confirms every line) for `hh_family_transfers`, `hh_work_education`, `hh_investment` and `hh_refund`. *Amended 15 Nov 2026 (Luis: „Künftig immer so zuordnen“ was greyed on the monthly money to family):* `hh_family_transfers` may still learn a rule (`LEARNABLE_WITHOUT_THRESHOLD`): the model never books it alone, a rule only repeats the person's own answer for that payee, and the line keeps its code for a later Unterhalt check. Merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #309, images c8ae6047ec8c, no migration). *Amended 15 Nov 2026 (Luis: „Can't click immer so für the credit card payments“):* `hh_credit_card` (a card whose statements are not in the app) joins `LEARNABLE_WITHOUT_THRESHOLD`: its settlement is paid to one issuer IBAN at a different amount each month, so a rule learned from one answer books the next ones; the model still never books it alone. Once the card is an account, confirming its payment IBAN switches off every learned rule on that IBAN (D176 (4)), no rule books a line naming that IBAN at all (D160 amended (5), 15 Nov 2026: the transfer by an own IBAN comes first), and a line a rule booked `hh_credit_card` is re-booked as the transfer when linked (D176, `rebook_on_link`). No migration; merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #331, images 86a02c3ba44d). (4) **„Alle N bestätigen“** on Zu prüfen (`POST /api/inbox/confirm-category`): per category, it confirms exactly the line ids the person was shown, each re-read under its lock and confirmed only while it is still an unsplit one-part proposal of that category with no question, on a private account; any other line is skipped and counted, a rental line or a category outside `BULK_CONFIRMABLE` refuses the whole request. Each line gets the same command and event as a single confirmation; no rule is learned. **Amended 14 Nov 2026 (Luis: „It seems the confirm all button missed some categories“; about 250 of 270 waiting private lines carried a model question, so whole categories had no button):** a line whose single proposed row carries a model question is listed too, ticked like the others, with its question shown under the payee, so the person unticks it when the answer changes the category; „with no question“ above no longer holds, and the skip reason `question` is gone from the API. Split lines, the categories outside `BULK_CONFIRMABLE`, „only the ticked ids“ and „no rule is learned“ are unchanged. Merged and deployed 14 Nov 2026 (PR #302, images 460a99ef1458, no migration). `BULK_CONFIRMABLE` = the household income and spending codes outside the tax, movement and depot groups, minus `hh_capital_income`, `hh_tax_refund`, `hh_family_transfers` and `hh_work_education` (`hh_refund` is in: it is one more spending line). (5) **The one-off re-ask** (`hausbuch household-reask --workspace <id> [--dry-run]`, run by the operator after the deploy): every private line whose current rows are all proposed and all in `hh_other`, `uncategorized`, `hh_leisure_travel`, `hh_subscriptions` or `hh_housing` gets a matching job that runs the usual order; a learned rule or matcher may still book it as in D45/D49, but **the model's new answer is only ever a proposal**, never auto-confirmed. A line with a confirmed row is never taken, before or during the job. Nothing is queued while `ai_private_accounts` is off. A line is asked once per prompt version; a retried job asks again so a failed write never loses the answer. **Left alone**: a line whose text names a purchase or financing cost of the flat (Notar, Grunderwerbsteuer, Grundbuch, Justizkasse, Makler, Darlehen and the like) or whose amount equals a Nebenkosten row to the cent, since re-sorting it is D161's decision for a person (known gap, §14.1: `record_proposed` dedupes a privately paid cost only against the same document's rows, not against a Nebenkosten row with the same Beleg). | The coarse codes hid most of the household's spending and made the model ask questions a finer code answers; a refund booked as income would inflate the household's income; a person who has checked fifty grocery lines should confirm them with one click, but only the lines on the screen, so a line re-proposed meanwhile is never confirmed unseen (principle 3). |
