| D159 amended (8) (15 Nov 2026, Luis: „Yes add the wage replacement category“, agreeing to a category for wage-replacement benefits and to the app proposing the year's Lohnersatzleistung from it) | **A category for Elterngeld and other wage-replacement benefits, and the booked sum as a to-do on the return.** (1) A new code `hh_wage_replacement` „Elterngeld & Lohnersatz“ in the income group (`income`) after `hh_child_benefit`: income, treatment private, threshold None (a model answer is never auto-confirmed), in D52's `ZERO_EXCLUDED`, not bulk-confirmable (it matters for the return, like capital income and a tax refund), proposed input `return_input:replacement_income`, `form: null` in the 2025 and 2026 rule sets (no Anlage V line). The categorize prompt names Elterngeld (from the Elterngeldstelle), Mutterschaftsgeld from the Krankenkasse (often „Leistung von … bis …“), Krankengeld and Arbeitslosengeld, and excludes a Krankenkasse's „Kostenerstattung“, a private insurer's „Leistungsabrechnung“, and the employer's Zuschuss zum Mutterschaftsgeld and Kurzarbeitergeld (both arrive with the pay and stay `hh_salary`). (2) A pure rule `rules/tax/anlage_n/booked_benefits.py` sums per tax year (by booking date) the confirmed `hh_wage_replacement` allocations on private accounts (`services/return_inputs.booked_benefits`, duplicates left out, a payment back lowering the sum) and compares them with the year's current `replacement_income` rows. The spouses are assessed jointly, so the gate is the household's: the whole-return estimate gains the warning `return.benefit_booked` only while the booked lines of all accounts and owners exceed the typed rows of all owners together, and the to-dos' differences sum to exactly that gap (severity warning; params year, booked, typed and difference, plus `owner_id` and `benefit` when known; refs the allocations and the typed rows that cover them; never a payee or purpose text). The lines are grouped by owner, the account's only current owner; each group's sum is clamped at zero, so a payment back larger than the benefits never counts as typed. The gap goes to the groups whose lines are not covered by their owner's own rows, owners first, then the lines on a joint account (or one without recorded owners), each capped by its shortfall. A group's owner is only the pre-fill's suggestion (the person may save it on the other spouse); the joint-account to-do names none and asks the person to pick who received it. (3) The benefit kind is read by code from the payee and purpose texts (Elterngeld, Mutterschaftsgeld, Krankengeld, Arbeitslosengeld), only when every line names exactly one and the same kind; a Krankenkasse's bare „Leistung von … bis …“ gives none, so Mutterschaftsgeld and Krankengeld are never guessed, and „Arbeitslosengeld II“ (also „-II“, „2“) reads as none. Kurzarbeitergeld is counted on neither side: a booked line whose text names it („Kurzarbeit“, „KUG“) and a typed `kurzarbeitergeld` row are both left out, because it is the wage certificate's Nr. 15, which phase 4b D102 already counts in the Progressionsvorbehalt. (4) The to-do „Lohnersatzleistungen <Jahr> eintragen“ shows on the Gesamt card (Steuern and the Anlage N tab), on Übersicht's „Was noch fehlt“ and in the archive's estimate pages; its button „Lohnersatzleistung eintragen“ opens the Anlage N tab's „Lohnersatzleistungen“ form of the named owner pre-filled with the difference and the kind (empty with „Bitte wählen“ when unknown); nothing is saved until „Speichern“, through the existing `create_return_input` command, no new write path. (5) The form's help says the paying authority's Leistungsnachweis or Bescheid wins where it differs from the booked payments, and the to-do's text and the form's help say that the pre-filled amount is what reached the account, and that for Krankengeld the Krankenkasse's Bescheinigung shows a higher amount (before the social-insurance shares), which is the one to enter. The form opens pre-filled whenever the button names the owner already on screen, and a pre-fill that no current `return.benefit_booked` of the loaded estimate backs (a stale Back-button entry) is dropped, so the same amount cannot be saved twice. No change to the questionnaire, the tariff or the estimate's figures; benefits on a wage certificate (Nr. 15) do not count as typed. **Corrected 15 Nov 2026 in the PR's review, before merge:** the first version listed Kurzarbeitergeld as a booked benefit and compared each owner with their own rows. A household with Kurzarbeitergeld on the certificate's Nr. 15 and the same money booked was asked to type it again, which counted it twice against D102 and then raised `return.benefit_possible_double`, a loop; and Elterngeld paid into one spouse's account but typed under the other kept its to-do, so following it counted the benefit twice. | A benefit on the accounts that nobody types is the Progressionsvorbehalt missed: it raises the rate on the rest of the income, and the household would have to know that (principle 7). The figure comes from code over confirmed lines, the person confirms it (principle 3), and the authority's statement stays the source of truth. Migration 0037 (shared with D159 amended (7)); fixture rows for the code; built (commits dc18736, e23a98b, a399e52); phase 4b's decisions note it.; merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #334, squashed, images 486fdb9d01ca, migration 0037 applied). *Checked 15 Nov 2026 (the monthly Elterngeld was thought to need a hand answer every month):* `hh_wage_replacement` has learned „Künftig immer so zuordnen“ since D160 amended (5) (PR #336, the same day: every household code outside `NEVER_LEARNED` may learn a rule), its box shown but starting unticked like every code without a threshold; its threshold stays None, so the model never books it alone, and a rule only repeats the person's own answer for that payee. The to-do „Lohnersatzleistungen <Jahr> eintragen“ reads every confirmed `hh_wage_replacement` line whoever booked it (person or rule), so nothing changes there. No code change; a test now pins it (`test_a_code_without_a_threshold_learns_a_rule_that_books_the_next_month`, Elterngeld case). |
| D160 amended (14 Nov 2026, Luis: the „Künftig immer so zuordnen“ question on a delivery-service line named a category, not the payee, beside other payees' amounts) | **A counterparty IBAN used by two or more payees is shared, and a rule on it names the payee.** DKB's CSV export gives every card payment one clearing IBAN (on the household's account about three hundred lines from some eighty payees), so an IBAN-only rule learned from one card payment would have booked every card payment of the account at confidence 1; none had been learned (checked 14 Nov 2026). (1) An IBAN is **shared** when the workspace's private-account lines it carries hold at least `SHARED_IBAN_MIN_NAMES = 2` distinct payees (compared as D160's name rules compare names; a line without a name is no payee), computed on read (`rules/matching.shared_iban`, `services/matching.is_shared_iban`), no migration. (2) On a shared IBAN „Künftig immer so zuordnen“ learns the IBAN **and** the payee's anchored name pattern (D160's `_name_rule`, with its too_broad and conflicts checks). (3) A rule on the IBAN alone never applies to a private line whose IBAN is shared, including one learned before the IBAN became shared; such a line takes the usual path (the model on a first match, Zu prüfen on a re-match) and the rule stays enabled (known gap, §14.1). (4) D49's waiting amounts, the rule's amount test and the similar answers shown to the model count only the same payee's lines on a shared IBAN; the question names the payee: „Von <Name> warten auch Zahlungen über … Gehören die auch zu <Kategorie>?“. (5) The rental account's IBAN logic (tenants, loans, owner transfers, D162) is unchanged. Merged and deployed 14 Nov 2026 (PR #265, images a66bbbdfb6b0, no migration). Same PR: „Alle N bestätigen“ shows the category's lines („Zahlungen ansehen“) with a tick box each, ticked by default, and „Ausgewählte bestätigen (N)“ sends only the ticked ones (D159 amended (4)); every decimal reaches the screen in plain notation (a typed 0 had come back as `0E-10`). | A bank's clearing IBAN identifies the bank, not the shop; a rule must never book a payee the person did not answer for (principle 3), and the question must name what it asks about. Two payees rather than three, from the review: with three, an IBAN of exactly two payees still learned an IBAN-only rule and the question showed one payee's name beside the other's amounts; a payee seen under a second name then only costs one more question. |
| D160 amended (2) (14 Nov 2026, Luis: „Künftig immer so zuordnen“ on a delivery-service line was refused with „Eine Regel für diesen Namen würde Umsätze treffen, die anders bestätigt wurden“; and „I don't see the Kaufland bank lines in the prüfen“) | **A learned rule can take the payee's earlier answers with it.** (1) When a name rule is refused because the payee's earlier lines on private accounts were confirmed in another category (`rule.conflicts`), the panel shows how they are booked („N frühere Zahlungen von X sind als Y bestätigt“) and offers „Diese auch auf <Kategorie> umbuchen“; ticked, the server re-books every such line to the answer's category through the ordinary correction (method user, the old rows superseded, `transaction.reallocated` per line) and learns the rule in the same transaction, all or nothing. Only a line with one confirmed row of a learnable household code, no scope and no Beleg, reconciled, not excluded, not awaiting a duplicate and not a leg of a transfer link or a depot movement is re-booked; lines still proposed or already in the category are skipped; a rule whose bookings were re-booked is disabled only when its matcher equals the new rule's. (2) A line that blocks it is named before the box is offered (`rebook_blockers` on the GET: date, amount, reason, „Zahlung öffnen“), and the PUT refuses with `rule.rebook_blocked` as the backstop. (3) The name rule's checks count only lines of the rule's amount sign, so a refund never blocks a spending rule. (4) „Empfänger suchen“ on Zu prüfen narrows the payment lines and the „Alle N bestätigen“ groups by payee or purpose, and a bulk row's payee opens that line. Never a rental line, never a tax-relevant code | No migration; `rules/matching.name_hits`, `services/review._rebook_earlier`; merged and deployed 14 Nov 2026 (PR #275, images 9dcfccdb81f6) |
| D160 amended (3) (14 Nov 2026, Luis: „Why is there no immer so Button for the bank fees bank line?“, then „Yes“ to a suggested pattern) | **A line without a counterparty IBAN gets a suggested purpose-pattern rule.** The bank's own postings (the monthly „Entgeltabrechnung siehe Anlage …“) carry no IBAN, so no IBAN rule could be learned and the box was hidden; D38's typed pattern needed the user to know regular expressions. Now `GET` on a line's allocations returns `suggested_purpose_pattern` (`^` + the purpose's first run of letters, at least 6, regex-escaped, at most 200 characters) and `suggested_pattern_waiting`, and the panel shows „Künftig immer so zuordnen“ ticked with the word in „Erkannt am Anfang des Verwendungszwecks“ and the number of waiting payments it will also book. Offered only when: `can_learn_rule` is false, the line has no IBAN and exactly one current row; the candidate passes the answer command's own `proposed_rule_or_refusal`; every same-sign line in the workspace the rule would hit has no IBAN and no confirmed row of another category or a split; and the row's category is neither one a matcher owns (D40: rent, NK prepayment, loan interest and principal, Hausgeld, owner transfers) nor one without an auto-confirm threshold. An edited word is sent as literal text (escaped, anchored). The waiting count covers lines on accounts of the line's own purpose, which are the only ones the rule books. The hand-typed pattern field is unchanged. | Principle 7: the user should not need to know regular expressions to teach the app a bank posting. Review (one round): a rental line in a D40 category would have got a one-click rule that books any amount (no IBAN means no D49 amount check), so a later cash rent could silently become an owner transfer; fixed by the category guard. Known limits: a purpose rule ignores the payee's name (no reader today produces such a line with a wrong match), and two rare Unicode case folds are not prefiltered by the database. |
| D160 amended (4) (14 Nov 2026, Luis: „There are some repeated disney plus from PayPal …“; „Please check for all these repetitions“) | **One payee, however a statement spells it, so one „Künftig immer so zuordnen“ books all its repeated payments on private accounts.** (1) *A longer or shorter spelling is the same payee.* `rules.matching.same_payee` is symmetric and decided in code from the name's words (letters only, umlauts written out, hyphens and punctuation dropped, a card processor's prefix SPC, UZR, SQ, SUMUP, ZETTLE dropped, a reference code before the name dropped (one token of 6 or more letters and digits with at least two digits, „G0A3LMSE ANTHROPIC …“, „375be52e SQ *ATARIGUA …“, but not a name with a date glued to it, „GLOVO24MAY“), and a web address's „www.“ and „.com“, „.de“, „.net“ naming no payee, so „WWW.DAZN.COM …“ is DAZN): the same payee when one name's words start the other's, when the first two words are shared, when the first three are shared with a linking second word („Hotel at Booking.com …“, added by the orchestrator), or when the first word is shared, has at least 5 letters and is not a generic word (Restaurant, Hotel, Stadtwerke, Sparkasse and others, a data list). That identity is *strong* (the same words, a prefix whose shorter name has two words or more, a one-word prefix of 8 letters or more, „KAUFLAND“ and „KAUFLAND DUESSELDORF …“ (`BRAND_WORD_MIN`, added by the orchestrator after the replay of Luis's lines), the first two words, Booking's three) or *weak* (a shorter one-word prefix, „ALDI“ and „ALDI TALK“, „MUELLER“ and „MUELLER BROT“, or only the first word, „ADLER MODEMARKT“ and „ADLER APOTHEKE“, „VOLKSWAGEN AUTOVERSICHERUNG AG“ and „VOLKSWAGEN AUTOVERS AG“); both are one payee for `same_payee`, a shared IBAN and D49's history, but a rule that hits a line only weakly never books it: it proposes it at guarded confidence with „Gleicher Empfänger wie „<Name>“? Bitte bestätigen.“, a strong hit by another rule wins, and a re-match never puts such a proposal over another answer waiting on the line (a model's proposal stays) nor over a confirmed one (review F2, principle 3). A learned name rule hits by that identity only when its pattern is the learned shape (`merchant_pattern` of a name); a pattern a person typed keeps its regular-expression meaning (a hit of the pattern itself is strong), and every rule learned before keeps working. (2) *A payment service's payee is its merchant.* PayPal, Klarna and Google Pay (a data list, `PAYMENT_SERVICES`) pay many merchants, so the merchant is read from the line: PayPal's direct debit „Ihr Einkauf bei <Händler>“ in the purpose, its card form „PAYPAL *<HÄNDLER>“, the debit card form „<Händler> - PayPal“, Klarna's „Purchase at <Händler> Gläubiger-ID: …“, Google's card form „GOOGLE *<Händler> g.co/helppay#“ (the help page's address dropped, review F1: a Netflix rule had booked Supercell through the shared first word), while „Google Payment Ie Ltd“ names no merchant. The rule learned there is the service's name pattern plus the merchant's purpose pattern, without the IBAN, so two merchants on one PayPal IBAN learn two rules. A service line whose merchant cannot be read learns no rule (`rule.no_merchant`): the panel greys the box with the reason. A rule learned earlier that names only the service's legal name, two words or more („^PAYPAL EUROPE S A R L ET CIE S C A“, with or without its IBAN), is ignored on a service's lines, decided on read, never deleted; a pattern a person typed on the service's word alone („^PAYPAL“) keeps working (review F4). The panel names the payee („PayPal: DisneyPlus“, `payee_label`). (3) *A per-purchase code is cut* from a card line's name („AMZN Mktp DE*ZD2HG0G54 …“), so the next order is the same payee. (4) *A name rule on a shared IBAN is the name and the sign, without the IBAN*, so it also books the payee's lines on another private account (Lieferando through a bank's card clearing IBAN on one account and as „Lieferando.de lieferse …“ on a card); a rule learned earlier with the IBAN stays as it is, except that correcting a line it booked with „Künftig immer so zuordnen“ disables it, since the new name rule is its matcher without the IBAN (review F3). D160's checks are unchanged (`rule.too_broad`, `rule.conflicts`, the re-book of earlier lines), read through the same identity; a shared IBAN counts distinct payees, not distinct spellings. `hausbuch rematch-waiting --workspace <id> [--dry-run]` queues one re-check per private account (trigger kind `private_lines`), so the lines that waited before the deploy are checked once against the rules and matchers, never the model. The SAME/DIFFERENT pairs are the fixture `tests/fixtures/phase6/payee_identity.yaml`. | Luis found the same Disney+ subscription asked about every month because PayPal's name came in two spellings and one PayPal IBAN carried every merchant, and Lieferando asked again on the card after it was learned on the bank account. Known limits: a name cut short at the end of the field on the same IBAN is not matched; a history line without its purpose counts as an unknown merchant for `rule.too_broad`; the panel's „N weitere“ count of waiting lines reads only the line's IBAN, so it misses the payee's lines on another IBAN or account that the rule will book or propose; a Google subscription line without a merchant after the star learns no rule. Built 14 Nov 2026 on `feat/payee-recognition` (commits e8cf3d9, 2c0775d, 33a5608, 368c49a, 857ae28, e5948a7, the reference-code and web-address fix b07cf4d after Luis's real lines were replayed, the Booking criterion 8e0af9d, and the review fixes 51c47ee (F1 Google, F2 strong and weak, F4), 2063024 (F3) and f5a6ffb (the no-merchant text), the long-brand criterion 95524b8, no migration); merged and deployed 14 Nov 2026 (PR #299, images 50b1765ca1a3, no migration). The first `rematch-waiting` run the same day booked 24 waiting private lines by rules learned earlier, 20 of them one level coarser than the subcategory proposed for them (an IBAN+name rule on `hh_dining` over `hh_delivery`, an IBAN rule on `hh_subscriptions` over `hh_music_audio`): a re-check now keeps a waiting proposal of a subcategory over a rule on its group's fallback (`matching.keeps_finer`), and learning the finer answer without the IBAN disables the IBAN+name fallback rule with it (merged and deployed 14 Nov 2026, PR #300, images 8cba933e8b7f, no migration; the 20 coarser lines stay confirmed until Luis re-books one line per payee with „Diese auch … umbuchen“). Two gaps found on Luis's lines after PR #299, closed on `fix/rule-covers-payee-forms` (no migration): a payment service's merchant rule learned on the direct debit (`^PAYPAL\b` with „Ihr Einkauf bei DisneyPlus“) now also hits the same merchant's card line „<Nummer> PAYPAL *DISNEYPLUS …“, whose purpose has no „Ihr Einkauf bei“ and whose name starts with digits, and a rule learned on the card form books the direct debit: a service rule in its learned shape (`_learned_service_payee`, the exact round trip of what a direct debit learns) hits a line of the same service whose merchant is the rule's, strong books and weak proposes with „Gleicher Empfänger wie „PayPal: <Händler>“?“, a purpose pattern a person typed keeping its regular-expression meaning, Klarna likewise (Google's rules are name rules and already did). And an IBAN rule learned on a private line also reaches that line's payee on another IBAN (one pay-TV company collecting from one IBAN until March and another from April, the earlier lines left waiting by the rule on the later IBAN): the rules loader reads the payee from the rule's `created_from_allocation_id` line (`MatchingRule.learned_from`, a private attribute, never stored or sent, so rules learned before are covered), a strong identity books only at the same amount as the rule's latest confirmed line on its own IBAN (the newest on or before the line's date, else the newest; without one, the amount of the line the rule was learned from), else it proposes (Luis, 14 Nov 2026, after review F1: one insurer's Kfz-Versicherung rule had booked its Hausrat on another IBAN, `rules.matching.cross_iban_reference`), and a weak one, or any such hit of a „nur bei gleichem Betrag“ rule, proposes with the question naming the payee, a rule on the line's own IBAN wins, and not on a shared IBAN (the rule's or the line's), the rental account, an own account's, tenant's or lender's IBAN, a payment service's line or for a split; the D49 check reads the line's own IBAN history, as before (`rules.matching.cross_iban_hit`, `waiting_lines` picks those lines for a new rule). A rule without its origin line (typed, or the line erased) keeps the IBAN alone; the panel's „N weitere“ count still reads only the line's IBAN, so it undercounts the lines such a rule reaches on the payee's other IBANs (known limit, review F2; since a cross line of another amount is only proposed, the missing question books nothing wrongly). Merged and deployed 14 Nov 2026 (PR #304, images b4d34147ee29, no migration); `rematch-waiting` the same day booked the seven lines these gaps had left waiting (two Disney+ card lines and two Microsoft card lines through the PayPal merchant rules, three Sky lines on the older IBAN through the pay-TV rule at the same 59 €), and nothing else. Amended 15 Nov 2026 (Luis: „some didn't get booked by the rule“): A cash rule (`hh_cash`) books a card withdrawal on another IBAN at any amount when the line it was learned from and the new line both read as a card withdrawal („Debitk.“, girocard, Geldautomat, Bargeld): the Sparkasse prints another IBAN for another machine, and the amount check meant for subscriptions had left a 20 € withdrawal waiting after a rule learned on a 100 € one; any other line on that IBAN still needs the amount (PR #315, images f4bcd8cfba3f, no migration, merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026; `rematch-waiting` booked that withdrawal the same day). Amended 15 Nov 2026 (Luis: „For a booking.com bank line it didn't use the rule“): a rule learned on one merchant PayPal printed as „BOOKING BOOKIN <Straße>“ did not book its line „BOOKINGCOMB BO <dieselbe Straße>“, since neither name's words start the other's and the first words differ. One payee now also when the first words differ but one starts the other („BOOKINGCOMB“ and „BOOKING“), the shorter of 5 letters or more (`FIRST_WORD_MIN`) and not a generic first word: *strong* when one name also repeats the merchant (a later word of 5 letters or more that starts the other's first word or is started by it, „BOOKIN“ and „BOOKINGCOMB“) and the two share another word (not the first) of 5 letters or more that is not a linking word (the merchant's street), so the rule books; *weak* without both, so it only proposes with its question (tightened in review the same day: a shared town alone, which a card line prints, had made „PENNY MARKT <Stadt>“ and „PENNYLANE PUB <Stadt>“ strong). Where a first word only starts the other's and neither name repeats the merchant, the two are two payees for an IBAN's lines, so an IBAN holding both is shared and no IBAN rule books one for the other. A shorter first word („BACK“ and „BACKSTAGE“) or a generic one („HOTEL“ and „HOTELLERIE“) is no payee by its prefix. Fixture cases in `payee_identity.yaml` (`strengths` and `different`), built on `fix/transfer-named-leg`, no migration; `rematch-waiting` after the deploy books the lines such a rule now reaches. |
| D160 amended (5) (15 Nov 2026, Luis: „Can you please check for all the places where the check box is disabled and enable the pertinent. And if it's disabled then it shouldn't show.“) | **„Künftig immer so zuordnen“ for every household code a person's answer can safely repeat, and no box where none can.** (1) *Learnable from now on on a private account* (`rules/matching.learnable_household`, the stay-off set `NEVER_LEARNED`), beside the codes with a threshold, `bank_fees`, money to family and a card's settlement (D159 amended, 15 Nov 2026): `hh_tax_refund` and `hh_tax_payment` (the Finanzamt refunds and collects from one IBAN; the rule's amount sign keeps the two apart); `hh_capital_income` (a bank's interest credit, the same each quarter); `hh_other_income` and `hh_other` (the catch-alls: a payee answered there is answered there again); `hh_interest_fees` (a card's interest, one issuer); `hh_refund` (a merchant's refund, a credit rule that never hits a purchase); `hh_investment` and its four subcategories `hh_investment_property`, `_securities`, `_crypto`, `_savings` (a standing order to a platform or account the app does not hold); `hh_donation`, `hh_childcare`, `hh_household_services`, tax-relevant, allowed anyway: a monthly donation or Kita fee should not be confirmed twelve times, and the line keeps its code, so whatever proposes its return input reads it the same. Their thresholds stay None: the model never books them alone, and the box starts unticked for them (`no_default_rule_categories`), so a person ticks it on purpose; a rule only repeats that person's answer for that payee. (2) *Still never learned* (`rule.not_learnable`, as before): `uncategorized` (no answer to repeat); `internal_transfer_in` and `_out` (the transfer matcher books them from an own IBAN, D162); `hh_property_cost` (each payment pays its own bill, matched by D161); `hh_work_education` (whether a purchase is for work depends on the item, not the shop); the depot codes `security_purchase`, `security_sale`, `distribution`, `capital_income_tax`, `depot_fee` (booked by code from the depot statement's text, D165 (1)). (3) *A transfer by an own IBAN beats every rule.* On a private account a line whose counterparty IBAN is another own account's (an account's IBAN, a card's confirmed payment IBAN, a depot's IBAN, an exchange's deposit IBAN: what the transfer matcher reads) is booked as the transfer and never by a learned rule (`rules/matching.own_rule_blind`), whether the rule names the payee, the IBAN or reaches it from another IBAN; the re-check of waiting lines and `hausbuch rematch-waiting` take the same order. Until now the learned rules came first in D158's private order, so a rule on a payee (a savings account at another bank answered as `hh_investment_savings`, or a card not in the app paid to the same issuer as a card that is) would have booked the own account's next transfer as spending. This amends D158's private order to: transfer by an own IBAN, learned rules, a payment to the seller, a recorded bill, a property bill, the question (moved once more by the review fixes below: the two bill matchers now come before the rules); the rental order is unchanged. As defence in depth, every way an IBAN becomes the household's own also switches off every enabled learned rule on that IBAN (`rule.disabled`), rental rules on it included (later rental lines from it then go to the owner-transfer matcher), and re-checks the waiting lines that name it (an account created with an IBAN does so when it is private or an owner account; a rental account created without the owner flag is still not re-checked, as before): an account created with an IBAN (a bank account, or a depot with its IBAN typed), as confirming a card's payment IBAN (D176 (4)), a depot's IBAN (D156 amended) and learning an exchange's deposit IBAN (D162 amended (5)) already did (`services/accounts._own_iban`). A rule on a name is not switched off (it names no IBAN); it simply never books such a line. Lines a rule booked before stay as they are. The header comment of `tests/fixtures/phase6/matching_order.yaml` names the new order (spec commit of this amendment, and again with the review fixes). (4) *A transfer is no earlier answer of its payee.* The checks a name rule must pass (`rule.too_broad`, `rule.conflicts`), the payee's history the panel shows and the lines „Diese auch auf … umbuchen“ names or re-books leave out a line whose counterparty IBAN is another own account's and a confirmed line the transfer matcher linked (`services/matching.name_lines`, `services/transfers.matcher_linked_ids`). A card not in the app paid to the same issuer as a card that is had been refused with `rule.conflicts`, because the other card's settlements, booked and linked as transfers, carry the issuer's name. A link a person made still counts (the panel names it). The waiting amounts the panel asks about are read on the line's own IBAN, so a line on a third party's IBAN never counts an own account's lines; they are left unchanged. (5) *A box that cannot be ticked is not shown.* For one row of a code in (2), and for a payment service's line that names no merchant (D160 amended (4)), the panel shows no „Künftig immer so zuordnen“ box, no suggested pattern and no pattern field, and no reason line; `learn_rule` is sent false, and a refusal from the server still shows under the rows. This replaces D160's amendment of 13 Nov 2026 (the box greyed with its reason). *Review fixes (15 Nov 2026):* (6) *No box and no rule from a line naming an own IBAN.* An own IBAN is always a transfer: a private line whose counterparty IBAN is another own account's (the same test as `own_rule_blind`, so the two cannot drift) learns no rule, with or without a typed pattern (`rule.not_learnable`), and the panel shows for it what it shows for a service's line without a merchant: no box, no suggested pattern, no pattern field (the answer carries `own_iban`). A second, differently named payee paid to an own IBAN is re-booked by hand; no rule can beat the transfer. Rental lines are unchanged. (7) *A bill the line pays beats a learned rule.* A rule learned on the Finanzamt's IBAN for an income-tax payment booked a later private payment of a recorded Grunderwerbsteuer as `hh_tax_payment`, confirmed, and the bill's payment was never proposed. The private order is now: transfer by an own IBAN, a recorded bill, a property bill, learned rules, a payment to the seller (still after the rules, as before), D158's question, then a rule's near-miss proposal (D49). The bill's proposal goes to a person and never auto-confirms, so the change only turns an automatic booking into a question; a payment of no bill is still booked by the rule. A line a rule booked before its bill was recorded stays as it is: a re-check replaces a rule's confirmed booking only with one that auto-confirms. Fixture cases `recorded_bill_beats_a_learned_rule`, `the_same_rule_books_a_payment_of_no_bill` and `property_bill_beats_a_learned_rule` in `matching_order.yaml`. (8) *Known limit:* a line without a counterparty IBAN that a person answered as a transfer and the matcher then linked is left out of the name-rule checks of (4), so a name rule for the same payee and sign can be learned and then books such a line as spending; the household's real card lines name the card's payment IBAN and are not affected. | Luis found the box greyed on payments he answers the same way every month (the Finanzamt, a Kita, a savings plan), and a greyed box with a reason still asks the person to read why nothing can be done. A code stays off only where a rule would book something the person did not answer: a transfer or depot booking another path owns, a bill that is different each time, or a purchase whose nature the shop does not tell. Then the box on a card not in the app, paid to the same issuer as a card that is, refused the answer with `rule.conflicts` because of the other card's transfers; and had it been learned, the rule would have come before the transfer matcher and booked the next settlement of the card in the app as spending. No migration. Merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #336, images 8942d2607f22, no migration). |

Check the deposits from my dkb account to the Quirion account.“) | **A depot's cash line is booked and linked by the bank debit that names the depot, credited up to three banking days early, and every import relinks.** (1) A depot's cash line carries no IBAN, so a direct debit the depot pulls („Lastschrift“), whose headline no depot pattern knows, waited with the model's guess while the bank's debit, booked `internal_transfer_out` by the depot's IBAN, waited on a „Übertrag prüfen“ card: the matcher links two transfer legs only. Now, when a leg booked as a transfer names the other account's IBAN, exactly one counterpart fits both ways, the amounts agree to the cent and that counterpart is a line on a private account not booked as a transfer that is free for the matcher, the matcher books it `internal_transfer_in` or `_out` (method `matcher_transfer`, confidence 1.0, auto-confirmed like every matcher set) and links the pair, whichever import came first (`services/transfers._book_named_leg`, `rules/matching.named_transfer_set`). A person's or a rule's confirmed answer is never replaced: that line keeps its „Übertrag prüfen“ card with the re-book button (D176 (4)); a later model answer on the booked line is skipped as answered meanwhile; the rental account is left to its phase 2 matchers. *Free for the matcher* (review, 15 Nov 2026) means the line holds nothing or only the model's own proposal (method `llm`), and no person or rule ever confirmed an answer on it, current or superseded since (`transfers._held_answer`): a rule's or a code matcher's proposal is not the model's, and the one-off household re-sort writes the model's proposal over a person's confirmed fallback row, which the first build had read as only a proposal and replaced. A line its own account books by code as something else (a depot's „Wertpapier Verkauf“ headline, a card's interest or fee line, `matching.own_code_set`) is never booked as the leg, so a sale next to an equal debit naming the depot stays a sale in either import order. The matcher locks the other line with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and skips it while another transaction holds it (that line's own job then books and links it through its hook): waiting for it while holding the transfer lock had deadlocked against that job. (2) `relink` runs after every depot statement import, so „Auf Konto importieren“, `follow-accounts` and a plain upload all reach it, and it also retries the other accounts' transfer lines that name the account's IBAN; `hausbuch relink-transfers --workspace <id> [--dry-run]` runs it over every account once (actor `cli:relink-transfers`). (3) When one leg names the other's account by IBAN, the in-leg may be booked up to three banking days before the out-leg (a direct debit the depot credits before the bank books it); without that tie the window still starts on the out-leg's day, and never early for a credit card's leg (review, 15 Nov 2026: only the depot's direct debit motivated it and no real card pair needs it; the card settlement test that had been changed to expect the early link was restored). *(Since D162 amended (5), the same day: a credit card's leg too, when the debit names the card's payment IBAN; a real Consors pair needed it.)* Fixture `transfers.yaml` (three cases). (4) **An exchange keeps up to 2 € of a SEPA deposit without a fee row** (Luis's DKB payments of 100, 901 and 1,001 € to the exchange's deposit IBAN were credited 1 € less each, one day later). A private bank debit and an exchange's EUR deposit row whose gap is above 0 and at most 2.00 € (`rules/matching.EXCHANGE_DEPOSIT_GAP`), neither carrying a recorded fee, fit as a deposit (`deposit_gap`); deposits only, never a withdrawal, never a line of the rental account. Untied, such a pair is never linked, from either side: the deposit's card proposes the bank line (or lists every short fit) with „1,00 € behält die Börse als Einzahlungsgebühr.“ and what the link books, „„Als Einzahlung verknüpfen“ bucht diese Zahlung als Einzahlung auf <Börse> und die 1,00 € als Bankgebühren. Spätere Zahlungen an dieselbe IBAN gelten dann als Einzahlungen auf <Börse>.“ (`TransferLegView.deposit_fee`, `deposit_fee_text`, `rules/matching.deposit_link_text`; the last sentence only while the exchange has no deposit IBAN, replaced by the reason when the IBAN will not be taken), and its button reads „Als Einzahlung verknüpfen“, never a plain „Verknüpfen“ (review, 15 Nov 2026). An untied short fit whose bank line holds a confirmed answer of a person or a rule other than a transfer or its bank fee is no candidate on the card and its link by hand is refused (`transfer.legs_invalid`, nothing written): the person answers the line as a transfer first, so the first link never replaces an answer unseen (`transfers._answered_otherwise`; review, 15 Nov 2026). The first link by hand (with or without the re-book flag) books the bank line as the person's answer, `internal_transfer_out` of the credited amount plus `bank_fees` for the gap (`deposit_split_set`, computed by code, never by the model), and records the line's counterparty IBAN as the exchange account's IBAN (`services/accounts.learn_exchange_deposit_iban`, the `confirm_depot_iban` pattern: `account.updated`, every enabled rule on that IBAN switched off, the waiting lines re-checked), unless the IBAN carries other payees too (`matching.is_shared_iban`: a payment provider's IBAN would make every later payment to it a deposit) or another own account holds it; then the link and the split still happen and nothing is learned. The link's `transfer.linked` names the outcome (`deposit_iban`: `learned`, `shared_iban` or `own_account`; `accounts.deposit_iban_outcome`, review, 15 Nov 2026). From then on the bank line names the exchange by IBAN, so the matcher splits and links the remaining and the later deposits itself (method `matcher_transfer`), in either import order, through `relink-transfers`, `rematch-waiting` and every import; a line already booked whole as `internal_transfer_out` by the matcher is split when its exchange row arrives, a line a person or a rule answered otherwise is not replaced and keeps its card, where one click („Als Einzahlung verknüpfen“) books the split and links it. A link counts the split line as a transfer only when its rows are exactly `internal_transfer_out` + `bank_fees` with the fee within the 2 € (`_one_category`). Not covered: three equal deposits inside one window stay ambiguous (the real ones are a month apart); an exchange paying out from its deposit IBAN would leave a withdrawal to link by hand (Binance pays out from another IBAN); no undo of a learned deposit IBAN (unlinking keeps it and the rules it switched off); a stale IBAN, when the exchange moves its deposits to a new IBAN, is never replaced (the new one's deposits wait on cards and are linked by hand, each split, and the account keeps the old IBAN); the lock order was left as built at first (the first link by hand took the transfer lock, then the bank line, then the exchange account's row in `learn_exchange_deposit_iban`, and a person's answer with the box ticked the line and the transfer lock, through its hook, before that row, while an exchange import locks the account's row first and takes the transfer lock in its relink, so either run at the same moment as an import could deadlock and Postgres would fail one of them); *fixed 16 Nov 2026:* the exchange account's row is now always taken before the transfer lock, as the import takes it: `link_by_hand` locks the exchange rows of its movement legs first (`transfers._lock_exchanges`), and an answer with the box ticked locks the row of the exchange its panel names before the line (`transfers.lock_deposit_exchange`, nothing locked when no box would show, so an exchange that holds an IBAN is still never locked by an answer; when a link written between that read and the line's lock names another exchange, the answer learns nothing and the box shows again); `tests/db/test_services_exchange_lock_order.py` holds the row in one transaction and checks that each command, waiting for it, holds neither the transfer lock nor the line (two connections checking the order in which the locks are taken, not a live race of an import and a link; both tests fail on the old order); two cycles that do not involve the exchange row remain and predate this change: the line lock against the transfer lock between a person's answer and a link by hand of the same line, and an import's relink (the transfer lock, then the new link's foreign key on the bank line) against a person's answer on that line (the line, then the transfer lock); the review reproduced the second with three connections; Postgres aborts the answer, nothing is written, the person answers again, and no command retries a deadlock (a retry on 40P01 in the command runner is the fix, not built); a depot import reconciled later through a typed balance is not relinked (the relink runs after an import, not after a balance is typed; `relink-transfers` or the next import reaches it). Migration `0035_exchange_deposit_iban` lets an `exchange` account carry an IBAN like a depot (downgrade refused while one does); phase 7 moves to 0036 (to 0037 since D159 amended (6) took 0036).
