Without the day (no transfer cards) nothing is hidden; GET /transfers/open is unchanged. (2) The way out: the card shows „Keine Überweisung – Zahlung anders zuordnen“ under the chosen bank line, which opens that line in the allocation panel beside the list (`/inbox?umsatz=…`, not inline). Answered there as something else, it is a person's answer: it never waited again, an untied short deposit's card no longer offers it, a tied one offers it with the re-booking (PR #321, unchanged). (3) Kept listed: a line holding a person's, a rule's or a code's answer or proposal (and the one-off re-sort's proposal over a person's answer); a line holding nothing (it stays where „KI-Vorschläge holen“ reaches it); a rental line; and **an untied candidate of the same amount, also on an ambiguous card**: a plain „Verknüpfen“ links it without booking it (`link_by_hand` re-books only a tied leg, D176 (4), and refuses `rebook` on an untied one with `transfer.legs_invalid`), so the model's proposal would stay on the line and it would come back as a waiting line after the link; it stays in both places until the link books a free line as the person's transfer answer, which is a change to the linking code not built here. (4) After the link the line is booked as the transfer (and its fee) and appears nowhere. (5) *A credit card's early credit* (Luis: „There are some credit card deposits that were not directly linked. Can you check?“): the second card issuer, Consors, credited a payment on 1 Jan and the Sparkasse debit that pays it, naming the card's payment IBAN, was booked on 2 Jan, so Luis linked it by hand: the early window of D162 amended (3), the in-leg up to three banking days before the out-leg, now holds for a credit card's leg too, but only when one leg names the other's account by IBAN (`names_the_other`; for a card its confirmed payment IBAN); without the tie a card's in-leg is never earlier than the debit (`rules/matching.legs_fit` and its mirror `_fits` in `tests/fixture_models_phase6.py`). Fixture `transfers.yaml` gains a `card` account and two cases (tied, credited one banking day early: linked; untied, the same dates: no link, the card's payment gets its card past the window); `test_services_card_settlement.py::test_the_link_needs_the_d162_window` asserts the tied credit one and three banking days early links, four does not, and an untied one a day early does not. (6) *No card for a leg nothing can pair yet* (the same question): a transfer leg without a counterpart (the card „transfer.unmatched_leg“) and without a same-amount line in its by-hand window, ±31 days (`_BY_HAND`, the card's own list), whose window ends before the first bank line of every other account (its earliest line of a reconciled import) gets no card, such as a card statement's payment of 2 Dec 2025 without an IBAN while every bank account's lines start in January 2026 (`transfers._first_days`, `_before_every_history`, read once per GET only when some leg has no counterpart). An exchange's or depot's movements do not count as history: the exchanges' exports reach back years before the bank accounts (D178), so one old trade would keep every card; an exchange deposit older than every bank line therefore gets no card either. When no other account holds a line, the card stays. An older statement of another account imported later brings the counterpart into reach: the card comes back proposing the fitting line, and once that line is booked as a transfer (by the matcher, for a debit naming the card's payment IBAN; by a rule; or by a person) `after_booking` links it, as any late counterpart is (`relink` runs after depot and exchange imports only, so a bank statement relies on this per-line path; a line without the IBAN tie waits on the proposed card). `test_services_transfers.py::test_a_leg_before_every_other_accounts_history_gets_no_card` covers the edge (window end 2 Jan: a first line on 2 Jan keeps the card, on 3 Jan drops it), an exchange deposit of 2024 that neither keeps the card nor gets one, and the later import. (7) *Review, 15 Nov 2026.* (a) **A card's line is booked as the settlement leg only when the card reader reads it as the payment**: `_book_named_leg` books a `credit_card` account's line as the transfer leg only when `matching.own_code_set` reads it as a payment („Bezahlung“, a transfer set); any other card line, such as a merchant's refund („Gutschrift“), is left as it is and the pair goes to the person as a card, because with (5) a refund one banking day before a debit naming the payment IBAN would otherwise have been re-booked as the payment and linked. This also stops the auto-booking that existed before (5): a card credit that is not the payment, on the debit's day or after it, was booked as the leg by the matcher too; it now waits on the debit's card as well (principle 3: the matcher never decides a refund is a payment). Once the real payment also exists, both fit, and the debit's card asks which („transfer.ambiguous“), never links alone. `legs_fit`, the window, a depot's lines and bank lines are unchanged. `test_services_card_settlement.py::test_a_merchant_refund_before_the_debit_is_never_booked_as_the_payment`. (b) **Only a surely paired line leaves Zahlungen zuordnen**: a line tied to the card's leg by an own IBAN, or the one candidate of a proposed card („transfer.proposed“) that is an exchange deposit credited short; a by-amount candidate on a card whose counterpart is missing („Gegenbuchung fehlt“, `transfer.unmatched_leg`), even a short deposit, stays listed, since nothing says it is that leg's counterpart (`transfers.lines_on_cards`). `test_services_transfer_cards_inbox.py::test_by_amount_candidates_of_a_card_without_its_counterpart_stay_listed`. (c) **A leg that names an own account is decided by that account**: (6) compared a leg with every other account, so a debit to the other owner's account, whose statements are not imported, lost its only card once an unrelated account with later lines existed. A leg whose counterparty IBAN is an own account's IBAN (an account's own IBAN, a card's payment IBAN, an exchange's learned deposit IBAN; `transfers._own_accounts`) keeps its card while that account has no line of a reconciled import and loses it only when that account's first such line is after the window; every other account counts only for a leg that names none. An exchange or depot holds movements, not bank lines, so a debit naming an exchange's deposit IBAN keeps its card however old it is: a deposit missing from the exchange's export is a real to-do. `test_services_transfers.py::test_a_leg_naming_an_own_account_is_decided_by_that_account` (an empty named account keeps the card while the only other account starts after the window; a named account starting after the window drops it while the rental account starts before). (d) A transfer card's key on the Zu prüfen list is its leg's id: two unmatched exchange deposits of one import had shared the import id and the time, so one React key (`frontend/src/components/inbox.tsx`, no API change). Merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #327, images 91196af3f70c, no migration); `relink-transfers` linked nothing new: the one early Consors pair had been linked by hand on 14 Nov. | No migration, no API shape change; tests `tests/db/test_services_transfer_cards_inbox.py`, one route test in `tests/api/test_transfers.py`, and the tests named in (5)–(7). Merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #327, images 91196af3f70c, no migration); this cell said „Built on branch `fix/one-transfer-card`, not merged“ until 15 Nov 2026, stale since the deploy |
| D162 amended (6) (15 Nov 2026, Luis: „I think I still see redundant bank lines“) | **A line the matcher links as a transfer by an own IBAN, holding only the model's proposal of that transfer, is booked as the transfer's leg.** (1) Five lines were linked by the matcher (four depot cash credits, one exchange EUR deposit) while their own rows were still the model's proposal `internal_transfer_in` / `_out`, so each still asked „Stammt die Gutschrift … von einem eigenen Konto?“ under Zahlungen zuordnen although the pair was linked and its other leg confirmed: a proposed transfer counts as booked as a transfer (`_one_category`), so `link_leg` linked the pair as it was, and `_book_named_leg`, the only path that books a free leg (D162 amended), is reached only for a leg not booked as a transfer. (2) Now every link the matcher writes (`transfers._write` with `matched_by = matcher`, so `link_leg`, `_split_and_link`, `_book_named_leg`, `relink` and `after_booking` alike) is followed by `_confirm_tied_legs`: when one leg names the other's account by IBAN (`rules/matching.names_the_other`: a bank debit naming the depot's IBAN, a line naming the exchange's learned deposit IBAN, a debit naming a card's payment IBAN, two bank accounts), each bank or depot line of the pair on a private account whose current rows are only the model's proposal (`_free_for_matcher`: method `llm`, no person's or rule's answer ever confirmed on it), all of the transfer code the line takes, is booked by the matcher as `named_transfer_set` does (or `deposit_split_set` for a deposit credited short), method `matcher_transfer`, confidence 1.0, auto-confirmed with its events, after the link is written so `record_set`'s hook finds the leg linked and writes no second link. Principle 3 and phase 2 D40 hold: the model alone never confirms; the IBAN tie is the deterministic fact `match_transfer_line` books by anyway, and the model only agreed with it. (3) **A pair linked by amount and date alone stays as it is**: `match_transfer` links a leg booked as a transfer (even only proposed) to any other account's opposite leg of the amount within the window when no IBAN contradicts it, so any link kind can be written untied (two bank lines without IBANs, an exchange withdrawal paid from another IBAN, an exchange without a learned deposit IBAN); there the link rests on the model's guess plus an amount and date, so both proposals stay listed for a person. (4) Never touched: a line holding a person's or a rule's answer, a rule's or a code's proposal, the one-off re-sort's proposal over a person's answer, a rental-account line (its phase 2 matchers decide, D40), a line whose proposal is not that transfer code (it keeps the card flow of D176 (4)), a line its own account books by code as something else (`matching.own_code_set`), and a credit card's line its reader does not read as the payment (a refund the model proposed as a transfer keeps its proposal, D162 amended (5) (7)(a)). (5) For the links written before: `hausbuch relink-transfers --workspace <id> [--dry-run]` also runs `transfers.confirm_linked` over every current matcher link and prints „booked N linked line(s) the model had only proposed as the transfer“ (dry run: „would book“) after the „linked N transfer(s)“ line; a booked leg is no longer free, so a second run books nothing. (6) Undoing or replacing a link later goes through the ordinary correction path; nothing extra. Not covered: a leg skipped because another transaction held it (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, as in `_book_named_leg`), or a matcher link that becomes current again without a new link being written, stays linked and proposed until `relink-transfers` runs again. Also not covered (from the review, 15 Nov 2026): a tied pair a person links by hand („Verknüpfen“, `matched_by = user`, when two equal candidates made the card ambiguous) keeps the model's proposal on the picked line; a booking made this way inside the model's answer job is recorded under the job's actor (`ai`, with method `matcher_transfer` and `auto` true), as `_book_named_leg` already did; the matcher can still link a bank debit naming a card's payment IBAN to a card merchant refund the model proposed as `internal_transfer_in` (pre-existing on main, rare; the refund itself is never booked); and `relink-transfers` counts as booked only the legs of links that existed before the run. (7) *A card's settlement before the paying account's history* (Luis: a credit card payment from before the uploaded bank statements): the main card's payment credit of 2 Dec 2025 kept its „Gegenbuchung fehlt“ card (`transfer.unmatched_leg`) although the account that pays the card starts on 2 Jan 2026 (the household's history starts 1 Jan 2026), so its debit will never be imported: a card's line names no IBAN, so D162 amended (5) (6) compared it with every other account over ±31 days, a window ending 2 Jan, not before the other accounts' first lines. Now a credit card's leg is decided by the accounts that pay the card, the other accounts with a line of a reconciled import naming the card's confirmed payment IBAN (`transfers._card_payers`; there is no stored payer, so the paying account is known by its settlements): no card when every such account's first line is after the leg's D162 window (`transfer_window_end`, three banking days, the latest day the debit could be booked after an early credit). A card no line pays yet, or whose payment IBAN is unknown, keeps the rule of (5) (6). Read once per GET, only when some leg has no counterpart, like the first days. | No migration, no API change; `services/transfers._confirm_tied_legs`, `_confirm_leg`, `confirm_linked`, `_card_payers`, `_before_every_history`, `cli.relink_transfers`; tests `tests/db/test_services_transfer_linked_leg.py` (depot first and bank first, the exchange deposit, a non-transfer proposal keeping its card, an untied pair staying listed, a person's answer, a code's proposal, a card refund, the one-off and the command with `--dry-run`; for (7) a card credit of 2 Dec 2025 whose payer starts 2 Jan 2026 gets no card, one whose payer starts 3 Dec 2025 keeps it, and a card no line pays yet keeps the older rule). Merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #329, images c36adc9dd1f3, no migration); `relink-transfers` booked the 4 linked lines the model had only proposed the same day (its dry run said 4, the row's (1) counted five; the fifth was not a tied link). |
| D162 amended (7) (15 Nov 2026, Luis: „What is the 3200€ payment still there?“) | **A returned payment is paired with its original by code and booked as one by a person, on the private accounts only.** A private account sent a transfer, the bank recalled it and credited it back on the same account two days later („RUECKUEBERWEISUNG Rueckgabe aufgrund eines Recalls (Rueckrufes) <the original purpose>“). The credit names an own account's IBAN, so the transfer matcher booked it `internal_transfer_in`, and with no counterpart on another account it waited as „Überweisung ohne Gegenstück“ for ever, while the debit waited with the model's wrong guess (`hh_property_cost`). (1) **Recognition, by code, never the model** (`rules/household/returns.py`): a credit is a return when its purpose holds one of `RETURN_WORDS` (Rücküberweisung, Rückgabe, Rückruf, Recall, Retoure, Rücklastschrift; case, umlauts as ue and spacing ignored, so a word wrapped across two purpose lines still reads) and a debit of the same absolute amount was booked on the same account at most 30 days before it, the same day included (`RETURN_WINDOW_DAYS`). Among several equal debits the choice narrows to those whose purpose the credit's purpose quotes (at least 8 characters, so an empty or one-word purpose never decides), else to those paid to the credit's counterparty (IBAN, else name); when neither narrows it, every equal debit is offered and the person picks: one is never guessed. A debit already linked as a transfer is never offered. (2) **The card „Rücküberweisung erkannt“** under „Überweisungen zwischen eigenen Konten“ names the credit and the debit (or the debits to choose from) and says the two together count as nothing; it is computed on read like the transfer cards, so a new import's lines are recognised on the next read without a job. Its button „Als Rücküberweisung verbuchen“ (POST /returns, `services/returns.book_return`) books both lines through the person's ordinary answer (`review.allocate_transaction`, method `user`, their allocation events) as `internal_transfer_out` and `_in`, then writes a `transfer_link` joining the two lines of the one account (`matched_by` user, `transfer.linked`), all in one transaction: if booking a line lets the transfer matcher link it to another account after all, nothing is kept (409). A debit a person already booked otherwise is still offered and re-booked by the same answer. Refused with `transfer.return_invalid` (422) when the pair is not such a return of one private account. „Keine Rücküberweisung – Zahlung anders zuordnen“ opens the credit in the allocation panel; a person's or a rule's confirmed answer on the credit other than a transfer ends the card. One decision, one place: while the card shows, the credit's „Überweisung ohne Gegenstück“ card and the credit's (and a single debit's) proposal or „Nicht zugeordnet“ entry under Zahlungen zuordnen are left out of GET /inbox (`review._lines_on_return_cards`); several debits to choose from stay listed there too, since the card guesses none. (3) **Once booked the pair counts as nothing**: a transfer code is neither income nor spending on the Haushalt and the Übersicht, the Haushalt's „Gespart, nicht ausgegeben“ list (`transfers.list_links`) never lists a link of one account, both lines are linked so neither gets a transfer card, and Konten › Umsätze shows each half as „Gebucht: Rücküberweisung“ with the other half's date (`Transaction.returned_with`). The link counts like a matcher's link, only while both lines are booked as transfers (`transfers._current_links`): a person who re-books one half withdraws the pair and the card comes back; booked again from the card, the same link counts again and no second one is written. (4) **No migration**: the `transfer_link` table has no rule that its two legs sit on two accounts; only the link by hand (`transfers._pair`) asks for that, and POST /returns does not go through it. (5) `hausbuch pair-returns --workspace <id>` counts the cards Zu prüfen shows now and how many offer more than one debit; it never books, and `--apply` changes nothing either, since the cards are computed on read and only a person books a pair. **Not covered:** the rental account, whose lines keep phase 2's matchers (a return there gets no card and POST /returns refuses it); a return of only part of a debit, or one debit returned in several credits; a return more than 30 days after its debit; a return that names none of the words (a merchant's plain „Gutschrift“ refund stays an ordinary line, booked as `hh_refund` by a person). *Review fixes (15 Nov 2026):* (a) Zu prüfen works out what the return card drops first and lets only the transfer cards that stay hide their candidates, so another account's equal debit that the dropped „Überweisung ohne Gegenstück“ card offered keeps its own entry under Zahlungen zuordnen instead of being listed nowhere; (b) the button accepts only a pair the card offers: refused with `transfer.return_invalid` when a person or a rule has answered the credit as something other than a transfer in (a second press on a booked pair still returns its link), and neither a card nor the button takes the later line of an open possible duplicate until that question is answered; (c) the database's first filter for the return words ignores spacing as the rule does, so a word the bank wrapped across two purpose lines („RUE CKUEBERWEISUNG“) still gives the card | No migration, one route (POST /returns), one inbox kind (`returned_payment`), one transaction field (`returned_with`), one error code (`transfer.return_invalid`), the CLI `pair-returns`; tests `tests/rules/test_household_returns.py` and `tests/db/test_services_returns.py`; merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #335, images e0dfa0a46d80, no migration) |
| D162 amended (8) (15 Nov 2026, Luis: „Yes the app should learn Bison's account number“; changed by its review the same day) | **A person who confirms a bank line paired with an exchange's deposit, with the box „IBAN als Einzahlungskonto von <Börse> merken“ ticked, teaches the exchange its deposit IBAN, not only the first link of a deposit credited short.** A private bank line of −1,000 € to the exchange's IBAN and the exchange's EUR deposit row of +1,000 € the next day (exact, no fee) were linked by the matcher by amount and date alone: untied, since the exchange account held no IBAN yet, so the line kept the model's proposal `internal_transfer_out` (D162 amended (6) (3)). Until now only `_link_deposit_by_hand` (a deposit credited up to 2 € short, D162 amended (4)) called `accounts.learn_exchange_deposit_iban`, so a person confirming that line taught nothing and the next deposit (a later DKB line, uploaded by CSV) was again pairable only by amount and date and stayed a proposal. (1) **The box in the allocation panel.** When the line is a private bank debit that is the **out** leg of a **current** link (`_current_links`, written by the matcher or a person) whose **in** leg is a deposit movement of an account of kind `exchange`, and `accounts.deposit_iban_outcome` says the line's IBAN would be learned, `GET`/`PUT /transactions/{id}/allocations` carries `deposit_iban_offer` (the deposit, the exchange's label, the deposit's date and amount, and a sentence, `transfers.deposit_iban_offer`); the panel then shows, for an answer of „Übertrag auf eigenes Konto“ alone or with bank fees, the box „IBAN als Einzahlungskonto von <Börse> merken“, **ticked by default**, and under it „Diese Zahlung ist mit der Einzahlung vom <Datum> über <Betrag> auf <Börse> verknüpft. „Bestätigen“ mit Häkchen merkt sich die IBAN dieser Zahlung als Einzahlungskonto von <Börse>. Spätere Zahlungen an dieselbe IBAN gelten dann als Einzahlungen auf <Börse>. Ohne Häkchen wird die IBAN nicht übernommen.“ (`rules/matching.deposit_iban_offer_text`, the card's wording below). A refused outcome (`shared_iban`, `own_account`) and an exchange that holds an IBAN show no box. The answer's body sends `learn_deposit_iban` (default false); only when it is true, the command is a person's (actor type `user`), and after the answer is written the line's live rows are confirmed and resolve to `internal_transfer_out` (`_one_category`: the whole line, or the transfer plus its fee within 2 €) and the line is still the out leg of the link to **the deposit the offer named** (read before the answer is written), does `review.allocate_transaction` call `transfers.learn_deposit_iban_on_confirm`, which learns in the same transaction. So a link the answer itself lets the matcher write (the model had guessed another category, say an investment, and the person corrects it to the transfer) teaches nothing with that answer: Zu prüfen closes the panel on „Bestätigen“ and the line leaves the list, so the box shows only when the person opens the line again from Konten › Umsätze (/inbox?umsatz=…), where „Bestätigen“ with the same answer and the box ticked learns (the same answer on a confirmed line learns whenever the box is sent ticked, whoever confirmed the line before). An older client, the transfer card's own answers, a pre-filled re-booking from a to-do and „Alle N bestätigen“ (`confirm_category`) send nothing and teach nothing. A withdrawal's bank credit (`internal_transfer_in`) never shows the box: only the out leg of a deposit can teach. **Why a box (review, 15 Nov 2026):** as first built, the person's plain confirmation learned silently, so a link the matcher made by amount and date alone, which the person never saw, decided the IBAN: a transfer of 1,000 € to an own account that is not in the app, and a 1,000 € deposit on the exchange the next day paid from elsewhere, would teach the exchange that foreign IBAN; every later payment to it would then be booked as a deposit without a person, the exchange's real deposits would no longer pair, the account behind the IBAN could no longer be added, and nothing in the app removes a learned IBAN. Ticked by default because the usual pair is the right one, with the deposit's date and amount on the panel so a wrong one is seen before „Bestätigen“. **Lock order:** `learn_deposit_iban_on_confirm` reads the outcome first and locks the exchange's row only to learn (`learn_exchange_deposit_iban` reads it again under the lock), so a person's answer never waits for an exchange that holds its IBAN already (an exchange import takes the account's row first, then the transfer lock). (2) **A link by hand** (`link_by_hand`) of an exact (not short) bank line ↔ exchange deposit pair learns too, when the bank line's live rows are confirmed as `internal_transfer_out` and the line's latest `transaction.allocated` / `transaction.reallocated` event is a person's (actor type `user`, `auto` false; the rows themselves cannot tell, since a confirmed proposal keeps the model's method), so a line the matcher, a rule or the model booked, or re-booked automatically after a person's answer, teaches nothing (both tested, and both tests fail with that gate switched off); a line still only proposed teaches when the person confirms it later with the box ticked, by (1). The same holds for a click on a pair the matcher linked since the card was shown (`link_by_hand` returns that link). Before the click, the transfer card says so on such a candidate, where a deposit credited short shows its fee text (`TransferLegView.deposit_fee_text` with `deposit_fee` null, `rules/matching.deposit_iban_link_text`): „„Verknüpfen“ merkt sich die IBAN dieser Zahlung als Einzahlungskonto von <Börse>. Spätere Zahlungen an dieselbe IBAN gelten dann als Einzahlungen auf <Börse>.“, or the reason it is not taken („Die IBAN wird nicht übernommen, weil …“); a line still only proposed gets no such text; the button stays „Verknüpfen“. (3) **The guards are `learn_exchange_deposit_iban`'s, unchanged**: nothing when the exchange holds an IBAN already, when the line is not on a private account or has no counterparty IBAN; refused (the confirmation and the link stand) when another own account holds the IBAN (`own_account`) or the IBAN carries other payees too (`shared_iban`). (4) **Recorded where the short path records it**: a link by hand this rule writes names the outcome on its `transfer.linked` (`deposit_iban`); when the link existed before the confirmation (written by the matcher), only a learned IBAN is recorded, by the `account.updated` event learning writes, and a refusal there is not recorded (no new event type). (5) Learning switches off the enabled learned rules on that IBAN and queues the re-check of the waiting lines that name it, so the next deposit to it names the exchange by IBAN (`names_the_other`), is booked by the matcher and linked (D162 amended (6)), whichever import comes first. **Not covered:** a corrected guess as above teaches only once the line is opened again from Konten › Umsätze, which nothing prompts; a pair the matcher never linked (two equal candidates, or the exchange's export not imported yet) teaches nothing until a person links it; a line a person confirmed as a transfer *before* the matcher linked it (the bank line answered before the exchange's export arrived) teaches nothing when the matcher links it later, since the matcher's link is no person's act: its panel, opened again from Konten › Umsätze, then shows the box, and the IBAN is learned once the person confirms it again with the box ticked (or from the next deposit confirmed so); on the confirm path a refusal (`shared_iban`, `own_account`) is not recorded, only a learned IBAN is (its `account.updated`); and nothing is back-filled by itself: a paired line a person confirmed before this change shows the box when opened and teaches only when confirmed with it. Merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #349, images 855c26171d79, no migration) after one review round (a silent learn from a pair nobody saw could store a foreign IBAN that nothing in the app can remove, hence the box; the hand-link person gate pinned by tests; the exchange row no longer locked when nothing is to learn, the opposite lock order of an export upload at the one learning moment left for a later change) | No migration, no event type; one body field (`ConfirmAllocationsBody.learn_deposit_iban`) and one response field (`TransactionAllocations.deposit_iban_offer`, schema `DepositIbanOffer`), `openapi.json` and `types.ts` regenerated; `services/transfers.deposit_iban_offer` and `learn_deposit_iban_on_confirm`, called from `services/review.allocate_transaction` (the first build's hook in `services/allocations` removed), `rules/matching.deposit_iban_offer_text` and `deposit_iban_link_text`, the box in `frontend/src/components/allocations.tsx`; tests `tests/db/test_services_exchange_deposit_iban_confirm.py`; not yet merged |
| D162 amended (9) (15 Nov 2026, Luis: „There are two transfers from the dkb account to the Quirion Depot that weren't automatically booked.“) | **No „Gegenbuchung fehlt“ card for a leg the named account's imported history does not reach yet: the mirror of D162 amended (5) (6) and (6) (7).** A private bank account pays two monthly savings transfers into two depots early each month, each debit naming its depot's IBAN; the matcher books them `internal_transfer_out` and links each to its depot's cash credit once the depot's monthly cash statement is imported. The depot statements arrive a month later, so after three banking days each new debit is booked but unlinked and showed „Überweisung ohne Gegenstück“ (`transfer.unmatched_leg`) in Zu prüfen, although its counterpart cannot exist in the app before the next depot statement is uploaded; this repeated every month for every savings plan. (1) **The rule.** A booked transfer leg without a counterpart and without a same-amount line in its by-hand window (±31 days, the card's own list; the same branch as (5) (6)) gets no card when it names an own account by IBAN (an account's IBAN, a credit card's payment IBAN, an exchange's learned deposit IBAN; every account holding that IBAN, so two cards sharing one payment IBAN both count) and every such account's imported history ends before the leg's D162 window ends (`transfer_window_end`, the third banking day after the leg). **The card direction too** (Luis: „Does the Quirion transfer card also apply to the credit card card?“): a bank debit of the monthly settlement names the card's payment IBAN, so it is decided by the card account, whose statements are uploaded monthly; a card statement is a reconciled import of its own (`card_pdf`) with its lines on the card account, so its history is read exactly as a bank account's, and a debit of 3 Nov while the card's statements end on 30 Oct gets no card until the next card statement reaches the window without the settlement credit. And the other way round, a credit card's payment credit (its line names no IBAN) is decided by the accounts that pay the card (`_card_payers`, as in (6) (7)): no card while every payer's history ends before the window end. (2) **An account's history** ends at the latest booking date of its lines from reconciled imports, not excluded as duplicates (the filters of `_first_days`), or, for an account synced over FinTS, the end of its latest successful sync (`bank_sync.requested_to` of a run that ended `nothing_new`, or `done` with a reconciled import), whichever is later: a quiet FinTS account whose sync brings no new line keeps no import (D127), so its lines alone would never move forward (`transfers._last_days`). (3) **Unchanged:** a leg naming no own account keeps today's card (an unrelated account whose statements stop would otherwise hide every card); a named account holding no line and no sync keeps the card, as in (5)'s review (the other owner's account, never imported); a leg with a same-amount line in reach keeps its card with that candidate; an exchange named by its learned deposit IBAN keeps its card, since its history is the export's movements, not bank lines; a leg whose line is booked otherwise is never a transfer leg. A line holding only the model's proposal of a transfer counts as booked as a transfer (`_Leg.booked`), so it is covered too, and it stays listed under Zahlungen zuordnen with its proposal. (4) **The card comes back by itself**: nothing is stored, so once the named account's next import (or sync) reaches the window end and still no counterpart was found, the card shows on the next read; with the counterpart, a depot statement's import links it (`relink` after the import, D162 amended) or `after_booking` does once that line is booked as a transfer. (5) **Everywhere the card counts**: GET /inbox, the sidebar's count (the list's length), the section counts and GET /transfers/open all read `open_transfers`, so they agree; Konten › Umsätze shows such a line as „Gebucht: Übertrag …“, as before, and has no „Gegenbuchung fehlt“ text; the Haushalt's „Gespart, nicht ausgegeben“ counts links only, so the leg joins it once linked, as before. **Limits:** an account never imported again keeps such a leg silent for ever (its booking as a transfer stands, it is only never paired); since phase 5 D122 amended (15 Nov 2026) such an account, unless it is an exchange or fetched daily over FinTS, gets the card „Kontoauszug fällig“ ten days after the end of the month that follows its last statement (deployed 15 Nov 2026, PR #351), so the missing statement is asked for; the history ends at the last line, not at the statement's period end (the import stores no period end), so a statement whose last line is days before its end leaves a leg in that gap silent until the next statement. Merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #345, images 6381f72ac1c3, no migration); on the live data no „Gegenbuchung fehlt“ card remained the same day. | No migration, no API change; `services/transfers._last_days`, `_own_account_sets`, `_after_every_history`; tests in `tests/db/test_services_transfers.py` (the two depots' monthly debits, the window-end edge, a FinTS run without new lines, a leg naming no own account, a named account never imported, both card directions), and `test_services_transfer_linked_leg.py`'s (6) (7) case gives its payer a line after the window; no fixture case (`transfers.yaml` covers the pure `match_transfer`; (5) (6) and (6) (7) added DB tests only) |
| D196 (15 Nov 2026, Luis: „Is there any way to input the information in the app without adding the variable to Scaleway. The app never shows the password.“, choosing „Encrypted in database“ over Scaleway Secret Manager and over container secrets only; then „Maybe the username as well?“) | **A bank login's PIN can be typed into the app, and the PIN and the login name are never stored in readable form.** (1) **The rule.** A PIN is kept either as a container secret (D119, D163, unchanged) or encrypted in the database under a key that exists only in the API container; it is never shown, logged, put into an event, a job payload or a read model, and no endpoint returns it or its ciphertext. (2) **The key.** HKDF-SHA256 over the existing `HAUSBUCH_SESSION_KEY`, info `hausbuch/fints-pin/v1` for PINs and `hausbuch/fints-login/v1` for login names, no salt: the derive-from-the-session-key-with-a-label pattern of D119's state MAC (which is one labelled HMAC, kept as it is), so no new secret and no Scaleway step; each row stores `key_version` 1. (3) **The cipher.** AES-256-GCM (`cryptography`), a random 96-bit nonce per write; the AAD is the workspace id and the credential slot for a PIN, the workspace id and the connection id for a login, rebuilt from the row's own columns, so a ciphertext copied to another row or workspace does not decrypt. (4) **Storage, migration `0039_bank_credential`.** A table `bank_credential` (workspace, slot under D163's CHECK, nonce, ciphertext, key version, `set_by`, created and updated), unique per workspace and slot, forced row-level security, the app role with SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE. A PIN is a secret, not history: setting it again replaces the row (an UPDATE) and „PIN löschen“ deletes it, so the table has **no append-only trigger** (principle 4 keeps the history of the act, not of the value: an old PIN kept would be a secret kept for nothing). The event log records each act with the slot and the actor only: `workspace.bank_pin_set`, `workspace.bank_pin_deleted` (named for the workspace, since an event's type starts with its entity's and a slot is not an entity; the brief's `bank.pin_*`). `bank_connection` gains `login_nonce`, `login_ciphertext`, `login_key_version`; `login_name` becomes nullable and a CHECK keeps exactly one form per row. The migration cannot encrypt (the key is not in the database), so **the API encrypts every readable login at its start-up**, workspace by workspace, and again at the first sync or change of a connection, nulling the readable column in the same UPDATE; a CLI was not chosen because the operator's computer does not hold the session key. The downgrade is refused while a PIN is stored or a login exists only encrypted. (5) **Precedence.** For a slot, the PIN typed in the app wins over the container variable (it is newer and explicit); deleting it falls back to the variable. The sync job resolves the PIN in process at sync time, stored first, then `Settings.fints_pins`; `bank.pin_missing` only when neither exists. `Settings` now accepts the product id without any PIN variable (D163's "the product id at least one PIN" dropped), so every PIN can live in the app; a PIN variable without the product id is still refused. (6) **The client state.** Its MAC (D119) is still computed over the readable login, decrypted in process, so the live connection's stored state keeps verifying after its login is encrypted: no new pushTAN. (7) **The API.** `PUT /bank-credentials/{slot}` with `{pin}` (a `SecretStr`, 5 to 64 characters, never echoed, a 422 never quotes it), `DELETE /bank-credentials/{slot}`; the Bankabruf read gains `credential_source` (`app`, `server`, `none`), `credential_set_at`, and `credential_readable` and `name_readable` (whether the stored PIN and the login still decrypt; named without "pin" and "login", which D119's guard tests reserve so no read model can carry a secret); `GET /bank-credential-slots` also lists the slots with a PIN in the app, so „Bankabruf einrichten“ can say so before a connection exists. Any signed-in member may set or delete a slot's PIN; nobody can read one. (8) **The card.** „PIN (Online-Banking)“, a password field never prefilled and not offered for saving by the browser, with „PIN speichern“; once saved „PIN in der App gespeichert am <Datum>“ with „PIN ändern“ and „PIN löschen“; with only the variable „PIN auf dem Server gesetzt“ (and „PIN in der App speichern“ to replace it); with neither „Noch keine PIN hinterlegt.“ and the field; the same field in „Bankabruf einrichten“ while the slot has no usable PIN, saved before the connection (by slot, so a refused connection keeps it), optional there; a PIN or login that no longer decrypts is named on the card with „PIN ändern“ or „Zugang ändern“. **Threat model.** A copy of the database alone reveals no PIN and no login name; whoever reads the API container's environment (the session key) can decrypt them all, exactly as that environment already revealed every container PIN. **Limits.** Rotating `HAUSBUCH_SESSION_KEY` makes every stored PIN and login unreadable: a sync then fails cleanly with `bank.pin_unreadable` or `bank.login_unreadable` and the card asks to type the Anmeldename and the PIN again (it already cost one pushTAN approval, D119). | Amends D119 ("never in the DB") and ARCHITECTURE's Security bullet, and D163's start-up check; migration 0039 (phase 7's step 0 moves on: 0040 went to phase 6 step 6's frozen month-end, so the wallets take 0041); `services/bank_credentials.py`, `services/bank_sync.py`, `jobs/bank_sync.py`, `api/routes/bank_sync.py`, the Bankabruf card; runbook step 17 (the app first, the variable as the alternative) and step 14's rotation note. **As built (15 Nov 2026, branch `feat/pin-in-app`; merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #353, images 57268b899c10, migration 0039 applied; the review's verdict was ship after fixes, all five applied; this image cannot be rolled back, since its first start stores the logins encrypted only); checked the same day on the live data: the rental account's login was sealed at the first start (its readable column emptied), no PIN typed in the app yet (stale the same evening: two PINs were typed on 15 Nov 2026 before the address binding of PR #382, so both are refused at their next sync after it deployed and must be typed again; Luis met the refusal on the DKB login on 16 Nov 2026, and the Bankabruf card and the Zu prüfen card now say why (PRs #393 and #395, deployed 16 Nov 2026, images 88c3669b7672 and c8f0b42074b9, no migration))):** commits 2481ffa (spec, migration 0039), 9036d33 (service, sync wiring, routes), bf72aea (HTTP tests: no echo, no log), then the card and the runbook; the events are `workspace.bank_pin_set` and `workspace.bank_pin_deleted`; the error codes `bank.pin_unreadable`, `bank.login_unreadable` and `bank.pin_invalid`; a sync refused for an unreadable PIN or login ends its run as failed with that code and never reaches the bank. **Security review fixes (15 Nov 2026, verdict „ship after fixes“, no leak found):** the first image with 0039 cannot be rolled back (earlier images cannot read an encrypted login), so the runbook keeps `HAUSBUCH_FINTS_PIN` set until it is confirmed working and rolls back only to a D196 tag (99335f2); the start-up encryption seals a login only while the row still holds the login it read, so a login changed meanwhile is never overwritten by the old one; a typed PIN and Anmeldename are trimmed of surrounding blanks, the length counted after; the login is kept out of the sync plan's repr (e33ae29); `app_slots` carry `used` (whether a connection names the slot) and the card lists a PIN no connection uses under „Gespeicherte PINs ohne Bankzugang“ with „PIN löschen“, never deleted by itself (8da3b18). **Amended 16 Nov 2026: a PIN typed into the app is sent only to the FinTS address it was typed for** (merged and deployed 16 Nov 2026, PR #382, images 90404265d9cb, no migration; this said „branch `fix/phase6-small`, not merged“ until then). Before, any member who could edit a Bankabruf could change its FinTS address, and the next sync sent the stored PIN to the new host. Now `PUT /bank-credentials/{slot}` takes `{pin, fints_url}` (https only, the address the card shows; the create form sends the address typed above it), and the row seals the SHA-256 of that address in front of the PIN in the same AES-GCM plaintext, under `key_version` 2 and info `hausbuch/fints-pin/v2`. Only a plain https address (a host of letters, digits, dots and hyphens or a bracketed IPv6 literal, an optional numeric port, a path of ordinary characters) is normalised, to the host lower-cased, an explicit port (443 when none) and the path; any other shape (user info, a backslash, a query, a fragment) is bound verbatim and matches only itself, because URL parsers disagree on such strings: `urlsplit` reads `https://evil.example\@fints.example.org/` as the bank's host while urllib3, which python-fints' requests session dials with, reads `evil.example` (checked 16 Nov 2026), so a normalised form of it would have let a changed address pass as the bound one. The binding is authenticated by the same tag, so **no migration** and no new column (the ciphertext stays within the CHECK's 17 to 1024 bytes). The address is fixed by the request that carries the PIN, never by a connection's create or edit, so editing a connection can never re-bind a stored PIN. The sync job compares it with the address it is about to dial (the run plan's `fints_url`) and refuses with `bank.pin_server_changed` before any request (the fake client is never built); the stored PIN is not replaced by the container variable either. The card reads `credential_server_matches` and says „Die gespeicherte PIN gilt für eine andere FinTS-Adresse“ with „PIN ändern“; the failed run's card and the error text say the same. **Old rows:** a version 1 row (typed before the binding) names no address, so it is refused the same way and must be typed once more; it is never bound to whatever address the connection has at its first use, since that address may already be the changed one. The live data held no PIN typed in the app (checked 15 Nov 2026, above), so nothing has to be retyped there. The container-variable PIN path is unchanged: it is the operator's and follows the connection. **Not covered:** the Anmeldename also follows a changed address (it identifies the login, it does not unlock it); one slot shared by two connections with different addresses works for the address its PIN was last typed for only. Tests: `tests/db/test_bank_credentials.py` (unchanged address used, the same server written another way, a changed address refused with no client built, the PIN typed again used at the new address, a PIN typed for another address than the connection's, a version 1 row, the container variable unchanged), `tests/test_bank_credentials_unit.py` (the normalisation), `tests/api/test_bank_sync_api.py` (the body needs an https address; no echo). |
| D260 (16 Nov 2026, Luis: „I want to be able to add the secrets in the app not through scaleway. Like the api keys etc...“) | **Provider keys, addresses and mail settings can be typed in the app, under Einstellungen › Zugangsschlüssel, and are stored encrypted, the way D196 stores a bank PIN.** (1) **What moved.** A closed allowlist of fourteen names, each a `Settings` field and the container variable `HAUSBUCH_<NAME>` of the same name, in four groups on the card: „KI“ (`anthropic_api_key`); „Blockchains & Kurse“ (`etherscan_key`, `solana_rpc_url`, `bsc_rpc_url`, `helius_key`, `coingecko_key`); „Bank“ (`fints_product_id`); „E-Mail“ (`tem_secret_key`, `tem_project_id`, `mail_from` for the reminder digest; `inbox_imap_user`, `inbox_imap_password`, `inbox_address`, `inbox_label` for email-in). Each name is secret (a key, a password, an RPC address that may carry a key) or not (the TEM project, the sender, the inbox's user, address and label): a secret is never returned by any endpoint, a setting that is not secret is shown back on the card, and neither ever reaches an event, a log line, a job payload or a read model. (2) **What cannot move, and why.** `HAUSBUCH_DATABASE_URL` (the app needs it to reach the table the values live in), `HAUSBUCH_SESSION_KEY` (the encryption key is derived from it; stored under itself it would be circular, and a copy of the database would then open every value), `HAUSBUCH_IBAN_HMAC_KEY` (it keys every stored IBAN and address hash; changing it from a screen would orphan them all, and it is not a provider's key), and the owner URL (never on the server at all; it stays on the operator's computer). Also not on the list: the bank PINs and logins (D196 has their own card), and the plain container settings that only an operator changes (the IMAP host and Gmail's authserv-id, the TEM region, the Esplora and Etherscan base URLs, the switches such as `HAUSBUCH_AI_ENABLED`). (3) **Crypto and storage, migration `0045_app_secret`.** HKDF-SHA256 over the session key, info `hausbuch/app-secret/v1` (D196's derivation, its own label); AES-256-GCM, a random 96-bit nonce per write, the AAD the workspace id and the name, so a row copied to another workspace or name does not decrypt; a value of 1 to 512 bytes. Table `app_secret` (workspace, name under a CHECK of the allowlist, nonce, ciphertext, key version, `set_by`, created and updated), unique per workspace and name, forced row-level security, the app role with SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE, no append-only trigger: a secret, not history (D196 (4)'s reasoning). Events `workspace.app_secret_set` and `workspace.app_secret_deleted` carry the name and the actor only. (4) **Precedence, resolved per workspace when it is used.** A value typed in the app wins over the container variable; deleting it falls back to the variable. Each consumer reads its workspace's values at the moment it needs them, never from a job payload: the chain sources (one per address read; a key or RPC address set re-queues the sync of the wallets that read through it), the daily price run (one CoinGecko client per key, so workspaces with the same key share its answers; the ECB, Kraken and the funds' files stay shared), the model client (built per key and cached by the key's SHA-256; a client handed in by tests or evals is kept), the FinTS sync and its daily schedule (the product id), the reminder digest (the TEM trio) and the email-in poll (the four inbox values). (5) **An unreadable value.** A typed value that no longer decrypts (the session key was rotated) is never replaced by the container's: the card shows „Kann nicht gelesen werden“ and „Schlüssel kann nicht gelesen werden, bitte neu eintragen“; a wallet reads `key_unreadable`; a price run prices that workspace's coins from Kraken alone and logs `coingecko:key_unreadable`; the model client is none (a read ends at gate 1, `ai.disabled`); the bank, mail and inbox groups read as not configured. (6) **Start-up checks moved to the moment of use.** `Settings` no longer refuses a container PIN without the product id (a sync with a PIN and no product id anywhere still fails with `bank.not_configured`), `HAUSBUCH_AI_ENABLED=true` without the key (a job without a key ends at `ai.disabled`; „KI-Vorschläge anfordern“ is refused with it), or a partial TEM or inbox set (the digest or the poll then does nothing, as an unconfigured mailer did). The host, authserv-id and region checks stay. (7) **Validation.** Keys are stripped, then refused when empty, containing a blank or a control character, or over 512 bytes (`app_secret.invalid`); the two RPC addresses must pass the same http(s) check `Settings` uses (`app_secret.url_invalid`); the Gmail password and the label may contain blanks; the sender, the Gmail account and the forwarding address must be mail addresses, the TEM project a UUID, and the product id must pass the check the sync uses; a refusal never quotes the value. (8) **The API.** `GET /app-secrets` (each name with its group, label, purpose line, `secret`, the container variable's name, `source` ∈ {app, container, none, unreadable}, `updated_at`, and the value for a name that is not secret only), `PUT /app-secrets/{name}` with `{value}`, `DELETE /app-secrets/{name}`; any signed-in member may set or delete; nobody can read a secret back. **Threat model.** As D196: a copy of the database alone reveals nothing; whoever reads the API container's environment (the session key) can decrypt every value. New with this decision: a member can now point the server at a host of their choosing through an RPC address, and redirect the IMAP login, the TEM sender and the FinTS product id; members are the household, so this was accepted, and the review should look at it. **Not wired:** the Helius key is stored and listed, but nothing constructs the Helius label client yet (D181 (d)), so it has no consumer. | Migration 0045, the number assigned for this work; **phase 8's spec had settled 0045 for its step 0 (its Q-O126) and phase 9's 0046, neither started, so those two specs must move to 0046 and 0047** (not edited here; they moved further, to 0048 and 0049, after phase 7 D261 took 0046 and D260 amended (10) took 0047); amends ARCHITECTURE's Security bullet (the session key now also derives the provider keys' key), phase 5 D119 and D123 (the product id and the TEM and inbox values may be typed in the app), phase 7 D181 (a chain key or RPC address typed in the app wins); `services/app_secrets.py`, `api/routes/app_secrets.py`, `ai/per_workspace.py`, the consumers named in (4), the card on Einstellungen; runbook steps 13 to 19 and the rotation note of „Before you start“. **Merged and deployed 16 Nov 2026 (PR #403, images 11156b6bfc8f, migration 0045 applied; review verdict ship; this said „branch …, not merged“ until then). Open from the review until D260 amended (9) below: an unreadable typed value showed only on Einstellungen, not as a Zu prüfen card.** As built (16 Nov 2026, branch `worktree-agent-a32b81f5fd4942c85`): commits 2a514762 (table, service, migration 0045), b19d978c (the allowlist widened to the bank and mail values), 58772641 (routes, chain sources), d99386d3 (the FinTS product id), 5977bacf (mail and inbox), 122ccdae (prices), 1074d79e (the model client per workspace), 2c3df0ab (the card), then these docs. A URL name whose value equals the public default reads as „Nicht gesetzt“. **D260 amended (9), 16 Nov 2026 (principle 7; numbered (9) because the decision's own items run to (8)): an unreadable value is a to-do, not a silence.** Merged and deployed 16 Nov 2026 (PR #405, images 7a31c26a406f, no migration). Until then (5) switched a group off without a word: the daily bank fetch skipped the workspace with one INFO line and no Zu prüfen card (only the 35-day stale reminder, which does not say why), the reminder digest and the email-in poll returned silently, and a read ended at `ai.disabled`, whose text said no key was entered. Now Zu prüfen shows one card, computed on read (`app_secret_unreadable`, no migration), while any value typed in the app no longer decrypts: title „Gespeicherte Zugangsschlüssel lassen sich nicht mehr lesen“, the text „In der App gespeicherte Zugangsschlüssel lassen sich nicht mehr lesen, meist weil der Sitzungsschlüssel des Servers gewechselt wurde. Betroffen – <Gruppe>: <Bezeichnung>, …“, one sentence per thing that does not run (KI: „Dokumente werden nicht automatisch ausgelesen, und die KI schlägt keine Zuordnung von Zahlungen vor.“; Etherscan, Solana, BNB Smart Chain: their addresses are not read; CoinGecko: „Die täglichen Coin-Kurse kommen nur von Kraken.“; Bank: „Der Bankabruf läuft nicht, weder täglich noch mit „Jetzt abrufen“.“; E-Mail: „Die tägliche Erinnerungs-E-Mail wird nicht verschickt.“ and „Weitergeleitete Belege werden nicht aus Gmail abgerufen (E-Mail-Eingang).“; Helius none, it has no consumer), then „Hausbuch nimmt dafür nicht den Wert aus der Server-Konfiguration.“, and the link „Zugangsschlüssel öffnen“ to Einstellungen › Zugangsschlüssel (`/settings#zugangsschluessel`); it goes once each value is typed again or deleted, and names a value by its label only, never the value. The bank schedule, the digest and the poll count the cause as `key_unreadable` and log their run at WARNING; a workspace that never had a value stays inert and silent, and the container's value still never stands in. The two `ai.disabled` texts now say „keinen lesbaren Anthropic-Schlüssel“ and to type it again when the card shows „Kann nicht gelesen werden“ (no new error code: the stored code stays `ai.disabled`). **D260 amended (10), 16 Nov 2026 (Luis: „I got the elster id. Can you also add a settings where I can input it?“): the ELSTER Hersteller-ID typed in the app.** A fifteenth allowlisted name, `elster_hersteller_id` (the Settings field and container variable `HAUSBUCH_ELSTER_HERSTELLER_ID`), in a fifth group „ELSTER“ at the end of the card, labelled „ELSTER-Hersteller-ID“: the number ELSTER assigned Hausbuch as a software maker, which ERiC needs even to validate a return without sending it (ARCHITECTURE, „Input for a later phase: filing through ERiC“). Secret like the FinTS product id: stored encrypted, never returned, logged or put into an event; the card shows only that it is stored. Checked as 1 to 10 ASCII digits after trimming (`app_secret.invalid` otherwise, never quoting the value). Migration `0047_elster_hersteller_id` widens `app_secret_name_check`; its downgrade deletes the stored IDs (lifting FORCE RLS for the delete) and restores 0045's CHECK, unlike 0045's own downgrade, which refuses.
