### 14.1 Readings and results (filled as the steps land)

**Step 0 (12 Nov 2026, PR #218).** Where the code did not match the spec, the fixtures follow the code and the spec is corrected here:

- The phase 3 fixture `private_cost.yaml` named in the artifact table does not exist; D161's two cases went into `tests/fixtures/phase3/anlage_v_2026.yaml`, where the private-cost cases live.
- `rules/matching.match_transaction` (§14) is not a pure function; the matching order lives in `services/matching`, so `matching_order.yaml` states the outcome at each stage and step 1 decides where it is replayed.
- D173's `balance_source = typed` is `user` in the code (the enum value since phase 2).
- `ParsedStatement` has no field for DKB's „Kontostand vom“ balance; in `dkb_csv.yaml` the parser returns no closing balance and the import-level expectation carries the pre-fill; step 1 adds the field.
- `issuer_nav.yaml` holds the adapters' NAV rows as data; the recorded issuer files and their parse come with step 5.
- `bison_csv.yaml` rows are the parser's output (`MovementLine`), not CSV text, until Q-O59 is answered on the real export.
- D171 lists `asset` among the append-only tables, §10 and D167 make it mutable (name and price-source ids, `asset.updated`); the migration follows §10, and D171 is read that way.
- A Sparkasse card line's purpose ends with the card's expiry („Debitk.1 2028-12“), which today's service-period reading takes as December 2028; `rules.yaml` case `name_rule_card_expiry_is_no_period` is pending step 1, which must read no period from it.
- The review found that the category picker on Zu prüfen offered the 28 new codes on rental lines (`hh_insurance` „Versicherungen“ beside `insurance` „Versicherung“), and that a confirmed booking with one of them vanishes from the draft without a to-do; fixed before the merge (status header). Step 1 widens the refusal to the per-purpose rule of D159 for private lines.

**Step 3 (12 Nov 2026, PR #220).** Built as specced, with three review fixes: a later sync's 14-day overlap stops at the end of the history imported from files without the bank's reference (`unreferenced_through`, D173 (4) amended), so a DKB CSV history and a FinTS window never import the same payment twice; DKB is recognised in the bank's parameters as „DKB“ or „Deutsche Kreditbank“ until the first dialog shows its spelling (D173 (7)); `bank.window_invalid` names both of its causes. A connection on a private account without a reconciled import is refused with `bank.history_missing` (§15). Still open: DKB's bank parameters (camt or MT940, the approval cadence, HIEKPS) are read at the first DKB dialog (Q-O70).

**The card statement (12 Nov 2026, Q-O77, before step 2b).** One real monthly statement read from Luis's computer (never committed; no figure from it appears here). The portal offers PDF only. The PDF is digital (pypdf extracts the text cleanly, two pages). Page 1: a statement number made of the customer number, the account number and the period's first and last day; the period; a summary „Kontoauszug“ with Alter Saldo, Rückzahlungen, Einkauf, Bargeldabhebungen & Quasi-Geld, Zinsen und sonstige Gebühren and Neuer Saldo, owed amounts negative, which reconcile with the lines to the cent; the card masked to its first six and last four digits; the payment details with the issuer's IBAN; the minimum payment and its due date (not needed). Page 2: one row per line with Bew.ID (the issuer's transaction id), Buchung (booking date), Bew.Datum (transaction date, absent on a payment or an interest line), Beschreibung (merchant and place, wrapping over two lines), the original amount and currency with its Wechselkurs, the amount in EUR and D or C; the payment received is its own C line „Bezahlung“, the interest a D line without a merchant; amounts use a space as thousands separator and a point as decimal mark. D176 (3) and (4) are amended accordingly.

**Step 3, a gap found 12 Nov 2026.** The account page still showed the Bankabruf card on the rental account only (`purpose === "rental"`, phase 5's condition), so a private account could not be connected although the API accepts it (with `bank.history_missing` until its export is imported); found while listing Luis's next steps, before he tried. Fixed in PR #228 (merged and deployed 12 Nov 2026, images 934489c56235, no migration): the card is shown on every account of kind `bank_current` or `bank_savings`.

**Step 1 (12 Nov 2026, PR #221).** Where the build differs from the spec:

- Ownership shares that do not sum to 1 are refused by the request schema (HTTP 422 from pydantic), not with the coded `ownership.sum_not_one`; the `account.ownership_missing` card on Zu prüfen was not built (the Konten screen shows „Eigentümer festlegen“ instead).
- D164: the upload does not pre-select the account by the file's IBAN, and a PDF statement without a readable IBAN gets no `statement.iban_unread` warning; a file naming another account's IBAN is refused (`statement.account_mismatch`). A DKB CSV without its IBAN line would import onto the chosen account unchecked (review M1; the real export always carries it, checked when Luis uploads it).
- D161 is `_rental_accounts()` in `services/tax.py`: the draft, its sources, the checklist and phase 4's figures read rental-purpose accounts only; for the household's data (one rental account with imports) the account set is the same as before, so no figure moved.
- A new account on the form defaults to „Privatkonto“.
- Review fixes before the merge: (F1) a learned rule's amount test (D49) and (F2) the similar answers shown to the model read only lines of accounts with the line's purpose, so a private line of a payee the rental account also pays never reaches a rental prompt, whatever the switch says (D168), and is no kind of the rental rule's (the rental rule was found to book correctly either way in the database test; the query now matches the spec); (F3) a DKB line hashes without its Kundenreferenz, by its content and its position among identical lines, as D173 (3) says, so two different lines of one day, amount and customer reference are both imported.
- A gap closed by the follow-up PR #224 (merged and deployed 12 Nov 2026, images 582094db656f), before any private upload: `auto_accept_llm` is one workspace switch and is on in production (checked 12 Nov 2026), so once both owners consent, the model's answers on a private account's first import (the 2026 history) would auto-confirm, against D26 and D168 ("first month never"; D172 defines the first month as the history import). `services/matching.load_line` now drops auto-accept for a private line of its account's earliest import (`in_first_import`); later imports follow the switch; the rental account is unchanged.

**Step 2 (12 Nov 2026, PR #232).** Built as specced (D161, D162, the Haushalt screen of mockup 10, the Übersicht's Cashflow card, the two transfer cards in Zu prüfen). Where the build differs, and what the two review rounds changed:

- **The Sparquote** is (income − spending) / income, as the household design and mockup 10 define it; the „Überschuss“ is income − spending; transfers and depot trades are neither.
- **`relink` is not a separate pass.** A leg whose counterpart was booked before the deploy is linked when either is booked again, and until then `GET /transfers/open` shows it as a `transfer.proposed` card, so no pair is silently left unlinked.
- **`tests/rules/test_matching_order.py`** puts "transfer by own IBAN (D162)" before the document match, as the fixture header says.
- **D162 amended (review F3): a link the matcher wrote counts only while both legs are booked as transfers.** This is decided on read, with no new column and no migration: the row stays (append-only), and a person who re-books or rejects a leg withdraws the link by that booking. A link made by hand stays current until it is replaced.
- **Review F1:** a candidate whose own counterparty IBAN is known and is not the leg's account is never the counterpart, checked both ways. A third party's credit of the same amount is therefore never linked, and two equal transfers into one account are told apart by their IBANs. Covered by DB tests in `tests/db/test_services_transfers.py`; a fixture case in `transfers.yaml` waits for a spec amendment.
- **D161 path 1, the year paid (review F2):** a confirmed private line proving `private_cost` rows of another tax year gets the card „In <Jahr> zählen“. The move is D143's own (`tax_inputs.move_private_cost_year`, shared by both paths), confirmed by the user. Covered by a DB test; a cross-year case in `private_cost_link.yaml` waits for a spec amendment.
- **Review F4 (i):** a line whose uploaded document yields no row gets a card that says why (`property_cost_document`), with a link to the document.
- **Review F4 (ii), F6, F-A: who proposes the `private_cost` rows.**
  - A bill whose private line is booked `hh_property_cost` with it is path 2's, so the reading proposes no rows and its note names Zu prüfen.
  - A bare document hit on a private line is not enough: the reading proposes the rows itself.
  - A guard stops either path from recording the rows a second time.
- **Review F5:** the sources drawer lists the private proof lines the draft cites (masked IBAN only).
- **Known gaps, not blocking:**
  - **One private line paying two bills:** only the first bill is recorded, and the second card's button repeats the first (phase 3 Q-O54 is still undecided).
  - **One bill paid by two private lines in two years:** the year card moves every row of the bill, so it flips between the two years.
  - **A corrected re-read of a bill a private line carries** never reaches its row; edit the tax input instead.
  - **No screen to undo a transfer link by hand yet.**

**Step 2b (13 Nov 2026, PR #237).** The credit card as built, after two review rounds:

- **The reader** (`services/bank/card_pdf.py`) parses and reconciles the household's real February statement, checked on Luis's computer and never committed. A card statement uploaded as a document is stopped before the model (review F1), and importing it on the card's page clears that card. *Since 13 Nov 2026 (PR #249, images 5d3f82d7b2db, no migration):* such a file need not be uploaded again: „Auf Konto importieren“ on its document page and on its Zu prüfen card imports the stored original (hash-checked) onto the account, offering the accounts of the file's kind whose number matches it (else those with no number yet; pre-selected when one remains; „Konto anlegen“ when none), and the „couldn't read this“ card is superseded on every import of that file. The same holds for the depot's cash statement (`depot_pdf`). A card account cannot change its purpose (F4). A month without bookings is `statement.empty` (F6).
- **The settlement link:** D176 amendments (b)–(e).
- **The fixture completeness rows:** the new kind and category added one row each to `phase2/categories.yaml`, `phase2/review_routing.yaml`, `phase2/thresholds.yaml` and `phase6/household_categories.yaml`, as the completeness tests force (the step 0 precedent). No existing case changed.
- **Known gaps:**
  - **A row wrapped across a page break, and a fee word on a „Bezahlung“ row**, are not handled (review F3, F5). Neither appears in the real statement.
  - **A learned rule on the issuer's IBAN made before the card existed** still wins until the payment IBAN is confirmed; confirming it switches the rule off.
  - **The 3-banking-day window counts from the bank's debit.** If the issuer dates „Bezahlung“ before that debit, every month leaves two unlinked legs with cards. The first real pair decides it; a D162 amendment follows if so. *D162 amended (15 Nov 2026)* first let a „Bezahlung“ up to three banking days before a debit naming the card's payment IBAN be linked and changed the DB test with it; its review the same day took credit cards out of that early window again and restored the test (no real card pair needed it), so this question stands as it was. *Answered 15 Nov 2026 (D162 amended (5)):* a real pair arrived, the issuer Consors crediting a payment on 1 Jan and the Sparkasse booking the debit to the card's payment IBAN on 2 Jan, so a card's in-leg may now be up to three banking days early when the debit names that IBAN; without the tie it is never early.

**After step 2b, the first real transfers (13 Nov 2026, PR #239, images 91a5d6925d03, no migration).** Luis entered the DKB import's closing balance and used „Anfangssaldo daraus übernehmen“ (PR #235); the import reconciled, and about half a minute later the matcher linked its lines, including the pair on a transfer card his Zu prüfen page still showed. His click on „Als Übertrag buchen und verknüpfen“ then failed with a 409 („a leg of this transfer is linked already“). Nothing in the data was wrong. Two fixes:

- **D162 amended: linking a pair already linked to each other is no conflict.** `link_by_hand` returns the existing link (a second click, or the matcher linked the pair since the card was shown) and, with `rebook`, confirms a leg the model had only proposed as a transfer. A leg linked to a *different* counterpart is still a 409. Two older tests that expected the 409 for the same pair twice were changed.
- **A private account created with its IBAN re-checks the lines that name it.** `create_account` queued `rematch_waiting` only for an owner account, so the DKB account, created after the Sparkasse lines were imported, never turned the Sparkasse lines that pay it into transfers: ten waited with the model's answer, and four of their DKB mirrors had none. A one-off `rematch_waiting` for both private accounts, run after the deploy, booked and linked all twenty (10 out, 10 in, `matcher_transfer`, confirmed).
- **Still open, as review cards:** one private line paying the rental account was answered by the model before step 2 existed and waits uncategorised (its rental mirror is booked `owner_transfer_in` but unlinked); a re-check triggered by the rental account selects nothing, since only a private account's IBAN is a trigger. Two lines on a private account naming that account's own IBAN keep the model's answer.
- The two links of an unmatched transfer card („Anderes Konto importieren“, „Buchung öffnen“) had no gap between them; fixed.

**The card's starting balance (13 Nov 2026, PR #242, images 0192ee0f6120, no migration).** Luis asked how to set the card's balance when its statements do not cover the whole year. PR #235's „Anfangssaldo daraus übernehmen“ derived the opening as closing less the lines, which is right for a CSV without an opening but was refused for any file that prints one, so a card's first statement (opening mismatch against the account's 0 at its creation day) had no way forward but typing. `services/statements.derived_opening` now returns the opening and its date: for a file that states its opening, that opening and the day before its first booking, offered only on the account's earliest import, failing only on the opening (`statement.opening_mismatch` or `statement.before_anchor`), and only while nothing else is reconciled or the file ends exactly where the current opening starts (an earlier statement extending the chain backwards); the CSV path is unchanged. A later import whose opening misses the previous closing shows „Zwischen dem Auszug bis … und diesem ab … fehlt vermutlich ein Kontoauszug“ instead of an offer. Luis's second card issues no statement for a month without bookings; that needs nothing, because the chain of Alter and Neuer Saldo stays unbroken (D176 amended). Tests: `test_services_card_import.py` (the first statement's offer, the gap not bridged, an earlier statement uploaded later), `test_services_statements.py` (a file with an opening offers it, then reconciles).

**Step 4 spec amendment (13 Nov 2026, the corrected fixtures and migration 0027).** quirion's real Postbox documents were read on Luis's computer (never committed; every value in the fixtures is invented). Per document kind, against what the step 0 fixtures assumed:

- **Kontoauszug (cash statement):** not as assumed. One headline per booking with detail lines below it; only the headline is categorised; read by code (D165 (b)).
- **Wertpapierabrechnung (trade confirmation):** not as assumed. Side in the heading, Referenz-Nr, four fee lines, a sale's taxes on page 2 before the Ausmachender Betrag (D165 (c)).
- **Erträgnisabrechnung (distribution note):** not as assumed. A USD fund's euro side is separate; four-decimal per-unit amounts (D165 (c)).
- **PM-Reporting (quarterly report):** not as assumed. Umsätze signed and repeated from earlier quarters; positions include units moved between own depots (D165 (e)).
- **Ein- und Ausbuchung:** not in the step 0 fixtures; a new kind `securities_transfer` (D165 (d)).
- **Finanzreport (yearly):** as assumed; two years have the same layout (no strategy name beside each account, line breaks vary); read for title and date only.
- **Depotauszug, Kostenausweis, Steuerbescheinigung, Rebalancing notice, interest-rate letter:** typed and stored with title and date (D131); the tax certificate's figures wait for phase 8 (D170).
- **Migration 0027:** a depot's IBAN (D156 amended), `statement_import.format = depot_pdf`, `allocation.method = matcher_depot`, `securities_transfer` for the document subtype, the extraction and proposal kinds and `asset_movement.source`; the downgrade is refused while such a row exists.
- **Not seen in a real document yet:** the cash texts for a deposit, a payout, a distribution and the Vorabpauschale; whether a trade's reference on the confirmation and on the cash line is the same number (the documents show references of two lengths). Both are checked on the first real import of step 4.

**Step 4 as built (13 Nov 2026, PR #244, images 74ab1bbf3b42, migration 0027 applied).** The depot by its documents, as D165 amended describes, plus D177's plain-words box on the document page and the suggestion card. Migration 0027 also gained, from the review, `holding_statement.superseded_by` and `document.depot_kind`. Review (one round, then a second pass on the fixes only):

- **Fixed:** a distribution note applied twice is refused by account, fund, day and amount when it carries no reference (`depot.movement_exists`); a fund a report leaves out is 0 on read and never a stored row; a report read again with other figures supersedes its position (`account.holding_corrected`), and identical figures from a second copy are refused (`depot.holding_exists`), while „Neu auslesen“ of the same document keeps its unchanged positions; a position with units and a value of 0 or less stores no price; a cost overview that mentions trade notes is no longer typed as one.
- **Recorded, not fixed:** a trade's cash line is not linked when the cash statement arrives after the trade's confirmation; units moved between two own depots get no `transfer_link`; the report-only movement of D165 (3) is not built (built since D165 amended (15 Nov 2026): a quarterly report books the trades its Umsätze list names); a statement price from a reading later corrected stays in `price` (append-only, no supersede column), hidden on the ETF tab by its time but to be settled before the prices step reads `price` by date; two first applies of one position from two different uploads at the same moment could both store (no constraint; not realistic for one household); a report date read wrongly cannot be withdrawn, since a correction is of the same date only.
- **Not verified:** the explanation box was checked by type and lint only, not seen in a browser before the deploy.
- **Asked after the deploy (Luis: "Why not just link it directly from the Dokumente card?"):** a depot cash statement uploaded under Dokumente was refused by the reading with a card telling the user to upload it again on the depot's page. `POST /api/documents/{id}/import-statement` and the button „Auf Konto importieren“ (document page and Zu prüfen) import the stored file instead; a second call returns the existing import (200); an account of the wrong kind or another number is refused with `statement.account_mismatch` (13 Nov 2026, PR #249, images 5d3f82d7b2db, no migration).
- **Missed, fixed after the deploy:** the account form „Konto anlegen“ still offered only the bank kinds and the credit card, so a depot could not be created in the app, while the depot suggestion's note sent the user there under a button name that does not exist („Konto hinzufügen“). The form now offers „Depot“ (always private, IBAN optional, hints for the cash statement's opening) and the note names „Konto anlegen“ (13 Nov 2026, PR #246, images 021bd0e15213, no migration).

**Step 5b as built (13 Nov 2026, PR #254, images 507515e4fff8, no migration).** Prices as D167 and D174 describe, after one review round.

- **The adapters, read 13 Nov 2026:** SPDR's NAV history is an xlsx per fund (`navhist-emea-en-<slug>.xlsx` on ssga.com) with **one** NAV column in the currency the file names (step 0's three currency columns did not hold for the real file); iShares is read from BlackRock's product-data API per product id, German-formatted numbers with two decimals, and a value that is not German beyond doubt (`58.82`, `1.002`) is refused rather than guessed, so a file in another format fails as a whole and stores nothing; a response is capped at 10 MB. The household's held funds are covered by these two adapters (checked by count only; no ISIN is written here), so no Vanguard adapter is needed. *(Stale, corrected 16 Nov 2026: the live data of 15 Nov 2026 held 32 fund ISINs, of which 5 are in the registry, so most held funds had no issuer source; the sentence had led to no further issuer being looked at. D174 amended 16 Nov 2026 adds discovery by ISIN for iShares and SPDR; the other issuers are in Q-O74.)* The fund reference per ISIN (slug or product id) is code data today; a SaaS workspace would need it per workspace, a migration follow-up.
- **CoinGecko and Kraken:** CoinGecko's Demo key goes in the request header only, never the URL or a log; HTTP redirects are not followed, so the key is never re-sent to another host; the Demo tier reaches 365 days back. Kraken needs no key and serves the 720 latest daily closes. The ECB backfill reads the 90-day history only, so a day further back takes no ECB rate.
- **Cards:** `price_source_failed` (a source failed, counted by failed runs) and `price_no_source` („Kursquelle wählen“) in Zu prüfen; `AssetPatch` refuses a malformed CoinGecko id or Kraken pair (422).
- **Review fixes:** the depot guard on read (D167 amended (b)), iShares numbers fail closed, no redirects, the patterns, XML parsers with `huge_tree=False`, and a typed price wins its day (D167 amended (a)).
- **Recorded, not fixed:** a source failing for more than 32 days writes its failure event again; the failure card counts failed runs, not failed days; the ECB's 90 days; the 10 MB cap.
- **Not verified before the deploy:** the first real 07:00 run; the next morning's run is the check.

**Step 5a as built (13 Nov 2026, PR #256, images b2aca2d75fe8, migration 0028 applied).** The exchanges by their exports, as D166 amended and D178 describe, after one review round. Migration 0028 adds `binance_csv` to `movement_import.format` and refuses its downgrade while a Binance import exists.

- **Readings** (from the real exports on Luis's computer, never committed; the fixture headers of `bison_csv.yaml` and `binance_csv.yaml` list them in full): Binance's EUR legs rounded to the cent per trade (D178 (6) amended); `rows_new + rows_skipped = rows_total` for both exchanges; the dedup hash takes amounts without trailing zeros; a crypto Deposit on Binance may come from a foreign address and stays D166's proposed reward, with an own wallet among the answers. BISON's Fee is in the row's own asset and was zero on every real row, so Q-O80 stays open. Neither export's file name carries the User ID (checked 13 Nov 2026).
- **Never to the model:** an exchange export uploaded under Dokumente stops in the reading job before any model call (`proposal.exchange_export_not_read`), and „Neu auslesen“ refuses an imported original, the stored withdraw history and a document typed `statement` / `exchange_export`; the guard counts only movements on accounts of kind `exchange`, so a depot note can still be read again (a regression found after the rebase onto 5b and fixed before the merge).
- **Review fixes:** a Transaction ID repeated inside one BISON file is kept once (it was a 500); a positive fee row is refused as `exchange.unreadable` (it was a 500); a withdraw history that completes nothing says why (`exchange.withdrawal_history_no_match`, `ExchangeUpload.withdrawals_reason`, the event `account.withdrawal_history_checked` with counts only, and the to-do „Auszahlung nicht im Auszahlungsverlauf gefunden“ while the last history check is at or after every open withdrawal's import).
- **Recorded, not fixed:** a withdraw history row is paired with the nearest debit, not the oldest (Q-O81); an import's „abgeglichen“ is the verdict stored when its balance was typed, so a later export adding rows dated before that import's end leaves it shown reconciled until re-typed; a not-yet-imported export can be queued for reading again, and the job stops it before the model.

**„Konto anlegen und einlesen“ as built (13 Nov 2026, PR #258, images df408ec4c68c, no migration).** Luis: "If I upload a statement and there's no account for it could the app suggest to create an account?" `POST /api/documents/{id}/statement-account` creates the account from the stored file and imports it in one transaction (a failed import leaves no account; two clicks at once give one account and a 409, by the unique IBAN and number constraints); the body carries label, bank, provider, purpose and shares only (`extra = forbid`). The suggestions for bank and provider come from the reader (Sparkasse, DKB, quirion); an account from a bank export is always `bank_current`. The suggestion (`StatementFileOffer.new_account`) shows the kind, the masked identifier and the starting balance with its date, and offers no account for a file with no booked line. Shared `components/ownershipShares.tsx` for the owner shares. Also in this PR: a line with no counterparty (the bank's own lines, the card's payment and interest) is labelled by its booking text in Zu prüfen and the allocation panel instead of „Ohne Gegenseite“.

- **Review (one round):** atomicity, uniqueness, identity, workspace isolation and the model stop confirmed by test and a two-thread probe. Fixed: the starting balance the button saves was not shown in the suggestion (D132); an empty export offered a button that could only fail; the event order is now a test.
- **Recorded, not fixed:** the stop before the model trusts the upload's declared media type, so a bank file sent by hand as an image would still reach the classifier (only by a member forging their own upload; no regression); with the AI switched off a bank export fails as `ai.disabled` and gets no offer.

**D159 amended (13 Nov 2026, PR #263, merged and deployed, images 1b26e89791f2, migration 0029 applied).** The re-ask was run the same day from the deployed commit: 297 lines queued, 4 left alone as possible purchase costs, none already asked. Fifteen finer household codes and „Erstattung“, „Alle N bestätigen“ per category and the one-off `household-reask`, as D159 amended describes; migration 0029. Review (one round): the code was found safe (the bulk confirm takes only the shown, still-current lines under their locks, refuses rental lines and other workspaces, and writes the single-confirm event; the re-ask never takes a confirmed line; no new code reaches a tax rule); one fix: a retried re-ask job had skipped as „asked already“ after its own first attempt's model call, losing the answer, and now asks again. The review also noted that the decision lived only in code comments and fixtures until this row was written; it is written in the same PR. Known and left: (a) during the re-ask a learned rule or matcher may still confirm a line, as in any re-match (D45/D49); only the model's answer is proposal-only. (b) `record_proposed` dedupes a privately paid cost only against the same document's `private_cost` rows, not against a Nebenkosten row with the same Beleg or a hand-typed private cost, so the re-ask leaves possible purchase costs alone. (c) A possible-duplicate pair locks its two lines in pair order and the bulk confirm in id order; the inbox never lists the later line of an open pair, so only a hand-made request could make them wait on each other, and Postgres would end one with a deadlock error.

**D160 amended (14 Nov 2026).** The shared IBAN, the bulk list with tick boxes and plain decimals, as D160 amended describes. Review (one round): safe to ship; fixes taken: the threshold lowered from three payees to two (see the row), and the plain-decimal serializer skips None so a patch's unset field still dumps as null. Known and left: (a) an IBAN-only rule whose IBAN turns shared stays enabled and silent, with no card saying it stopped; re-answering the payee adds an IBAN-and-name rule beside it. (b) The rental prompt's similar answers and the cross-format duplicate check still compare by IBAN alone (they also compare amount and date, and rental lines carry no card clearing IBAN).

**D160 amended (2) (14 Nov 2026).** Re-booking with the rule and „Empfänger suchen“, as the row describes (merged and deployed 14 Nov 2026, PR #275, images 9dcfccdb81f6). Review (one round): safe with fixes; taken: a blocked re-book had been refused with the same `rule.conflicts` as before, a dead end, so the GET now names each blocking payment and the PUT refuses with its own `rule.rebook_blocked`; only lines that can be re-booked are locked. Known and left: (a) two concurrent re-books of one payee may deadlock, and the aborted request writes nothing (one household); (b) a rule with a different matcher (an IBAN-only rule, a typed pattern) whose bookings were re-booked stays enabled, as under D49, and disagrees with the new rule into review rather than mis-booking; (c) under a search, a bulk group that shrinks to one line leaves „Alle N bestätigen“ (that line stays in the list). Follow-up the same day: the rule's background re-check of the waiting lines takes about a second per line (a payee's 39 lines in 27 s), past the page's reloads at 3 and 10 s, so its group still showed under „Alle N bestätigen“ until a manual reload; Zu prüfen now reloads for two minutes after a rule is learned and says so (merged and deployed 14 Nov 2026, PR #277, images f3aed7a033f6, frontend only). A second follow-up the same day: the D49 question under „Künftig immer so zuordnen“ listed at most five other amounts, so a payee whose 112 waiting grocery lines were nearly all different amounts showed five amounts of 1× and looked as if the rule would cover only those; the payment panel now gets the uncapped totals (`waiting_other_count`, `waiting_same_amount_count`, no migration) and says how many waiting lines each answer's rule covers, and the hint says so when there is no question (merged and deployed 14 Nov 2026, PR #279, images 0eda493b9fda, no migration). Speed, the same day (Luis: the app was slow between tabs). On the 0.25 vCPU container GET /inbox took 2.6–4 s and ran on every route change for the sidebar count, and a rule's re-check took about 0.7 s per line. Fixed without changing any list's content: the sidebar refetches only when its count is older than 30 s; the after-rule checks fetch the list alone and stop once it is unchanged; the document evidence for matching is read in one batch (`evidence_of_many`); the transfer cards and the after-booking transfer hook read only lines with a live transfer row. On a seeded workspace this took inbox() from ~240 to ~150 ms CPU, the payment panel from up to 580 to 150 ms, and a rule's re-check from ~230 to ~90 ms CPU per line. `inbox()` logs `inbox_sections` (each section's ms and count, no data) to find production's slow section. One review round: safe, no findings. Open: load the documents and tenancies once per re-check run, the reminders section, and `/api/reminders` computed twice per page load. More CPU was offered (about €9 or €27 a month) and deferred by Luis until after these fixes are measured (merged and deployed 14 Nov 2026, PR #281, images 0fba583ad7e6, no migration). Its `inbox_sections` log line found production's slow part the same hour: the transfer cards took 1.6–1.9 s of each GET /inbox's ~2 s (38 open cards, all „Gegenbuchung fehlt“), because each card read two months of every account's lines once for itself and again for the counterpart's check. `open_transfers` now reads that span once per call and takes each card's window from memory, with each account's own IBANs kept per call; on the live data, read-only, the 38 cards came out identical and `open_transfers` took 0.8 s instead of 7.3 s from the operator's computer (merged and deployed 14 Nov 2026, PR #283, images 882902719ed1, no migration).

**D178 amended (14 Nov 2026).** „Differenz als Korrektur buchen“ as the row describes (migration 0030). Review (one round): safe with fixes; taken: the command locks the account row as well as the import, so an upload in the same instant cannot change the sum under its stale and newest-import checks (verified by reading only; no test runs the two at once). Known and left: (a) `declare_holdings` itself (a typing racing an upload) still locks only the import, as before this change. (b) Two imports with the same last movement both count as the newest; a correction on one is refused as stale on the other, and the other's stored badge stays until it is typed again. (c) The account page's line under the badge still says Hausbuch never bridges by itself, which stays true, without naming the button beside it.

**D161 amended (14 Nov 2026, PR #287).** As the row describes. Review (one round): no Anlage V figure changes, no confirmed booking touched, workspace isolation holds for the CLI. Fixed: the card matched Nebenkosten rows to a bill's parts by amount only, so a recorded purchase part could cancel an equal loan-fee part (a Grundbuch bill with equal Eigentümer and Grundschuld fees) and the fee was never offered. Recorded, not fixed:

- **A second whole-amount proposal through the plain document match:** when the open-amount bound refuses a second line, the older document match (±10 days, the reading's total) can still propose it; a person confirming both books two lines as one bill's payment. No tax figure changes (proofs are never summed). Pre-existing; skipping the document match for a recorded bill would lose the only path for a bill whose rows disagree with its reading.
- **Not re-offered:** a line the bound refused is not re-proposed when its sibling is re-booked, until a Nebenkosten change or `rematch-bills`; it can be booked by hand meanwhile.
- **A bill booked whole with a partial Nebenkosten row** proposes nothing for the remainder (whether code should propose total − rows is undecided).
- **The seller's IBAN also a bill's payee** (a notary's Anderkonto taking both the price and its fee): the down payment proposal wins; not seen in the real data.
- **A bill partly paid from the rental account** is not offered to a private line; D143's „Vorhandenen Nachweis zuordnen“ remains.
- **A learned payee rule wins first** (D158 order), so a private line a rule already books is not re-proposed by `rematch-bills`.

**Statements typed by code when stopped (14 Nov 2026, PR #288, no migration).** A statement the read job stops before the model because code reads it on its account (a bank export, a card statement, a depot's cash statement; proposal.bank_file_not_read) is now typed by code at that moment: `statement`, with subtype `depot_statement` for a depot's cash statement, only when the document is untyped (a person's typing stays). Before, it stayed untyped until „Auf Konto importieren“ or „Konto anlegen und einlesen“ imported it, so Dokumente showed no type and only the account button. A document stopped before this change keeps no type until it is imported or read again („Erneut auslesen“).

**D159 amended (2) (14 Nov 2026, PR #289, migration 0032).** As the row describes. Review (one round): migration 0032, tax isolation (every new code is private-only and `null` in the rule sets), the D49 replacement and the dry run confirmed. Fixed before the merge: a learned rule's near-miss guess (D49, confidence 0.5, chosen by amount) could supersede a person's confirmed answer without asking the model; a re-run re-proposed a rule's answer over a person who had kept the fallback; the command's wording on model calls. Accepted: a re-proposed confirmed line leaves the Haushalt totals until it is confirmed. Recorded: a later edit of the household prompt makes `household-reask` re-take lines asked before (as on 13 Nov); the re-sort's once-per-line rule does not depend on it.

**Step 6 as built (15 Nov 2026, migration 0040; reviewed 15 Nov 2026, one round, its fixes applied; merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026 (PR #364, images aab24238a9b4, migration 0040 applied); checked the same day on the live data: the figure is computed for both owners with no account or property unassigned, no month-end stored yet (31 Oct offered with „Monatsabschluss festhalten“, the first automatic run at 08:00 UTC on 1 Dec 2026), 29 fund positions marked stale because their only prices are the depots' 30 Sep 2026 reports (no issuer adapter covers those funds yet), and five balances older than 7 days; to be checked against each account's own source once a month-end is stored (Luis dropped the spreadsheet comparison on 17 Nov 2026)).** NetWorth(on) on read (`services/net_worth.py`, pure parts in `rules/net_worth.py`), the `month_end` periodic task, „Monatsabschluss festhalten“, `GET /accounts/auto-rate` and the Übersicht's Nettovermögen card and Vermögensaufteilung bar (mockup 07). What it computes: a bank account's balance is its anchor plus its lines (duplicates left out) up to the last day its newest reconciled import covers, never past the day asked; lines after that day are a to-do (they count once a statement covers them) linking Zu prüfen, and a balance more than 7 days older than the day is marked stale. A depot's cash and a credit card's balance (negative, class `liabilities`) count only when the account has statements or a nonzero opening balance. Positions come from the Anlagen overview's valuation (`assets_overview`), classed by the account's kind. A property counts from its handover until its ownership ended, at the default scenario's „Wert heute“ or else its purchase price, marked as an estimate; each loan at its schedule's balance (with the scenario's follow-up rate), also an estimate. Owner shares are those in force on the day (the newest recorded set effective by then); an account or property without shares counts in the total and in no owner, and is a to-do with its button. Where the build differs from the spec:

- **A stored month-end holds the property and its loans too: migration `0040_valuation_snapshot_scope`** (D167 (5) amended (d)). As first built the run held the accounts only, since 0025's `account_id` was required, and a stored month-end recomputed the property's estimate and its loans on read, so a later „Wert heute“ or loan change moved a past total. The orchestrator chose the migration (15 Nov 2026): `account_id` nullable, `property_id` and `loan_id` added with workspace-scoped foreign keys, a CHECK that exactly one of the three is set and one that an asset sits only on an account's row, and 0025's unique key widened to all three (else a run could hold only one property row). `forbid_mutation`, the no-truncate trigger, row-level security and the grants are unchanged. The downgrade is **refused** while a property or loan row exists (account rows are kept). A run now stores a row per property (its estimate) and per loan (its balance, negative), and a stored month-end is read from its rows alone; only labels and the owner split (the account shares in force on the month-end, the property's current shares) are looked up; the property's shares carry no date, so a later change of them would change a stored month-end's owner split, never its total. Tested: after a capture the flat's „Wert heute“ and value growth and the loan's first payment and rate change; today's figure follows, the stored month-end's total, classes and owner split do not. Its `down_revision` is `0039_bank_credential` (the bank PIN in the app, PR #353, merged and deployed 15 Nov 2026); the branch merged `main` with it the same day, so it has one alembic head.
- **Runs, not overwrites.** The table is append-only (SELECT and INSERT for the app role, a mutation trigger), so every capture writes a new `net_worth_run_id` and the newest run of a month-end wins on read. The task (08:00 UTC on the 1st, the last month-end in Berlin time) and the button both write a new run only when the rows (account, asset, property, loan, units, price, source, value) differ from the stored run, answering `written`, `recaptured`, `unchanged` or `empty`. **Reversed by the review (15 Nov 2026, D167 amended (e)):** the task first wrote only when the month-end had no run yet, and the card invited pressing the button before the 1st, so an early press froze a figure without the month's last lines and prices and the 1st's run then kept it. Now the 1st's run stores it again when it differs, and the card says the 1st's run stores it after the morning fetches instead of inviting an early press. The Übersicht compares the stored last month-end with today's reading of that same day (`month_end_now` in `GET /net-worth/overview`) and, when they differ, says so and offers „Monatsabschluss neu speichern“; a Planung change of „Wert heute“ or of a loan also shows that difference, since the property's estimate does not depend on the day. Each write records `workspace.valuation_snapshot_written` with the run id, the day and the row count; captures take a per-workspace advisory lock.
- **The task runs at 08:00 UTC on the 1st, after the day's bank fetch (06:30 UTC) and price fetch (07:00 UTC),** (since 23 Nov 2026 the morning jobs run as a chain: wallets 06:30 UTC with one retry of a failed address, then prices no earlier than 07:00, then the month-end once the prices and the morning's bank runs have ended, then the key figures and the warm step; the clock times stay as deadlines, prices by 07:30, the month-end at 08:00 and the key figures at 08:30 running once more as a second pass, `jobs/morning.py`) so the month's last lines and the last day's prices are in the run (built at 05:00 first; moved by the orchestrator's decision, 15 Nov 2026). A statement or price that arrives later still is stored by the button as a new run.
- **A depot's cash is added beside the positions,** since the Anlagen overview leaves it out (a depot's cash is no movement).
- **API:** `GET /valuations?on=` (§12) is not built: `GET /net-worth?on=` returns every valuation line by line with its source. Added: `GET /net-worth/overview` (the Übersicht card: today, the latest stored month-end, the month-end still to store) and `GET /net-worth/snapshots/{run_id}` (one stored run). The `net_worth` routes are included before `accounts`, so `/accounts/auto-rate` is not taken by `/accounts/{account_id}`.
- **The auto-rate reads D172's "first imported month" as each account's first import** (the orchestrator's decision, 15 Nov 2026): the 2026 history is one import spanning many months, and D26's rule that the model never auto-confirms applies to the first import, so the criterion counts every confirmed allocation outside each account's first import (the earliest by creation). The response also gives the totals with the first import included.
- **Schemas:** the step 0 placeholder `household.NetWorth` (never used) was replaced by `schemas/net_worth.py`; `rules/performance/projection.value_today` was added for the property's estimate.
- **The screen:** the Vermögensaufteilung bar shows the property net of its loans, the depot, bank and crypto; a credit card's debt (the class `liabilities` less the loans) is listed beside it without a bar segment, and the percentages are of the positive parts. The card lists each estimate with its source and a link to Planung, and every to-do with its link.
- **Review fixes (15 Nov 2026, one round):**
  - **A fund the depot's latest report lists with no unit booked is listed, unvalued.** The Anlagen overview values only booked movements, so such a fund was missing from the net worth without a word. It is now a position line with no value (the report's units named in its note, never valued from them), counted in `unvalued_positions`, with the to-do `net_worth.position_not_booked` („Zu prüfen öffnen“: the „Depotbestand“ card's „Umsätze aus dem Bericht buchen“ books it). The check reuses the depot's own report check (`depot.latest_report_checks`), read as of the day asked; the Anlagen screen is unchanged.
  - **An outdated balance is a to-do.** A bank, card or depot cash balance whose last covered day is more than 7 days before the day asked (measured to the newer of the newest reconciled statement and the account's history, which includes a bank fetch's requested end) gets `net_worth.balance_outdated` with its button: „Konto öffnen“ for an account with a bank connection, else „Kontoauszug hochladen“ (a card's „Kreditkartenabrechnung hochladen“). The card lists each balance older than the day with its „Stand vom“, and „Monatsabschluss festhalten“'s answer names how many positions it stored without a value. A quiet account whose last statement had no lines may still read as outdated.
  - **Owner shares before the first recorded set.** A day before an account's first ownership set now uses that earliest set (it had used none, so a month-end before the shares were typed counted the account in no owner).
  - **The auto-rate's ratios are decimal strings** (`ratio`, `criterion_ratio`, `target`, 4 places), like every other figure of the API.
  - **The auto-rate counts only what no person confirmed** (D172 amended 15 Nov 2026).
- **Still open:** the first month-end checked against each account's own source (the step's done criterion; it said „against Luis's spreadsheet“ until 17 Nov 2026, when Luis dropped the spreadsheet as more work and more error than it would catch).

**Step 7, closing: first reading (16 Nov 2026, read-only on the live data, by the app's own functions `services.auto_rate.auto_rate` and `services.transfers.open_transfers` inside a transaction that was rolled back).** Two of the four measurements pass, two cannot be read yet:

- **Every account with an owner share: met.** All 11 accounts (four bank accounts, two cards, three depots, two exchanges) have live owner rows that add up to 1.
- **Every internal transfer linked: met today.** `open_transfers` returns no leg, so no „Gegenbuchung fehlt“ card waits. To be read again at the close, since a new month brings new legs.
- **≥ 80 % booked without a person in the second month: not measurable yet.** Every private bank line in the app so far belongs to its account's first import (the 2026 history, imported 12–15 Nov 2026), which D172 leaves out of the criterion, so `criterion_ratio` is empty for every month. The criterion is read on **December 2026**, in early January 2027, once December's lines arrive by the daily fetch (all three private logins have „Täglich automatisch abrufen“ on; the DKB login's last run was refused by the bank and needs Luis's retry) or by upload (the account fed by uploads). As an indication only: inside the history, the share booked without a person per month ran from about 46 % to 86 % per account (September 76 %, October 63 %, November so far 47 % over the three accounts); the history never lets the model book alone (D26), while later imports do when its answer is confident, since `auto_accept_llm` is on.
- **One month-end net worth whose every account agrees with its own source: waits for the first month-end** (until 17 Nov 2026 this waited for Luis's spreadsheet, which he dropped the same day). No month-end is stored yet (the first is written on 1 Dec 2026 at 08:00 UTC, or with „Monatsabschluss festhalten“); the funds are priced by their 30 Sep reports until the issuers' daily prices are fixed (the fix that stores the issuers' prices merged and deployed 16 Nov 2026, PRs #381 and #391; this said „being built“ until 17 Nov 2026).

**D174 amended 19 Nov 2026: more issuers, the ECB history, the first-read check (spec merged 19 Nov 2026 in PR #451; built, reviewed (ship after fixes, all applied, D174 (h)–(k)) and merged and deployed 19 Nov 2026 in PR #452, images 25027af14f41, no migration; this said „not merged, not deployed“ until then; the first daily run with the new adapters is still to be read; a fix of the same evening, before that run: the price write's ON CONFLICT predicate reached Postgres as bind parameters, so once psycopg prepared the statement (after five executions) the eleventh 500-row chunk of one write failed with „no unique or exclusion constraint matching the ON CONFLICT specification“ and rolled back the whole run, storing no price and no event, every day again; it is now SQL text, with a test of twelve chunks on one connection (found by the D264 review, merged and deployed 19 Nov 2026 before that run, PR #458, images e3516554b93b, no migration)).** The fixture contract: issuer_nav.yaml's `read_cases` (one adapter's read of one fund, every request answered by a recorded sample by a part of its URL), `catalogue_cases` (ARK's fund list), `discovery_cases` (`Sources.discover`, with the requests it costs), `ecb_history_cases` (the history ZIP) and `guard_cases` (the first-read check), replayed by `tests/test_prices_issuers.py`, which skips a case whose adapter is not built yet so the spec branch stays green. The samples were built by a script from scratch in the real layouts with invented ISINs, names and values; the real files read on 19 Nov 2026 stayed in the dev box's scratch directory. The live verification of each adapter is recorded below.

The live verification ran on 19 Nov 2026 from the dev box. Each adapter was run through the app's own code (`Sources.discover`, then `Sources.nav`) and its own client and user agent, one held fund at a time. Only the newest day, the currency and the row count were printed; no figure was written down. Every held fund whose name carries a new adapter's brand was found and read, each with a newest NAV of 18 Nov 2026.
- Amundi: `POST https://www.amundietf.de/mapi/ProductAPI/getProductsData`, asked by ISIN.
- Xtrackers: `GET https://etf.deutsche-am.com/api/pdp/de-de/etf/<ISIN>/historicaldata/download?startDate=<since, else 2000-01-01>&endDate=<today>&includeTax=false`.
- Invesco: `GET https://dng-api.invesco.com/cache/v1/accounts/de_DE/shareclasses/<ISIN>?expand=nav&idType=isin`, then `…/<ISIN>/performance?idType=isin`.
- BNP Paribas: `GET https://api.bnpparibas-am.com/push-raw/navs/fast?currency=<CUR>&product_gids=<ISIN>&granularity=daily`.
- L&G: `GET https://fundcentres.landg.com/srp/api/fund-centre/48?audience=150&language=3`, read once a run. It gives each fund's latest NAV only, one row.
- ARK Invest: the fund list `GET https://europe.ark-funds.com/funds/`, then `GET https://europe.ark-funds.com/funds/<section>/<slug>/`. The first live read refused the held funds' pages as unreadable: every real page also names the ISIN of its intraday indicative value („iNAV ISIN“), and one of them prints „NAV per share“ without its currency. The reader now ignores the iNAV ISIN and reads a currency-less NAV in the base currency (the read case `ark_live_layout`, with its own sample). The second read priced them.
- HANetf: `GET https://hanetf.com/fund-sitemap.xml`, read once a run, then `GET https://hanetf.com/fund/<slug>/` for at most three pages whose slug matches the fund's name, then the NAV history workbook the page links. It answered the app's own client with no browser user agent.
- The ECB history: `GET https://www.ecb.europa.eu/stats/eurofxref/eurofxref-hist.zip` gave 41 currencies, each with rates from 4 Jan 1999 to 19 Nov 2026.

**D264, Lang & Schwarz exchange prices (19 Nov 2026; reviewed (ship after fixes, all applied: a near-real fund name removed from the fixtures before any push, the price write fixed separately in PR #458, the no-rollback note, a past point off midnight refused) and merged and deployed 19 Nov 2026 in PR #460, images ec4bdafd2c2a, migration 0049 applied before the image; this said „not merged, not deployed“ until then; the first daily run with it is still to be read).** The contract is `exchange_price.yaml`; migration 0049 (`0049_exchange_close_price`) extends the two price-source CHECKs and `price_daily_key`. The URL patterns are §3's „Lang & Schwarz exchange prices“ row. *Live check, 19 Nov 2026,* through `lang_schwarz.instrument` and `lang_schwarz.history` with the app's own client and user agent (14 requests, a search and a history read per fund; no ISIN, name, id or price recorded here): seven held funds, the three with no issuer source and the four Legal & General funds. Each search found exactly one listing, and each history read gave EUR prices with the newest day 18 Nov 2026 (the run's day dropped). Each history reaches back before 1 Jan 2026 (the earliest to 2015, the latest to December 2025), between about 240 and 2,800 weekdays per fund. In every answer each past point stood at 00:00 UTC, no price was zero or negative and no past day appeared twice; the older years lack some weekend points, which the reader drops anyway. So each of 2026's report days has an exchange price for all seven. *Deploy order:* migration 0049 must be applied before the image that carries this code starts. Its price insert names the five daily sources in `ON CONFLICT … WHERE`, which matches only the rebuilt `price_daily_key`; against the old index every price insert of the daily run would fail, not only the exchange's. *No rollback once an exchange price is stored:* this image cannot be rolled back after its first daily run stores an `exchange_close` price (like PR #353's image, which sealed the bank logins at its first start). An earlier image does not know the source: its `PriceSourceKind` raises on such a row wherever prices are read, so Anlagen, the net worth and the month-end would fail, and 0049's downgrade refuses while such a row exists. Roll back only to an image of this change or later.

Steps 1 and 3 are built in parallel worktrees (Luis, 12 Nov 2026: "Why not do step 3 in step 1 if we have the foundation already?"): step 0 already holds `credential_slot`, and their paths are disjoint (step 1: accounts, statements, matching, review, the taxonomy, the DKB reader, the Konten and Zu prüfen screens; step 3: `bank_sync`, the FinTS facade, `Settings.fints_pins`, the Bankabruf card); what stays in order is Luis's: the 2026 export first, then the connection. Step 3 therefore refuses a FinTS connection on a private account without a reconciled import (`bank.history_missing`, „Laden Sie zuerst den Kontoauszug-Export für 2026 hoch“), so the first window always starts after the history (amends D173 (4), which only moved the window). Steps 1 and 2 meet three of the four parts of the criterion on bank accounts alone; 4 and 5 are independent of each other and can run in parallel worktrees (disjoint paths: `services/depot.py`, `rules/depot.py`, `schemas/extraction.py` additions vs `services/exchange/`, `services/prices/`, `rules/exchange.py`); 6 needs both.

## 16. Edits to other docs this spec requires (made by the orchestrator in the spec PR)

*Made in the spec PR (12 Nov 2026): ARCHITECTURE.md's Bank and Market-and-chain-data Stack rows, the phase 6 delivery row and the phases note, the Decisions row „Phase 6 spec“; CLAUDE.md's phase list; phase 5's Q-O43 answered with D120's amendment noted; a pointer at the top of the household design. The rest below describes behaviour that does not exist yet (domain-model rows, the AI rows, Security and Privacy bullets, the Retention row, phases 2, 3 and 4b, the runbook step 17, the mockups' README) and is made in the step that builds it, so no doc claims an unbuilt feature.*

the household's private accounts, the depot and the exchange are accounts of other kinds with owner shares, phase 6 D156, D157"); the **Allocation** row (the household taxonomy and `TaxTreatment.PRIVATE`); State Street's XLSX verified 12 Nov 2026, the others written from the real page when a held fund needs them; the depot's documents as the fallback), CoinGecko Demo history limited to 365 days, Kraken 720 daily candles, ECB daily XML, all verified 12 Nov 2026; ## 17. Luis's decisions to take (before step 0)

Recommendation first, then why; only what changes the design.

1. **Which accounts, and under which login.** *Decided 12 Nov 2026 (Luis).* Three current accounts at three different institutes, each with its own online-banking login: one joint account of A and B and one sole account per owner; no savings account. The institutes are of the two kinds the app supports from this phase, Sparkassen (two, with different BLZ and FinTS servers) and DKB; the rental account's existing connection is one of the Sparkasse logins (if the rental account's Sparkasse is not the one of the owner whose login it uses, the private account there is one more connection, nothing else changes). Which account is at which institute, and under whose login, is recorded in the app only, never in this file. What it changed: D163 (named credential slots instead of two fixed ones), D173 (DKB: its decoupled approval, its CSV export, the camt question, one source per account), the Out list (no savings kind in use, no second-bank exclusion), step 3 and the fixtures `dkb_csv.yaml` and `fints_slots.yaml`. The recommendation had assumed one Sparkasse for everything and two slots.
2. **Consent to send private-account lines and the depot's documents to the model** (`ai_private_accounts`, D168). *Decided 12 Nov 2026 (Luis): yes for both, the private bank lines and the depot's documents; both owners agree.* *Amended the same evening (D168): the switching member's consent is enough, since Luis's personal account holds only his data and the joint account only household expenses fed by transfers.* Owner B's own click is still recorded in the app as the second consent event (D168 as designed), because the record must come from the person it concerns, not from a message relayed by the other owner. The reasoning stands: a month of ~100 lines in review without a model is tedious and the minimisation is the rental account's; the joint account's lines name owner B's employer and purchases, and some lines reveal health or religion by their counterparty.
3. **History start.** *As recommended (Luis accepted the default, 12 Nov 2026).* 1 Jan 2026 for the bank accounts (as agreed 6 Nov 2026); for the depot **every confirmation since opening** (a position bought before 2026 reconciles only with its confirmation stored); for the exchange the full export (it is one file; Luis provides it).
4. **ETF prices from documents only, or a price feed.** *Decided 12 Nov 2026 (Luis): not documents only.* "These are standard funds. You can fetch their prices at the daily close from the funds' issuer." → D174: an `issuer_nav` price source, one adapter per issuer keyed by ISIN through the registry, the day's NAV the official price above a report's value and below the trade's own price on the trade day (D167's precedence became date-first); State Street's file verified, the other issuers' pages written from the real page when a held fund needs them, Vanguard's terms accepted by Luis for one daily request for own use (Q-O73, 12 Nov 2026), the documents the fallback for any issuer without a usable page (Q-O74). The recommendation had found no free official source because it had looked for one feed across issuers; per issuer, the issuers do publish it.
5. **Sparer-Pauschbetrag, Vorabpauschale and Freistellungsauftrag now or in phase 8.** *As recommended (accepted 12 Nov 2026).* Phase 8 (D170), with the two cards' places on the ETF tab saying so; computing them right needs the Basiszins per year and the fund class as rule data, which is phase 8's spec.
6. **Any loan besides the property's.** *Decided 12 Nov 2026 (Luis): none.* The net worth subtracts the property loans only (D167 as designed); a later consumer loan would become a `loan` without a property (0001's `loan.property_id NOT NULL` relaxed) by a spec amendment.
7. **Sidebar regrouping.** *As recommended (accepted 12 Nov 2026).* Add `Anlagen` and `Haushalt` now and leave `Eigentümer`, `Mieter`, `Steuern`, `Planung`, `Dokumente` where they are; the design's `Immobilien` group (§1) can wait until phase 7 adds `Krypto` and the sidebar gets long.
8. **The exchange's crypto "Deposit" lines.** *As recommended (accepted 12 Nov 2026), with Luis's note on purchase dates recorded in D175: "the purchase dates can come from the blockchain and from the exchange … A deposit into the wallet doesn't necessarily mean the purchase date".* Every one proposed as a bonus (`cashback_reward`) for review, never auto (D166), because the export could not be read today and the design's "bonuses come as Deposit" is a reading from 6 Nov 2026. If you know a Deposit that was a real inbound transfer from a wallet, say so: it becomes an open leg for phase 7 instead.

## 18. Open questions for the orchestrator

- **Q-O59 The BISON column set and row shape.** Not verifiable today (HTTP 403); settled on the anonymised real export in step 5 (D166). A row shape of one row per trade with both legs would make `MovementLine` carry two amounts; the schema sketch assumes one row per trade.
- **Q-O60 The quirion layouts.** The help centre could not be fetched; the three readers' fixtures are written from the forum's description and corrected in step 4 on the anonymised real PDFs; a layout difference is a spec amendment recorded in §14.1.
- **Q-O61 One or two cash accounts for the depot.** *Answered 13 Nov 2026 from the real documents: one account per portfolio plus the Verrechnungskonto, each with its own IBAN and balance, under up to two customer relationships; D165 amended (a).* D165 (1) assumes quirion states separate balances for the Verrechnungskonto and the portfolio's cash; if one Kontoauszug carries both, the depot is one account and the fixture loses a case.
- **Q-O62 ETF price source.** *Settled 12 Nov 2026 by D174 (the issuers' own NAV); what remains per issuer is Q-O73 and Q-O74.*
- **Q-O63 A "Quartalsbericht erwartet" reminder.** Cheap on phase 5's catalogue (one kind, quarterly, cleared by a confirmed `holding_statement` of the quarter); not in this spec's steps; add in step 4 if Luis wants it.
- **Q-O64 The CoinGecko Demo limits.** 10,000 credits a month and the 365-day history cover this phase (a handful of coins × at most 31 days per run); phase 7's backfill for lots older than a year needs Kraken's 720 candles or typed prices; the Demo's per-minute limit is from the pricing page, not the rate-limit page (404).
- **Q-O65 A second Sparkasse's references.** The second Sparkasse (§17 Q1) runs the same software family, so its camt export and FinTS camt are expected to carry `AcctSvcrRef` as the first one does (phase 2 D22, D116); only its real files show it, recorded in §14.1 with step 1 and step 3. DKB's references are Q-O70.
- **Q-O66 The two-accounts-with-imports migration refusal** (D171): if the production workspace has only the rental account with imports (true as of 12 Nov 2026), the data step is unambiguous; the refusal exists for a database the spec does not know.
- **Q-O67 `TaxTreatment.PRIVATE` vs reusing `pass_through`.** A new value makes the Anlage V's invariant testable ("no `PRIVATE` row is ever read"); reusing `pass_through` would avoid a schema change but blur the invariant; the spec chooses the new value and a reviewer may disagree.
- **Q-O70 DKB's statement format over FinTS (D173 (2)).** No public source says whether DKB's BPD lists HICAZS (camt) or only HKKAZ (MT940); the facade refuses the latter with `bank.unsupported_format` today. Answered by the stored BPD at the first real DKB connection (step 3, recorded in §14.1): camt → nothing to build; MT940 only → the reader D173 designs becomes a spec amendment with `mt940.yaml`, or the account stays on the CSV export, Luis's call by how often he wants it fetched. Also open until then: whether DKB's camt entries carry `AcctSvcrRef` the way the Sparkasse's do (D22's hash) and whether the FinTS window is limited to about three months (a forum note of 2021).
- **Q-O71 DKB's approval cadence (D173 (5)).** Third-party guides say an approval at every login and every retrieval. If the first two manual syncs confirm it, the daily fetch is useless for that account and the choice is a weekly manual „Jetzt abrufen“ with the app, or the CSV export; if a later sync runs without one (as the Sparkasse's did, phase 5 Q-O35), `auto_sync` is allowed as for any connection. Recorded in §14.1.
- **Q-O72 One dialog for several accounts of one login (D163).** Phase 5's model is one `bank_connection` per account, each with its own client state and approval; two accounts under one login (a rental and a private account at the same Sparkasse) therefore cost two approvals every 90 or 180 days instead of one. `get_sepa_accounts()` lists every account of a login in one dialog, so a connection per login serving several accounts is possible; it is a phase 5 refactor (the sync row, the lock, the window per account) and is not in this spec's steps. Worth doing only if the approvals turn out to be frequent (Q-O71).
- **Q-O73 Vanguard's terms (D174 (1)).** *Answered 12 Nov 2026 (Luis): one request a day per held fund for the household's own use is acceptable; Vanguard gets an adapter in step 5, with the same one-request-a-day limit and the documents as fallback.* The site's terms forbid "devices or software that provide repeated automated access" and grant a personal, non-commercial licence; one request a day by a household's own tool is a reading Luis has to make, not the architect; until he does, a Vanguard fund is priced from the documents. The other issuers' terms that were read (iShares, State Street's page notices) say nothing about automation and allow personal use. *Extended 19 Nov 2026 (Luis: „Read them, own use only“):* the same reading covers Xtrackers (robots.txt `/api/`, terms §5.1), ARK Invest Europe (the reproduction clause) and Legal & General (robots.txt `/srp/api/`), and Amundi's and BNP Paribas' internal services, one request a day per held fund (D174 amended 19 Nov 2026).
- **Q-O74 Issuers without a verified NAV page (D174 (1)).** Xtrackers, Amundi, UBS, HSBC, Invesco, BNP Paribas (and Assenagon, an active fund) could not be read today; each adapter is written in step 5 from the real product page of a fund the household holds, with the URL and the date recorded in §14.1, and a fund whose issuer still has no usable page stays on the documents with „Kurs aus dem letzten Bericht“. The registry and the adapters are repo files, so a new fund of a known issuer needs a registry line, never code; a new issuer needs an adapter. *Updated 16 Nov 2026 (§3, „Other issuers' NAV, re-checked 16 Nov 2026“):* the last sentence no longer holds for iShares and SPDR, whose held funds are found by ISIN in the issuers' catalogues (D174 amended 16 Nov 2026); a held fund of any other issuer is still priced from the reports. **No further adapter was built**, for one reason that applies to all and one per issuer. All: `tests/test_prices_sources.py::test_every_adapter_has_a_parse_case` requires a parse case in `tests/fixtures/phase6/issuer_nav.yaml` for every adapter, and that file changes only in a spec PR, so each new adapter needs one (a parse case and a synthetic sample per issuer). Per issuer: **Invesco** is buildable as it stands (an XLSX keyed by the ISIN, its terms and robots.txt not against one daily request), but its file names neither the ISIN nor the currency, so the adapter must read the share class's JSON first (ISIN, currency, name, latest NAV) and pick the column block by that name, the JSON's NAV of its day checking the column; **Xtrackers** gives exactly the file the protocol wants (ISIN and currency in it, keyed by the ISIN, so no catalogue) but its robots.txt disallows `/api/`, the path its own page button uses: a reading for Luis, like Vanguard's terms (Q-O73); **Amundi** answers ISIN, currency and the full history keyed by the ISIN, but only to a POST to the page's internal API (the app's HTTP client sends GET only): a reading for Luis and a client change; **HANetf** is buildable from the visible „NAV History“ file, but the file states no currency, and the ticker its address needs is found only on the fund page, so a read is two requests (the page for the address and the currency, then the file); **ARK** only as a read of the fund page's inline chart data, the currency from the same page; **BNP Paribas** only through its internal JSON service, which states no currency; **Legal & General** gives the latest price only and its robots.txt disallows the path; **Dimensional**'s terms forbid repeated automated access beyond what it makes generally available (a reading for Luis, Q-O73's kind) and give no public history; **UBS** (georestricted from here, robots.txt disallows `/bin/`) and **WisdomTree** (behind a browser check) not usable. Not re-checked: HSBC, Assenagon; **Vanguard**, whose adapter Q-O73 settled on 12 Nov 2026, was never built (step 5b's note that the held funds needed none was stale, §14.1). Third-party aggregators (justETF, extraETF, boerse-frankfurt.de, onvista) would cover every one of these issuers; using one is a new decision for Luis, not part of D174. *Updated 19 Nov 2026 (§3, „Issuers' NAV, verified on held funds 19 Nov 2026“; D174 amended 19 Nov 2026):* adapters for Amundi, Xtrackers, Invesco, ARK Invest Europe, BNP Paribas, Legal & General (latest NAV only) and HANetf are specced and built on a branch, not merged; UBS and WisdomTree answer 403 to a non-browser client and Dimensional gives no history, so their funds stay on „Kurs aus dem letzten Bericht“ with the existing „Kurs eintragen“ to-do once that price is a week old. **Still open:** a third-party source for them; none was found whose terms allow a daily automated request (onvista, justETF and Tradegate forbid it), so it stays Luis's decision, with its own source kind.
- **Q-O75 The DKB CSV layout (D173 (3)).** Two layouts are described by secondary sources (header in line 5 with twelve columns in 2025; header in line 7 with a period line in 2021) and one reports a delimiter change in January 2025; the reader sniffs both, and the anonymised real export of step 1 is the contract, as the camt was in phase 2 (a difference is a spec amendment recorded in §14.1). Also unverified: whether „Kontostand vom <date>“ is the balance at the export day or at the range's end; until the real export shows it, the value only pre-fills the typed closing (D173 (3)), and `balance_source = file` for DKB is an amendment.
- **Q-O76 BISON's export and the transfer hash (D175).** *Answered 13 Nov 2026 from the real export (kept in the private samples folder): it carries neither a hash nor a destination for a Withdraw, only asset, units and date (nine columns, separated by „; “), so the fallback below holds; checked against `bison_csv.yaml` in step 5.* Whether the export carries a transaction hash or a destination for a crypto Withdrawal is part of Q-O59; if it carries neither, phase 7 links the wallet's deposit by asset, amount less fee, network and a 48-hour window, and the chain side supplies the hash. Phase 7 D184 (1a) takes this amount-and-window path for BISON's withdrawals.
- **Q-O77 The card statement's layout (D176).** *Answered 12 Nov 2026 (Luis: "Avarda is only pdf"; one statement read from his computer, kept in the private samples folder): PDF only, digital text layer, balances printed, the payment its own credit line, the card number masked, a transaction id per line, the payment IBAN printed; D176 (3) and (4) settled, the layout in §14.1.* Avarda's statement PDF is not public; whether it prints the previous and new balance, the payment received as its own line and the card number masked is checked on the first real statement in step 2 and recorded in §14.1; a statement without balances imports with typed balances like DKB's CSV. Also asked of Luis (12 Nov 2026): whether Avarda's portal exports the transactions as CSV or Excel; if it does, step 2b reads that file by code (like DKB's CSV, no model and no consent gate) and builds no PDF import.
- **Q-O78 Units moved from one owner's depot into the joint depot (phase 8).** An Ausbuchung from a depot held by owner A alone and the Einbuchung into the joint depot is booked as a transfer of units that keeps the acquisition dates (D165 (d)). Whether half of those units is a gift between spouses (exempt under §13 ErbStG) and whether it changes the acquisition data owner B's half carries for the capital-income rules is a phase 8 question; nothing in phase 6 computes a tax on it.
- **Q-O79 Binance's EUR and stablecoin remainders (D178 (6)).** On the real transaction history the EUR rows net to a positive remainder and one stablecoin's rows to a small negative one, while every other coin nets to zero. Either Binance still holds the EUR (and the stablecoin's row is rounding), or the export misses rows. Luis types the balances Binance shows when he imports; a mismatch is then named per coin, never bridged. *Answered 14 Nov 2026:* the export misses rows. Binance's own transaction history takes the stablecoin below zero at a withdrawal, which no complete history can do, and the trade, withdraw and transaction files agree with each other, so it is no reading error; the person books the gap with „Differenz als Korrektur buchen“ (D178 amended).
- **Q-O81 Which debit a withdraw history row completes (D178 (3), step 5a).** `match_withdrawals` pairs each Binance withdrawal with the nearest Completed history row of the same coin whose Amount + Fee equals the amount sent within the hour before. When two withdrawals of one coin and amount are both requested before the first is debited, nearest-row pairing swaps their TXIDs if Binance debits in request order; phase 7 links the wallet deposit by TXID and would then attach it to the other withdrawal (the exchange-side lots are unaffected). The household's real history has no such pair (checked 13 Nov 2026), so the rule stays; settle it from the first real case.
- **Q-O96 How phase 8 counts a balance correction (D178 amended).** An inflow `balance_correction` has no price and no date of purchase of its own: phase 8's FIFO could treat it as a lot of unknown cost (its full disposal value then taxable inside the year), or the person could be asked for the missing movement's date and price when one is sold. Nothing assumes either until the phase 8 spec decides; the correction stores only its quantity, date and reason. *Settled by phase 8 D212 (spec merged 16 Nov 2026, PR #384, not built): an inflow correction is a lot with no known cost or day; nothing is asked while it is held, and when a disposal consumes it the to-do „Anschaffung nachtragen“ asks for the day and the cost, or takes 0 € on the correction's day as the cautious estimate.*
- **Q-O80 BISON's withdrawal amount and its fee (D166 amended).** The export prints a Withdraw's Asset (amount) and its Fee in the asset's units but not whether the amount includes the fee. Step 5 reads it as Binance's does (the amount sent includes the fee, D175) and the typed balance after the import confirms or corrects it, recorded in §14.1.

- **Q-O172 HANetf found by its name (D174 amended 19 Nov 2026 (d)).** HANetf's sitemap names no ISIN, so a held HANetf fund is found by matching its name's word starts to the fund pages' slugs, and only a page stating the fund's ISIN is used. A depot's short name whose abbreviations do not start the slug's words (or an issuer that renames its pages) finds nothing and the fund stays on its report price with no card. Open: whether a person may type a fund's issuer reference instead (a screen, not built).
- **Q-O173 A first read the check refuses has no card (D174 amended 19 Nov 2026 (g)).** The run's event names the fund (`nav_disagreed`) and it keeps its report price, so the to-do that appears is the stale-price one a week later; nothing says that the issuer's figure was refused. Open: a card „Der Kurs von <Fondsgesellschaft> weicht vom Depotbericht ab“ (no new code until Luis asks).
- **Q-O174 Legal & General's history.** Its fund centre gives the latest NAV only; a price-history request exists (`/srp/api/price-history/<id>`) but how it is called was not found in about twenty tries. If Luis copies the request one fund page's price chart makes in his browser once, the past month-ends of its funds could be stored too; until then a day whose run failed is lost.
- **Q-O175 Ending the „Kurs weicht vom Depotbericht ab“ card (D264 (7)).** The card ends when a newer report prices the fund or a person types a price for the report's day. A person who checked and finds the daily price right has no way to end it but typing that same price. Open: an „Erledigt“ button (a stored acknowledgement per fund and day, which needs a table or a reminder-ack kind), built only if Luis asks.
